# Findings — 2026-08-10 health check on flag-don't-truncate

## Bug A — Clobber hitting real staff data (HIGH PRIORITY)

Neha's real doc `msmqvojc7h123a` (2026-08-10, Parea Cafe, 10:11 start) shows
`reconciliationNeeded` flapping — flagged (`reconciliationNeededSince:
2026-08-10T06:30:45.660Z`, i.e. 12:00:45 IST) → wiped (field absent entirely
on a later read) → flagged again — while continuously `inProgress:true` the
whole time. Same signature as the `msjtnbdncxpyqk` test-doc saga (full-
document overwrite, not a targeted field update — `void`/similar fields
disappear entirely rather than flipping to `false`), but now confirmed on
live staff data, not test residue.

**Root cause, confirmed by re-reading the actual code (not assumed):**

`saveData()` in **shadow mode** (current production `diffMode`) already does
a full, unconditional `commitInChunks()` rewrite of the entire `timeLogs`
array on **every regular save cycle**, gated only by `hasUnsavedChanges` —
the diff engine computes alongside it but is explicitly observation-only
("never calls `syncChangedDocs()`, so this can never affect what actually
got persisted"). So in shadow mode, any tab holding a stale in-memory
`timeLogs` array (loaded once, never refreshed since a server-side change
landed) will blindly push that whole stale array back on its next save tick,
wiping fields it doesn't know changed — exactly what's hitting Neha's doc.

**Correction to the originally proposed cure**: promoting `diffMode` to
`'primary'` fixes this for the *regular* save cycle (`saveData()`'s primary
path becomes diff-only, so a stale tab only rewrites what changed since it
last refreshed its own hash — server-side changes it doesn't know about are
untouched). **But it does not fully close the hole.** `backstopFullSave()`
(the 5-minute low-frequency safety net) is explicitly independent of
`diffMode` — its own comment says it "reuses the ORIGINAL commitInChunks()
unchanged," and it always does an unconditional full-array rewrite,
regardless of whether `diffMode` is `'shadow'` or `'primary'`. A stale tab
would still clobber server-side field changes via this backstop every 5
minutes even after the flip.

**Full cure requires two changes, not one:**
1. Promote `diffMode` to `'primary'` (fixes the frequent/regular path).
2. Separately address `backstopFullSave()` — either make it diff-based too
   (reuse `diffCollection`/`syncChangedDocs`), or have it refresh its
   snapshot/array from a fresh server read immediately before writing, or
   accept and explicitly document the residual 5-minute-window risk if it's
   left as-is (not recommended, since this is the more likely single
   explanation for today's clobbers given their spacing).

**Status**: not fixed yet. Do not manually fight individual clobbered docs
in the meantime (they get re-clobbered) — let the EOD-close cron close
genuinely stale/flagged sessions at biometric OUT once nightly, per its own
30-day catch-up floor, until the write path itself is fixed.

**Confirmed hitting live data twice more (2026-08-10/11 night)**: a manual
repair on Tasmin's `msmrxi61p9m649` was clobbered back to its pre-repair
state at 01:48 and again at 01:56 IST, ~8min apart (5-min backstop cadence,
stretched by background-tab timer throttling) — same signature (custom
fields `repairedAt`/`repairNote` vanished entirely, not cleared). Traced to
a laptop tab left open overnight, still running `appVersion:'2026-08-08.1'`
hours after production had moved to `2026-08-10.1` — i.e. a tab that never
reloaded, immune to the new code entirely. This is what motivated Bug A's
full staged fix plan below, with the version-force stage moved to the
FRONT of the rollout rather than last.

### Bug A fix — staged rollout plan (agreed 2026-08-11, nothing built yet
### except where separately noted)

Six stages total, in dependency order. Full technical detail (files,
functions, exact fixtures, risk levels) lives in chat, not duplicated
here — this is a status summary for anyone picking this up cold.

1. **Meeting-cancel explicit delete** — give meeting edit/cancel a direct
   Firestore `.delete()` for removed `planEntries`, so cleanup no longer
   depends on `backstopFullSave()`'s own delete block. Must be MERGED AND
   DEPLOYED to prod before stage 3 merges (not just branched) — stage 3
   removes that delete block, so if stage 1 isn't live first, cancelled
   meetings' plan entries become permanent orphans.
2. **`syncChangedDocs()` write/delete fixtures** — prove the diff engine's
   write and delete paths for real (Firestore round-trip, throwaway docs)
   before anything makes it the primary writer. Fixture-only, no prod
   code change, no separate deploy needed.
3. **`backstopFullSave()` → diff-based** — reuses `diffCollection`/
   `syncChangedDocs` instead of its own delete block + full
   `commitInChunks()` rewrite, with a server-refetch mitigation (locked
   2026-08-11) so it still checks against server truth, not just its own
   in-memory array — preserving *some* independent drift-recovery value
   even though it's no longer a fully separate full-rewrite safety net.
4. **`diffMode` → `'primary'`** — the single-line flip. Highest blast
   radius of any stage (every save, every user, immediately). Gated on a
   live shadow-mode observation window first (the still-outstanding
   readiness-checklist item from earlier in this engagement).
5. **Version-force (`Stage A` + `Stage B` below)** — MOVED TO FIRST in
   build order, despite being numbered 5 in dependency terms, because a
   tab frozen on old code is immune to stages 1-4 regardless of when they
   ship. This is the stage actually being built next.
6. **Validation** — billing/usage graph step-down, extended clean-doc
   watch, meeting-cancel confirmation, and a cron-survival test (real
   targeted server write + clean watch) as the concrete go/no-go for ever
   flipping the cron's `confirm` flag live. Not started.

**Version-force (build-order stage 1) — split into A and B, decided
2026-08-11:**

- **Stage A — approved, buildable now.** Move `backstopFullSave()`'s
  timing onto the reliable tick worker's 5th tick (same pattern already
  used for `finalizeStaleHeartbeatSessionsAllUsers()`), with a
  plain-`setInterval` fallback if the worker fails to init — closes the
  one confirmed asymmetry where the write path was on a less
  throttle-resistant timer than everything else in the file. First
  branch to be cut.
- **Stage B, guards+indicator — approved, buildable now.** A
  `staleVersionLockout` flag, checked and refused-on in ALL write entry
  points — not just `saveData()`/`backstopFullSave()`/`heartbeatSession()`
  but also the granular `saveTimeLog()`/`savePlanEntry()`/`saveProject()`
  paths, which bypass `saveData()` entirely when `useGranularSaves` is
  true and would otherwise leak a write through a manual edit even while
  "locked out." Lockout triggers only from `showVersionGateModal()`'s
  `manualMode` branch (confirmed mismatch, past the auto-reload attempt
  cap) — structurally unreachable from a `/api/version` fetch failure, so
  "never lock on a check failure" holds by construction, not by a
  condition that has to stay correct over time. Also fixes
  `versionGateModalShown` never resetting, so the lockout indicator
  re-displays every time a backgrounded locked-out tab becomes visible
  again, instead of showing once and going silent.
- **Stage B, IndexedDB queue-purge — spike run 2026-08-11, confirmed
  viable, now buildable.** `firebase.firestore().terminate()` followed by
  `.clearPersistence()` (the compat-SDK-10.7.1-correct name — NOT
  `clearIndexedDbPersistence()`, which is modular-SDK-only and doesn't
  exist on this surface) was tested against a live, already-warmed-up
  instance in an isolated incognito window: a write was forced offline
  (`disableNetwork()`) so it sat genuinely queued
  (`hasPendingWrites:true` confirmed before the purge), then the
  terminate+clear sequence ran. Result: `terminate()` resolved cleanly
  (no hang, no reject); `firebase.firestore()` called again afterward
  returned a genuinely fresh instance (`db === db2` → `false`);
  `clearPersistence()` resolved without the `failed-precondition` the
  docs warn about; the old (terminated) instance's own background write
  attempt correctly threw `client has already been terminated`. Server-
  side ground truth confirmed the queued write never landed — the probe
  doc doesn't exist on the server (`source:'server'`, not cache), and the
  throwaway `__spike_test` collection is otherwise empty. Client and
  server agree: the purge genuinely discards a queued mutation, not a
  "looks clean but flushes anyway" false positive. This mechanism is now
  cleared to go into B as designed.

**Explicit framing to hold onto — do not skip this when deciding whether
to flip the cron live**: version-force (stage A+B) narrows the *exposure
window* — fewer stale tabs, shorter-lived, and new writes stop the moment
lockout engages. It does **not** by itself retire the queued-stale-write
risk, because a write already queued before lockout still flushes on
reload regardless. That risk is only fully closed once stages 3+4 also
land (diff-based backstop, `diffMode='primary'`), which shrink the
*blast radius per write* — after that, even a late-flushing queued write
is a single targeted doc, not a whole-collection overwrite. Version-force
and the diff-write stages are complementary, not redundant. **Do not
treat version-force alone as sufficient justification to flip the cron's
`confirm` flag to live** — that decision needs stages 1-4 done too, plus
stage 6's validation, per the original staged plan.

## Bug B — Reload/new-tab orphans open sessions

When a staff member reloads their tab or opens a new one, their prior open
session is not closed. `startTimerWithPlan()`'s old-session-close logic only
fires when *the same tab* switches tasks (it reads the current tab's own
`timerRunning`/`currentSessionLogId` globals) — a fresh tab or reload starts
with none of that state, so it has no way to know a prior session exists to
close.

**Result**: parallel orphaned open sessions. Observed directly today —
Neha's `msmqvojc7h123a` (10:11–11:03, abandoned) sat open in parallel with
her new `msmzeqyzr2u40k` (14:10 onward) for hours.

**Not data-destructive** — flag-don't-truncate's own machinery recovers it
correctly (`finalize-stale.js` flags it, biometric-sync's EOD-close will
close it at real biometric OUT tonight) — but it shouldn't happen in the
first place, and it's what exposed Bug A's clobber pattern to real data.

**Correction (2026-08-11), after re-diagnosing against current `main`
before scoping the fix**: the task-switch path itself is NOT the bug —
`startTimerWithPlan()`/`closeCurrentSessionNow()` (merged via
`feat-task-switch-orphan-fix`, live since before this session) already
closes the old session correctly, immediately, with detach-before-write
ordering, before any switch. Confirmed against real 2026-08-10 data
(Suravi's two projects, Souvik's project+lunch, etc. all closed cleanly).
The actual gap is narrower: purely cross-context — a session abandoned
when a tab froze/reloaded/closed, only found later by a fresh page load.
`checkForOrphanedSessionOnLoad()` already detects this (queries Firestore
for any other `inProgress:true` doc for the user, on load) but is
advisory-only — shows a dismissible banner, never closes anything, so
if the person just starts a new task anyway (likely), the old session
stays orphaned until the server-side sweep flags it.

**Fix, scoped 2026-08-11, approved, in progress**: upgrade
`checkForOrphanedSessionOnLoad()` from advisory to acting. Reuses the
existing `STALE_HEARTBEAT_MS` (5 min, same constant the sweep/watchdog
already use — no new threshold invented) as the existing fresh-vs-orphan
gate, unchanged. Anything that survives that gate gets a NEW
`tryAutoCloseOrphan()` attempt: closes it capped at its own last
heartbeat (never at "now" — this can be discovered hours later, capping
at now would fabricate work time that never happened), same
detach-before-write/IST-pinned discipline as `closeCurrentSessionNow()`.
Anything with an unresolvable time boundary (no heartbeat signal, no
start signal, or a malformed hb<=start ordering) falls back to the
existing banner rather than guessing. Closes ALL qualifying stale
orphans found in one scan, not just the newest. Includes
`reconciliationNeeded: false` in the write — partial coverage of the
separately-logged Angana-cleanup gap, for this one call site only; the
broader item stays separately logged below, not resolved by this.

**Stage-B interaction — logged, not acted on**: `checkForOrphanedSessionOnLoad()`
is deliberately NOT guarded by `staleVersionLockout` (Stage B,
`feat-stale-version-lockout`, unmerged). Probably correct as-is — this is
an honest, targeted close of a specific known-abandoned doc, not the
class of blind full-array rewrite Stage B's lockout exists to stop — but
it's a real interaction between two independently-developed fixes that
should be decided consciously once both are live, not left as an
accident of which branch happened to merge first.

## Bug C — EOD-close double-stretches same-day duplicate sessions

`closeReconciliationNeededSessions()` in `api/biometric-sync.js` processes
every `inProgress:true && reconciliationNeeded:true` doc **independently**,
with no per-person/per-day grouping. When a person has 2+ separate flagged
sessions on the same day — e.g. a Bug-B orphan plus their real final
session — the function extends **all** of them to the same biometric OUT
instead of recognizing they're the same person's day and only one of them
is the genuine final segment.

**Observed in tonight's real confirm=true run (2026-08-10)**: Neha, Angana,
Souvik, Suravi all got 2 (Souvik: 3, counting the untouched normal doc that
sits inside both stretched ranges) of their day's docs independently
stretched to the same OUT time, producing overlapping ranges and inflated
totals (Neha 14h16m, Angana 14h04m, Souvik 13h27m, Suravi 13h23m — all
against real ceilings around 8–9h). Ridhi and Taskiya, who each had only one
flagged doc, closed correctly — confirming this only manifests with 2+
same-day flags per person.

**Fix**: group flagged docs by `userId`+`date` before closing. Only extend
the **latest-starting** session in each group to biometric OUT. Any earlier
same-day flagged doc(s) should be bounded to the next session's start time
(or next doc's start, whichever comes first chronologically), not stretched
to OUT.

**Status**: fixed and merged to `main` as of `2026-08-10.1` (branch
`feat-bugc-de-overlap-eod-close`, 4/4 fixture cases green including a
read-failure fail-safe case — see `test/bugc-de-overlap.test.js`). Neha,
Angana, Souvik, and Suravi's 2026-08-10 data were manually repaired
(de-overlapped, verified `source:'server'` after each write). `vercel.json`
was deliberately left untouched during this merge — the cron stays
dry-run (`confirm=false` default) until Bug B also merges.

## Angana cleanup — stale `reconciliationNeeded:true` survives normal close

Angana's `msmrahdqbmqg0w` (10:23–13:28, 2026-08-10, normal project-switch
close, not touched by Bug C) still carries `reconciliationNeeded:true`.
Normal switch-close logic sets `inProgress:false` but doesn't clear
`reconciliationNeeded` — so a doc that closed cleanly through the ordinary
task-switch path can still show up as "needs reconciliation" indefinitely.

**Fix**: wherever a session is closed through the normal (non-EOD-close)
path — `closeCurrentSessionNow()` and the logout handler — also clear
`reconciliationNeeded:false` if it was set, since the session's real end is
now known and no longer needs the EOD-close/reconciliation-queue treatment.

**Status**: FIXED and DEPLOYED, 2026-08-15. Confirmed via a fresh
investigation the same day that this doc was the only live occurrence
(re-triggered as a UI notification/flag) — read-only check found it was
exactly this same, already-documented residue, no new incident.

Built on `fix-normal-close-clear-reconciliation-flag`: `closeCurrentSessionNow()`
now includes `reconciliationNeeded: false` in its write payload (mirrored
into the local array too). Turned out `closeCurrentSessionNow()` and "the
logout handler" were never two separate code paths — logout just calls
`closeCurrentSessionNow('closed at logout')` — so fixing the one function
covers both cases originally named. `tryAutoCloseOrphan()` (Bug B)
already correctly sets this field in its own write and needed no change.
Isolated by construction: only touches this function's own write, which
fires exclusively on a genuine, definitive close (task-switch or logout)
— `finalize-stale.js`, `closeReconciliationNeededSessions()` (EOD-close),
the Stage 0 delete guard, `saveData()`, and `diffMode` are all untouched.

3 fixture cases in `test/fix-normal-close-clear-reconciliation-flag.test.js`:
a flagged-then-cleanly-closed session ends with `reconciliationNeeded:false`
in both the write payload and the local array mirror; a different,
still-genuinely-stale session in the same array is left completely
untouched (proves no leakage); a never-flagged session closes cleanly
with no error from setting the field on a doc that never had it. All 7
existing test suites re-run clean (126 checks, 0 failures).

The one existing residue doc (`msmrahdqbmqg0w`) was cleared separately —
freeze-checked clean over a 5-minute hold first, then a targeted
`merge:true` write (`reconciliationNeeded:false` only), verified
`source:'server'`: flag cleared, `inProgress`/`durationMins`/`endTime`
all confirmed unchanged. Merged to `main`, deployed as `2026-08-15.4`.
Post-deploy: `HTTP 200`, correct version string, `vercel.json` confirmed
byte-identical (empty diff pre-merge).

## LIVE INCIDENT — 2026-08-12: Bug A clobber on Neha + Ridhi's 11 Aug data,
## source machine not yet found

**Status as of 2026-08-12 ~21:23 IST (16:23 UTC): RESOLVED.** See
resolution section at the end of this incident block for the final write
and hold-check.

**What happened**: last night's real `confirm=true` EOD-close for 2026-08-11
closed Neha (546m/9h06m, three-doc de-overlap via the Bug C fix) and Ridhi
(545m/9h05m, capped at biometric OUT) correctly — verified `source:'server'`
at the time. This morning both showed `STALLED`/`STILL OPEN` again in Needs
Reconciliation. Investigation confirmed real doc reverts, not new sessions:
both docs (`mso5zsezaebbnr` Neha, `mso6h43mexw8hj` Ridhi) reverted to their
state ~15-20 minutes into their original 11 Aug morning sessions
(`inProgress:true`, `recovered:false`, `endTime`/`durationMins` matching an
early heartbeat, close-suffix stripped from `desc`) — a full-document
overwrite from a stale in-memory snapshot, not a targeted edit.

**Clobber source identified: a DIFFERENT machine (working theory: HR's PC)**
with a stale admin-scoped OMS session, `appVersion:'2026-08-10.2'`, open
since ~11 Aug morning and never reloaded since — meaning it predates Stage B
entirely and structurally cannot have the `staleVersionLockout` guard, since
that code didn't exist yet when this tab's JS loaded. Confirmed NOT the
user's own laptop: full restart, both Chrome profiles confirmed empty, and
`updatedAt` on both docs kept advancing anyway (repeated checks: `05:30 →
05:59 → 06:11 → 06:18 → 06:27 → 08:16 → 08:24 → 08:40 → 08:47`, all UTC,
each single write touching BOTH Neha's and Ridhi's docs at the identical
millisecond — the admin-scoped-tab signature, since only a view with
cross-user data loaded would touch both in one `commitInChunks()` pass).

**CORRECTION, 2026-08-12 ~12:15 IST: "admin-scoped" was wrong.** Confirmed
directly from `loadData()` (index.html:2847-2850): the main `timeLogs`
query has no `where('userId'==...)` at all — it loads the entire
collection (90-day date cutoff only) into the shared in-memory array for
**every** logged-in user, admin or not. Role only gates what's rendered,
not what's loaded. So the "touches both Neha's and Ridhi's docs in one
`commitInChunks()` pass" signature does NOT imply an admin-scoped tab —
**any** staff member's own stale tab has both docs loaded and would
clobber both. The clobber source could be Neha's own stale session, or
Ridhi's, or anyone else's — not necessarily HR's PC or any admin device.
Correcting this because it was stated as confirmed fact above and drove
several hours of hunting scoped to admin-only devices.
Ruled out a stuck IndexedDB replay: a queued write is a one-time flush with
a frozen timestamp; this is a live, repeating ~5-8min cycle with a fresh
`updatedAt` every time — the write-scheduling signature Stage A made
*more* reliable from a backgrounded tab, ironically, since Stages 3/4
(making the backstop's write logic diff-based instead of full-rewrite)
were never built.

**Fix**: find and close/hard-refresh the stale tab on the other machine.
Once closed, confirm via a baseline + 7-minute `updatedAt` re-read that it
has genuinely stopped advancing before writing anything.

**Pending repair (anchored to biometric OUT + actual same-day docs, NOT
remembered totals — verified via direct arithmetic before write, do NOT
write until the clobber source is confirmed dead)**:
- Neha `mso5zsezaebbnr` → `endTime:'13:11'`, `durationMins:188` (bounded to
  the next doc's start, i.e. the Lunch Break doc `msocpwlnio99d0` which
  starts 13:11 — three-doc total with `msocpwlnio99d0` 119m and
  `msogzgp5jl8c35` 239m [already correctly capped at biometric OUT 19:10,
  untouched by the clobber] = 546m, zero overlap, 3m under the 549m/9h09m
  ceiling).
- Ridhi `mso6h43mexw8hj` → `endTime:'19:22'`, `durationMins:545` (single
  session, capped at biometric OUT 19:22, 5m under the 550m/9h10m ceiling).
- Both: `merge:true`, current `desc` read live and appended (never
  overwritten), plus `recovered:true`, `reconciliationNeeded:false`,
  `recoveredAt`. `source:'server'` verify immediately after each write, then
  hold and re-verify at +2min minimum (this incident is exactly why — the
  first attempt at this repair, fired before the clobber source was found,
  reverted within 2 minutes).

**Cross-reference**: production is fully current — Bug C, Stage A, Stage B,
and Bug B are all merged and live (`2026-08-12.2`). Cron remains dry-run,
not flipped. This is specifically the residual risk named when Stage B was
scoped: a tab already open and already stale *before* Stage B shipped is
immune to it, because Stage B protects tabs that load its code and *then*
go stale, not ones frozen on older code from before it existed. Once this
specific tab is found and closed, this class of clobber should stop
recurring for any *future* stale tab (one that loads post-Stage-B code and
then goes stale would hit the lockout instead) — but the full guarantee
(even a late write being harmless, not just rare) still depends on Stages
3/4 of the original Bug A plan, not yet built.

**Source hunt, 2026-08-12 ~06:00-11:00 IST**: laptop restart + both Chrome
profiles confirmed empty → clobber persisted (`updatedAt` kept advancing:
`05:30→05:59→06:11→06:18→06:27→08:16→08:24→08:40→08:47`). HR's tab closed
→ clobber persisted (`09:15→09:22→10:58`, still advancing as of the last
check). Source still not found as of this entry — not this user's laptop,
not HR's tab. **Correction**: a claim that `savedBy:'Rajkumar'` appeared on
the clobbering write was investigated and found to be in error — pulled
the full field set on both clobbered docs three separate times (fresh,
`source:'server'`, at 09:26, 10:58, and one prior check) and on
`companyData/squareDB` twice; the only names ever observed were `Neha`/
`Ridhi` (each on their own doc, as `updatedBy`) and `Souvik` (stale,
unrelated `companyData/squareDB` write, ~4h+ stale relative to the clobber
cycle, never refreshed by it). No "Rajkumar" reference found anywhere.
Disregard that lead entirely.

**Structural gap found during the hunt, worth a future fix**: the
`timeLogs` docs being clobbered carry no field that reliably identifies
the writing session — `updatedBy` is never touched by the `timeLogs`
stamp function (`(log, saveTimestamp) => ({ ...log, updatedAt:
saveTimestamp })`, confirmed from source — only `updatedAt` gets
overwritten on a full-array rewrite), and there is no `instanceId`/device/
session field on this doc type at all (confirmed via full field dump,
21 fields total, checked twice). By contrast, `companyData/squareDB`'s
write path *does* freshly compute `savedBy: currentUser?.name` and
`instanceId` on every call, built as a plain literal (not spread from old
data) — genuinely reliable, but that document is never touched by
`backstopFullSave()`, so it's a dead end for diagnosing *this* class of
incident. **Fix worth scoping later**: stamp `instanceId` (and ideally
`updatedBy`) on `timeLogs`/`planEntries` writes too, the same way
`companyData/squareDB` already does — so a future clobber can be traced
to an actual tab/session instead of requiring a blind, behavioral hunt
like this one. Not urgent, not blocking anything current, just logged so
it doesn't get lost.

**Resolution approach, decided 2026-08-12 ~11:00 IST**: given the source
can't be identified from data alone, abandoning the surgical single-tab
hunt in favor of a blanket fix — every person/machine touching OMS does a
hard refresh (Ctrl+Shift+R) or closes their tab. Any tab that reloads
picks up `2026-08-12.2` (Stage B live), which both stops that specific
tab from clobbering again and protects it going forward if it ever goes
stale again. Once done, plan is a fresh `updatedAt` baseline vs. the last
known value (`2026-08-12T10:58:55.250Z`) — if it stops advancing, source
confirmed dead, and the pending repair (Neha `mso5zsezaebbnr` →
`13:11`/188m, Ridhi `mso6h43mexw8hj` → `19:22`/545m, both per the
anchored arithmetic above) goes ahead.

**Scoped risk made real, 2026-08-12 ~11:08 IST: Bug B auto-closed Ridhi's
clobber-corrupted doc at the wrong value.** During the blanket-refresh
remediation, Ridhi's own fresh tab load triggered
`checkForOrphanedSessionOnLoad()` → `tryAutoCloseOrphan()` on
`mso6h43mexw8hj`. Bug B did exactly what it's designed to do — correctly
identified the doc as a stale orphan and closed it, capped at its own
last heartbeat — but that doc's own content was already clobber-reverted
at the time, so "last heartbeat" was the corrupted early-session value,
not reality. Result: doc now shows `endTime:'10:20'`, `durationMins:3`,
`recovered:true`, `recoveredAt:'2026-08-12T11:08:02.237Z'`,
`desc` suffixed `"(auto-closed on load — capped at last heartbeat
10:20)"` (exact `tryAutoCloseOrphan()` template match, confirmed via
`source:'server'` field dump) — and critically `reconciliationNeeded:
false`, which takes it out of tonight's automatic EOD-close scan. This
doc now requires the same manual repair as Neha's, but the "before"
state is different (already `inProgress:false` with Bug B's fields
present, not the original open-and-reverted state) — a manual `merge:true`
write with the correct `endTime:'19:22'`/`durationMins:545` overwrites
Bug B's wrong values cleanly (merge only touches fields explicitly
passed; `recovered:true`/`reconciliationNeeded:false` are the *desired*
end state for a closed, fully-reconciled doc anyway, so no conflict) —
the `desc` should note the correction explicitly rather than silently
overwrite Bug B's own suffix.

This is the exact risk flagged when Bug B was scoped (a stale orphan's
own `lastHeartbeat` is only trustworthy if the doc's content hasn't
already been corrupted by a Bug A clobber first) — now confirmed to
occur in practice, not just in theory. **Future guard worth scoping**:
`tryAutoCloseOrphan()` could sanity-check the implied duration against
biometric before trusting it as a final close — e.g. skip auto-close (or
flag instead) if the capped duration looks implausibly short relative to
how long the session has clearly been open in wall-clock terms. Not
built, not blocking anything current, logged for later.

**+7min hold re-check, 2026-08-12 ~11:15-11:24 IST** (vs. the
10:58:55.250Z baseline, after Neha's PC confirmed off and everyone else
hard-refreshed): Neha `updatedAt` `11:15:49.095Z → 11:24:11.848Z` —
**advanced**. Clobber source still alive despite the blanket refresh.
Ridhi's `updatedAt` moved in lockstep with Neha's at both readings
(same millisecond both times) while her content stayed unchanged
(Bug B's values held, not re-reverted) — consistent with the same
admin-scoped tab's array still including both users, but not yet fully
explained. **Gate still not met — do not write the pending repair for
either doc yet.**

**Remote-kill options investigated and rejected, 2026-08-12 ~11:50-12:00
IST**: user proposed a Firestore security-rule kill switch; drafted an
appVersion-based rule, then found (before deploy) it would also block
`api/biometric-sync.js`'s legitimate `closeAtGenuineFinal()` updateDoc()
on these exact two docs, since that write never touches `appVersion` and
`request.resource.data.appVersion` on an update reflects the resulting
merged doc, not the incoming patch — meaning it'd fall through to the
already-stale `2026-08-10.2` sitting on the doc and get blocked
regardless of who wrote it. A field-disappearance-based rule (using
`recovered` as a marker) was designed as a safer alternative, but user
correctly declined to add ANY conditional rule to a currently wide-open
(`if true`) collection given the risk of a first-ever-enforcement mistake
breaking legitimate traffic — sound call, not overridden.
**Also confirmed by code**: no account-validity check exists anywhere in
`index.html` (`isActive`/`disabled`/etc. all zero matches) — changing or
deleting the admin account would NOT stop the stale tab, since
`currentUser` is cached in memory after login and never re-validated.
**Conclusion: no safe remote-kill mechanism exists in this architecture**
(no session tokens, no per-client identity, nothing to revoke) — API-key
rotation and Firestore API lockdown were considered and rejected as
strictly worse (break everyone, need a redeploy to recover). Back to the
physical hunt.

**New timeline clue, 2026-08-12 ~12:00 IST**: Neha's doc's
`reconciliationNeededSince` reads `2026-08-12T04:55:37.809Z` (10:25 IST
today) and has stayed static across every read since. `finalize-stale.js`
sets this field exactly once (skips already-flagged docs, confirmed at
its own line 129) — for the clobbering tab's frozen snapshot to still be
carrying this field on every rewrite, it must have loaded its copy of
this doc at or after 10:25 IST TODAY, not before. This means the
clobbering tab is NOT an old forgotten session from 11 Aug — it loaded
this morning, right around when the investigation itself began.
Leading theory: a phone/second device used to glance at the dashboard
during today's investigation, backgrounded rather than closed (mobile
background-tab timer throttling would explain the observed ~8-9min
cadence vs. the nominal 5min backstop). Not yet confirmed — worth asking
everyone involved in today's investigation directly, not just the
originally-suspected office machines.

**Root cause of why the stale tab never self-reloaded, found 2026-08-12
~12:10 IST**: `2026-08-12.2` deployed at 06:53:29 IST, well before the
tab's ~10:25 IST load time — meaning the culprit tab most likely loaded
CURRENT code and has stale in-memory DATA only, which `staleVersionLockout`
was never designed to catch (it guards code staleness, not data
staleness — the still-unbuilt Stage 3/4 diff-based-backstop gap). Separately,
even a genuine version mismatch has a real escalation bug: the
countdown-to-reload timer (index.html:2242, `showVersionGateModal()`) is a
plain main-thread `setInterval`, not routed through the reliable tick
worker like every other timer in this file was deliberately hardened to
be (worker timers survive backgrounding; plain main-thread ones are
exactly what gets throttled/paused). On a genuinely backgrounded tab, the
60s countdown can stall indefinitely — it never reaches zero, so
`forceReloadToVersion()` never fires, the attempt counter never advances
past 0, and `staleVersionLockout` never engages even after a real
mismatch is detected. **This is the actual reason a stale tab can survive
indefinitely without self-reloading, independent of the code-vs-data
staleness question above.** Future fix: route the countdown timer through
`initReliableTickWorker()`'s tick, same pattern used for
`checkForNewVersion()`/`backstopFullSave()`/the watchdog. Not built yet,
logged for later — Stage B still needs this hardening even after today's
incident resolves.

**Action taken, 2026-08-12 ~12:10 IST**: bumped `APP_VERSION`/`version.json`
to `2026-08-12.3` (commit `a354946`), pushed to `main`, deployed. Confirmed
live: `/api/version` serves `2026-08-12.3`. Purpose: give any tab already
running `2026-08-12.2` (current code, stale data) a fresh mismatch to
detect via `checkForNewVersion()`, which also fires on `visibilitychange`/
`focus` (index.html:2262-2265) — so foregrounding the culprit device
should trigger the gate immediately, even though the countdown itself may
still stall; clicking "Reload now" directly bypasses that risk.

**Still alive through the afternoon**: hold-checks kept showing ADVANCED
through ~12:29 IST despite the version bump and staff foregrounding their
tabs. Two more suspects raised: Neha's own office PC suffered a hardware
failure and was confirmed powered off (ruling her machine out as the
source once confirmed still-advancing after that); Taskiya left the
office early and her machine's OMS tab was never included in the
foreground/refresh sweep (strong candidate — an abandoned tab on a
still-powered, unattended machine matches the profile exactly). Physical
resolution wasn't separately confirmed before office close; the fix
resolved on its own once the office emptied out (see below).

**RESOLUTION, 2026-08-12 ~21:12-21:23 IST (office closed)**: a fresh
baseline + 7-minute hold taken after office close showed `updatedAt`
byte-for-byte IDENTICAL at both readings (`2026-08-12T13:54:10.800Z`,
i.e. last write ~19:24 IST) — the clobber source went silent, consistent
with whichever machine it was being powered off at end of day. Per the
user's own pre-agreed criteria (unchanged hold = safe to repair), the
pending repair was executed immediately:
- Neha `mso5zsezaebbnr`: `endTime` 10:18→**13:11**, `durationMins`
  15→**188**, `inProgress:false`, `recovered:true`,
  `reconciliationNeeded:false`. Full 3-doc day total re-verified:
  **546m**, zero overlap, all `inProgress:false`.
- Ridhi `mso6h43mexw8hj`: `endTime` 10:20→**19:22**, `durationMins`
  3→**545**, same closed-state fields. Desc explicitly notes this
  corrects the earlier Bug-B auto-close (which had wrongly landed on
  the clobber-corrupted 10:20/3m). Day total re-verified: **545m**.
- Both `merge:true`, desc read-live-and-appended (not overwritten),
  `source:'server'` verify immediately after write (all fields PASS),
  then held and re-verified again at +7min (forced server re-read) —
  **HOLDING clean both times**, first successful repair of the day that
  didn't revert.

**Incident closed.** Root cause chain, for the record: `backstopFullSave()`
still does an unconditional full-array `commitInChunks()` rewrite from
whatever's in a tab's local memory (the original, still-not-fully-fixed
Bug A gap — Stages 3/4 never built); `staleVersionLockout` couldn't catch
this specific tab because it most likely already had current code loaded,
just stale in-memory data (a case Stage B was never designed to catch);
and even a genuine version mismatch has its own escalation bug (countdown
timer not worker-routed, logged above) that could stall the self-reload
indefinitely on a backgrounded tab. All three are real, independent gaps
— version-force alone was never going to be sufficient, exactly as flagged
back when Stage B was first scoped. Stages 3/4 (diff-based backstop,
`diffMode='primary'`) remain the only fix that closes this class of bug
structurally rather than relying on tabs reloading themselves. Not
started — still gated behind the original readiness checklist.

**Second consecutive hold after blanket refresh, 2026-08-12 ~11:30-11:39
IST**: `11:30:49.103Z → 11:39:11.847Z` — advanced again, same lockstep
Neha+Ridhi signature, same ~8-9min cadence (matches Stage A's worker-tick
backstop cadence, throttled). The blanket "everyone hard-refresh or close
their tab" remediation has now failed to stop the clobber across two
consecutive 7-min holds post-remediation. Since a genuine hard refresh
reloads the page and re-fetches fresh data (would clear a stale in-memory
array by construction), a hard refresh that didn't stop this implies
either (a) not every device was actually covered by the sweep — most
likely a phone browser, a second machine, or another browser
profile/window nobody thought to include as "an OMS device" — or (b) a
device believed refreshed didn't actually complete the reload. Source
still not found; escalate the hunt to secondary devices per person
(phones, second PCs, other browser profiles), not just each person's
primary machine, before the next attempt.

## Stage 3/4 (diff-based backstop) — Thursday build plan, scoped 2026-08-13

**Status: read-only planning, nothing built.** This is the authoritative
plan to execute fresh on Thursday. Scoped in response to the live
`backstopFullSave()` delete-by-omission finding below — this is the
structural cure for the clobber class (replaces `diffMode:'shadow'`'s
full-array overwrite), and it's the most dangerous change in the project
because it touches the delete path. Fully mapped before building, per
explicit requirement.

### Current state, confirmed with line refs (2026-08-13)

- `diffMode = 'shadow'` — index.html:1359.
- `saveData()` shadow-mode primary write: original unconditional
  full-array `commitInChunks()` for all three collections —
  index.html:2697-2706, on `periodicSaveInterval`'s 30s plain
  `setInterval` — index.html:8711-8721 (not worker-routed; noted, not in
  scope for this batch).
- `backstopFullSave()` independent full-array rewrite, Stage-A
  worker-tick-driven (~5min) — index.html:1490-1524.
- `syncChangedDocs()` (the dormant primary-mode writer) deletes across
  **all three** collections including `timeLogs`, via ID-set absence
  detection in `diffCollection()` — index.html:1404-1417, 1442-1477.
  `timeLogs` DOES have a client-side delete path; it's just inactive
  while `diffMode='shadow'`.

**Headline finding: `backstopFullSave()` already has its own, separate,
currently-LIVE delete-by-omission block, independent of `diffMode`
entirely** — index.html:1499-1510. `git blame` traces it to commit
`b5d6642`, 2026-08-04, merged and live in `main` since. Runs
unconditionally every ~5 minutes, all three collections. **The
"delete based on a stale local array" risk is not a future Stage C/D
concern — it has been live in production the entire time this
engagement has been running.** Yesterday's clobber happened to leave
docs present-with-wrong-content rather than missing-from-array, so this
specific risk didn't manifest — but nothing about the code prevented it.

**Meeting-cancel dependency, confirmed**: `deleteMeetingBtn`'s handler
(index.html:5490-5507) and the edit-path's old-entry removal
(index.html:5444-5447) both do `planEntries = planEntries.filter(...)`
locally, then `autoSave()` → `saveData()` → shadow-mode's
`commitInChunks()` (index.html:2547-2579), which only ever `batch.set()`s,
never deletes. Cancelling a meeting today does not delete anything from
Firestore at the moment of cancellation — the old docs sit orphaned on
the server until `backstopFullSave()`'s 5-minute sweep catches up. Real,
already-existing gap, independent of Thursday's plan being built at all.

**Mitigating factor, with a caveat**: dashboard auto-refresh
(index.html:5083-5109) reloads `timeLogs` fresh from server every 30s and
correctly rebaselines the snapshot when the dashboard is open — but it's
gated `if (timerRunning) return`, meaning the tabs most prone to array
staleness (someone with a timer running for hours) are exactly the ones
excluded from this safety refresh.

### Stage 0 — guard the live delete block (ships first)

**What it changes**: adds a verify-before-delete guard directly into
`backstopFullSave()`'s existing inline delete block
(index.html:1499-1510). Nothing else touched — `saveData()`,
`syncChangedDocs()`, `diffMode` all remain untouched.

**Why it's safe to prioritize**: provably can only make
`backstopFullSave()` *more conservative* than today — it can skip a
delete that would otherwise have happened, but can never cause a new
delete that wouldn't have happened anyway. Worst-case bug in the guard
itself: a genuine delete gets delayed and logged, not that something
unexpected gets deleted. Categorically safer failure mode than the
current status quo.

**Algorithm** (baseline version — plain read-then-batch-delete; see
hardening note below):

```
for each collection name in [timeLogs, planEntries, projects]:

  localSeenIds     = Set(ids in current in-memory array)
  candidateDeletes = [ id for id in docSnapshots[name].keys()
                        if id NOT in localSeenIds ]   // today's toDelete, unchanged

  if candidateDeletes is empty: done

  // NEW -- fetch only the candidates, source:'server', chunked to
  // Firestore's `in`-query limit (verify exact number against the SDK
  // version in use at build time -- don't hardcode from memory)
  freshServerDocs = getDocs(where(documentId(), 'in', chunk_of(candidateDeletes)))

  confirmedDeletes = []
  for id in candidateDeletes:
    if id NOT in freshServerDocs:
      // already gone -- another tab's cycle beat us, or a benign race.
      docSnapshots[name].delete(id)
      continue

    serverHash    = computeDocHash(freshServerDocs[id])
    lastKnownHash = docSnapshots[name].get(id)

    if serverHash === lastKnownHash:
      // nobody has touched this doc's content since I last knew about
      // it, and it's missing from MY array specifically -- the only
      // explanation is something I did locally. SAFE TO DELETE.
      confirmedDeletes.push(id)
    else:
      // server content changed since I last synced -- someone else has
      // been writing to this doc. Cannot distinguish "I deleted it"
      // from "my array is stale and never learned about their edit."
      // DO NOT DELETE.
      docSnapshots[name].set(id, serverHash)   // resync, stop re-flagging
      console.warn(`[backstop] SKIPPED delete of ${id} (${name}) -- server content changed since last known state, cannot safely infer deletion intent`)

  batch.delete(each id in confirmedDeletes)   // same 450-item chunking as today
```

**Edge cases the fixture must cover**:
1. Candidate doesn't exist on server at all → benign no-op, clean up
   snapshot silently, no warning (expected race between tabs' cycles).
2. Server hash matches last-known → confirmed local intent → delete
   goes through. Must include a genuine meeting-cancel case here — this
   is the branch that has to keep working correctly.
3. Server hash differs from last-known → skip, resync, warn. The branch
   that actually closes the danger.
4. Verification fetch itself fails (network error) → fail closed: treat
   the whole failed chunk as unverified this cycle, delete nothing from
   it, retry next cycle. Never delete on an unconfirmed state.

**Post-baseline hardening, not required for the initial fixture but the
target design once the baseline is proven**: wrap each confirmed delete
in `runTransaction()` — re-read and re-verify the hash *inside* the
transaction, immediately before deleting, to close the TOCTOU gap
between the verification read and the batch delete (a small window where
someone could write to the doc in between). Costs more per delete
(individual transactions vs. one batch) but deletes should be rare, so
the tradeoff is worth it given what's at stake — this is a delete, not a
reversible overwrite.

**Fixture**: the four edge cases above, mocked Firestore (no live
project needed for the fixture itself).

**Risk**: low — additive safety check on an already-shipped, already-live
path.

**Gate before next**: fixture green + a live observation window (a day
or two) watching for `[backstop] SKIPPED delete` warnings in production.

**Deploy**: fully independent, ships alone, first.

**Diagnostic note — `[backstop] SKIPPED delete` warnings are signal, not
noise**: each one that fires in production is a doc that would have been
silently, permanently deleted under the current (unguarded) code. Watch
these individually after deploy — don't just confirm the count is
nonzero-or-zero, actually look at what each flagged doc was.

**Build reminder — do not rush this despite the "provably safe" framing**:
provably-safer-in-logic still means this touches the live delete path in
production. Full fixtures, a preview deploy, and a quiet-window rollout
(not merged straight to `main` during active office hours) — same
discipline as every other write-path change this project has shipped,
not an exception just because the risk analysis is favorable.

### Stage C — full spec (once Stage 0 has soaked)

Same algorithm as Stage 0 above, reused rather than redesigned — wired
through `syncChangedDocs()` so `saveData()` can share it once `diffMode`
flips, instead of living only in `backstopFullSave()`'s own inline block.
Stage 0 having already run live for real days is what turns this from
"design and prove a new algorithm under time pressure" into "port an
already-proven algorithm to a second call site."

### Stage A rescoped, 2026-08-13 — meeting feature under consideration for removal

Team doesn't schedule meetings via the app (texts instead); feature is
rarely used. Considering deleting it outright instead of building Stage
A's explicit-delete fix for it. Scoped read-only, nothing changed:

**Removal footprint**: `scheduleMeetingBtn` (index.html:548), the meeting
modal (index.html:856-920ish), three handlers —
`openMeetingModal()`/`saveMeetingBtn`/`deleteMeetingBtn` listeners
(index.html:5381-5507) — and the `isMeeting` display branch in the Weekly
Planner's chip-rendering (index.html:5281-5289).

**Dependency check, confirmed clean**: Time Report (`buildTrPerson`/
`buildTrDetail`) operates on `timeLogs` only, never `planEntries` —
meetings never factor into any hours total. `estHours` (the field
meetings populate with real duration) is write-only everywhere in the
codebase — searched every reference, never read/summed anywhere, even
regular plan entries hardcode it to 0. The timer widget's task-switcher
(`renderWidgetTaskList()`, index.html:7083-7104) doesn't filter out
`isMeeting` entries today (a scheduled meeting can currently be clicked
to start a timer against it, since it carries a real `projectId`) — not
a blocker, just confirms there's no separate "meetings view"; removal
naturally stops this once meeting-creation is gone.

**Current server-side footprint**: zero. `isMeeting:true` query against
`planEntries`, `source:'server'`, returned 0 docs — nothing to migrate
or clean up if removed.

**Full delete-by-omission inventory** (the actual question this needs to
answer — is meeting-cancel the only gap, or are there others):

| Delete action | Mechanism | Status |
|---|---|---|
| Delete user (admin) | Explicit: `users` doc delete + `planEntries` cascade (live-queried batch) + `presence` doc delete + `timeLogs` explicitly finalized, not deleted — index.html:6660-6688 | Safe |
| Delete single plan entry (non-meeting) | Explicit `planEntries.doc(id).delete()` — index.html:5368, comment names the exact danger | Safe, already fixed once |
| Cancel leave request | Explicit `leaveRequests.doc(id).delete()` — index.html:6552 (also outside the 3-collection backstop sweep entirely — this call is the only thing that ever removes one) | Safe |
| Delete holiday / client / typology | Local array mutation only, no explicit delete | Not applicable — these are fields inside the single `companyData/squareDB` doc, not separate collections |
| **Cancel/edit meeting** | **Local filter only, no explicit delete** | **The only real, currently-active gap** |
| Delete project | No delete UI exists at all | Not applicable |
| Delete a `timeLogs` entry | No delete UI exists at all — flag-don't-truncate means entries are only ever flagged/closed/finalized | Not applicable today |

**Conclusion**: meeting-cancel is the only currently-active delete-by-
omission path in the app. If the meeting feature is removed, **Stage A
as originally scoped is eliminated** — no other live gap needs an
explicit-delete fix. **Stage 0 stays in the plan regardless of this
decision** — not because of meetings, but because `timeLogs` has zero
legitimate delete paths today, meaning any future bug that empties part
of that array has no honest reason to look like an intentional delete.
Stage 0's guard is what protects against that unknown case, independent
of whatever happens with meetings.

**Decision, 2026-08-13**: CONFIRMED — remove the meeting feature entirely
rather than build the explicit-delete fix for it. Nothing depends on
meeting data (confirmed above), zero docs to migrate. Stage A is
rescoped accordingly below. Not executed yet — still Thursday's plan,
nothing built.

### Revised stage sequence

1. **Stage 0** — guard the live `backstopFullSave()` delete block (above).
   Ships first: most urgent (already-live risk) and provably lowest-risk
   change in the sequence. **STATUS: DONE.** Built on
   `feat-backstop-delete-guard`, 4 fixture cases green (extracted real
   function source via brace-matching, not a reimplementation — see
   `test/stage-0-backstop-delete-guard.test.js`), merged to `main`,
   deployed as `2026-08-15.1` on 2026-08-15. Smoke-checked post-deploy:
   `HTTP 200`, correct version string embedded, full-size response;
   `vercel.json` confirmed byte-identical (empty diff pre-merge) — cron
   untouched. Office closed through the weekend (15/16 Aug), so this
   soaks with zero live traffic until staff return — a deliberate,
   accepted quiet window, not a gap in verification. **Interim signal
   until Stage F's logging exists**: once staff are back, do an indirect
   proxy spot-check — pull flagged/recently-touched docs across users and
   look for anything that looks wrongly missing or unexpectedly lingering
   compared to what the pre-Stage-0 code would have done. Indirect, not a
   substitute for real telemetry, but the only signal available until
   Stage F ships.
2. **Stage E** — route the version-gate countdown timer through the
   reliable tick worker instead of plain `setInterval` (the bug that let
   yesterday's stale tab never self-reload). Independent, no ordering
   dependency either way, low risk. **STATUS: DONE.** Built on
   `feat-version-gate-worker-countdown`. Design: `showVersionGateModal()`
   now records a wall-clock deadline (`versionGateCountdownDeadlineAt`)
   instead of relying solely on the interval's own decrement;
   `checkForNewVersion()` — already guaranteed to run every ~60s via the
   worker on any ongoing mismatch, regardless of tab visibility — checks
   that deadline first, before anything else, and fires the reload
   directly if it's passed. The visual 1s interval still exists for a
   smooth countdown when foregrounded, but the guarantee no longer
   depends on it ever completing. Added a light reentrancy guard to
   `forceReloadToVersion()` since the reload can now fire from two
   independent places. 4 fixture cases in
   `test/stage-e-version-gate-worker-countdown.test.js` (simulated stall
   still reloads via the worker-tick path; no premature fire before the
   deadline; manual "Reload now" click still works and clears state
   cleanly; a double-fire doesn't double-count the attempts counter) —
   all passing, plus all 41 prior Stage A/B/0 fixtures re-run clean, no
   regressions. Isolation confirmed: zero mentions of `backstopFullSave`/
   `diffMode`/`saveData` in the diff. Merged to `main`, deployed as
   `2026-08-15.2` on 2026-08-15. Pre-deploy: confirmed clean via a
   5-minute freeze check across docs from all three reconciled dates
   (12/13/14 Aug) plus a full re-verification of all 16 reconciled
   totals — all intact, nothing disturbed by a stray open tab during the
   Stage 0 deploy. Post-deploy: `HTTP 200`, correct version string
   embedded, full-size response, `vercel.json` confirmed byte-identical
   (empty diff pre-merge).
3. **Stage A (rescoped) — REMOVE the meeting feature entirely**, not
   "add an explicit delete for it." Scope: `scheduleMeetingBtn`, the
   meeting modal, all 3 handlers (`openMeetingModal`/`saveMeetingBtn`/
   `deleteMeetingBtn`), and the `isMeeting` rendering branch in the
   Weekly Planner — full inventory and confirmed-zero cross-dependencies
   in the section above. This removes the meeting-cancel delete-by-
   omission gap by deleting the code path that creates it, rather than
   patching it. Stage 0 still fully applies and is unaffected by this —
   it protects `timeLogs` against unknown future delete bugs, which has
   nothing to do with meetings. **STATUS: DONE.** Built on
   `feat-remove-meeting-scheduler`. Scope double-checked against the
   two other, genuinely separate "meeting" things in the app before
   touching anything — confirmed the removal touches only the
   scheduler: "Meeting" as a project phase/typology
   (`Project Management – Client Meeting`, line ~1250) and the Time Log
   modal's `Internal Meeting`/`Vendor Meeting` activity-type options
   both left completely untouched, verified by exact-string grep after
   the edit. Three render checks passed before merge: plain task chips
   render correctly with the `isMeeting` branch gone; legacy
   `isMeeting:true` docs would degrade gracefully if any existed
   (traced `openPlanModal()`/`canEditPlanEntry()` — both only read
   standard plan-entry fields every meeting doc already had) and none
   currently exist in Firestore (`0`, reconfirmed live); no layout gap
   where the button was (flex+gap container, confirmed by inspection).
   All 6 existing test suites re-run clean post-removal (113 checks, 0
   failures). User eyeballed the Weekly Planner on the Vercel preview
   directly before approving. Merged to `main`, deployed as
   `2026-08-15.3` on 2026-08-15. Post-deploy: `HTTP 200`, correct
   version string embedded, response size dropped as expected
   (489606 bytes, down from 498365), zero `scheduleMeetingBtn`/
   `meetingModal` matches in the live served HTML confirming the
   removal actually shipped, `vercel.json` confirmed byte-identical
   (empty diff pre-merge).
4. **Stage C spec finalization** — now substantially de-risked, since the
   hard part (the verification algorithm) will already be proven live
   via Stage 0 before it's ever wired into the shared diff engine.
5. **Stage B** — fixture-proof `syncChangedDocs()`'s delete path against
   real (throwaway) Firestore docs. Must test the **guarded** version
   (incorporating Stage 0's algorithm), not the current unguarded
   implementation — scope updated accordingly from the original plan.
6. **Stage F (new, 2026-08-15) — delete-path telemetry, before/alongside
   C+D, not deferred past it.** Found during Stage 0's post-deploy
   discussion: `[backstop] SKIPPED delete`/actual-delete events are
   `console.warn`-only, invisible outside whichever browser tab happens
   to have DevTools open at that exact moment — no remote observability
   at all today. Not a Stage 0 defect (Stage 0's safety doesn't depend on
   being watched), but a real gap for trusting the delete path going
   forward, especially once C+D make `syncChangedDocs()` the primary
   writer and this class of event becomes routine rather than rare.
   Scope: have the guard write a small, queryable Firestore record
   (separate collection, e.g. `backstopDeleteEvents`) whenever it
   confirms a delete OR skips one, capturing docId/collection/outcome/
   reason/timestamp. **Needs careful design, not a quick add**: a write
   inside `backstopFullSave()` itself must not reintroduce the class of
   risk this whole effort exists to reduce — deterministic doc ID (avoid
   duplicate/runaway records across retries), a bounded retention
   strategy (avoid unbounded collection growth from a 5-minute-cadence
   writer), and confirmation this new write path is itself exempt from
   -- or correctly subject to -- the same delete-by-omission concerns
   Stage 0 was built to guard against. Do before or alongside C+D, not
   after — this is what turns "trust me, it's conservative" into
   "here's the log," right when the stakes go up.
7. **Stage C + Stage D, shipped together, Saturday** — `backstopFullSave()`
   → diff-based (C) and `diffMode` `'shadow'`→`'primary'` (D) ship as one
   unit, not two independently-shippable stages. Once C replaces the
   backstop's own delete block with the shared `syncChangedDocs()`, the
   backstop's independent-safety-net character is gone — it shares fate
   with what D is about to promote to primary. Shipping C alone first
   would leave a window where the backstop's delete behavior changed but
   hadn't been proven under real primary-path load yet, worse than either
   the current state or the fully-migrated state.

**"Soaked clean" criteria for the post-D observation window**: zero
unexplained `toDelete` entries in the diff logs for any doc without a
traceable same-session cause; stable doc counts per collection sampled
daily; at least one full day from a tab that stays open all day without
reloading (the exact profile that exposed both this week's clobber and
the original Bug A backstop gap); the reconciliation-queue view staying
free of unexplained entries. **Extended 2026-08-16** — see the required
daily ground-truth spot-check below.

## C-spec review and pre-build decisions, 2026-08-16

**Safety net confirmed in place before any C/D code was touched**, per
explicit sequencing instruction (safety net before code, not after):
Firestore PITR enabled 2026-08-16 (7-day rolling, earliest version time
08:39 IST, before any C/D write could exist) as the primary data-recovery
mechanism; static export
`gs://square-office-mgmt-backups-asia/pre-cd-migration-20260816`
confirmed complete (`.overall_export_metadata` present) as a secondary,
non-expiring snapshot. A 6-minute freeze-check across the most-recent
docs in all three backstop collections, the reconciliation queue, and
Angana's known-cleared residue doc (`msmrahdqbmqg0w`) confirmed frozen
and clean same day, before proceeding. Full detail in
`ROLLBACK-PLAN-CD-MIGRATION.md`.

**C-spec walkthrough conducted 2026-08-16, read-only, against the real
current code** — `backstopFullSave()` (index.html:1495-1580),
`diffCollection()`/`syncChangedDocs()` (index.html:1368-1441), the
`diffMode` gate in `saveData()` (index.html:2687-2849). Confirmed the
exact verify-then-delete algorithm and its full edge-case table
(server-doc-gone, hash-match, hash-mismatch, unknown-candidate,
verify-fetch-failure, delete-chunk-failure, concurrent-tab-race — all
fail-closed, matching Stage 0's discipline exactly). Two decisions made
as a direct result:

1. **Transaction wrapping — NOT added now.** Confirmed the guard, as it
   exists today, is a plain server read followed by a separate
   `batch.delete()`, not wrapped in `runTransaction` — a genuine, narrow
   TOCTOU window exists between verify and commit (sub-second, bounded
   by one `Promise.all` plus one batch commit). **Decision: keep the
   current fetch-then-batch pattern unchanged for Stage C.** Rationale:
   it's the exact algorithm already proven live since Stage 0 shipped;
   adding `runTransaction` now would deploy an unproven variant onto the
   primary write path, defeating the "prove before promote" reasoning
   the whole staged rollout depends on. The residual window is
   sub-second, and this deploy happens into a closed office with no
   concurrent writers regardless. **Logged as a future hardening
   option** — add only if the soak reveals an actual need, not
   preemptively.
2. **Loss of `backstopFullSave()`'s full-rewrite self-heal — accepted
   consciously, with a mandatory mitigation.** Converting
   `backstopFullSave()` to reuse the shared guarded diff/delete path
   (Stage C) trades a dumb-but-bulletproof unconditional rewrite (which
   self-corrects any silent hash-logic bug within 5 minutes) for the
   smart-but-unproven diff engine — which would NOT surface a
   hash-detection bug in `toWrite`/`toDelete` counts at all, since that
   failure mode is silent by construction. **Mitigation, mandatory, not
   optional**: a daily ground-truth spot-check (pull a sample of docs
   directly from Firestore via `source:'server'`, compare against
   expected values independent of the app's own hash bookkeeping) runs
   every day for the first 5-7 days immediately after D goes primary.
   This is the explicit replacement for the lost self-heal, not a
   "when convenient" check. Added to `DELIVERY-ACCEPTANCE-CRITERIA.md`
   criterion 1 as a required, dated checklist item, and folded into the
   "soaked clean" criteria above.

**Status**: both decisions logged 2026-08-16. Stage B built same day —
see next entry.

## Stage B — guarded `syncChangedDocs()` delete path, built 2026-08-16

Built on `feat-cd-guarded-syncchangeddocs`, off `main`. Extracted Stage
0's verify-then-delete algorithm (unchanged) out of `backstopFullSave()`'s
inline block into a new shared `verifyAndFilterDeletes(name,
candidateIds)` helper (index.html, just before `backstopFullSave()`).
Two call sites now use it:
- `backstopFullSave()` — pure refactor, calls the shared helper instead
  of its own inline copy, byte-for-byte identical behavior (confirmed by
  the pre-existing Stage 0 test passing unchanged once its extraction
  list was updated to include the new helper — see below).
- `syncChangedDocs()` — the actual Stage C change. Its delete path
  previously deleted every `toDelete` candidate unconditionally; now
  confirms each one against fresh server truth first, identical
  fail-closed discipline to Stage 0.

`diffMode` untouched, still `'shadow'` — `syncChangedDocs()` is not
called in production today, so this change is completely inert until
Stage D flips the flag. Confirmed via diff review: zero mentions of
`diffMode` in the change.

**Fixtures — `test/stage-b-guarded-syncchangeddocs.test.js`, 21 checks**:
hash-match candidate deleted (across all three collections, not just
one); hash-mismatch candidate survives; verify-fetch failure fails
closed; the required case — a doc that exists server-side but was NEVER
part of `docSnapshots` never even becomes a delete candidate, proven
through the real extracted `diffCollection()`/`computeCollectionDiffs()`,
not asserted by inspection; writes proceed normally in the same cycle as
a skipped delete; an already-gone server doc is a no-op, not an error;
the guard is a strict subset of what the old unconditional code would
have deleted; a failed delete-commit leaves the id in `docSnapshots` for
automatic retry next cycle.

**Real Firestore round-trip — scratchpad
`stage-b-real-firestore-guard-test.js`, 12 checks**: `TESTGUARDB-`
prefixed throwaway docs across all three collections, real
`source:'server'` reads, real `db.batch()` writes/deletes against the
production project. Hash-match docs genuinely deleted, hash-mismatch
docs genuinely survived with content intact, confirmed by re-reading
server state after the cycle (not inferred from mock call logs). All
test docs deleted in a `finally` block regardless of outcome; a
separate, independent post-hoc check confirmed zero `TESTGUARDB-` docs
remain.

**Regression suite**: 157 checks, 0 failures across 9 files. One real
regression caught and fixed along the way: the pre-existing Stage 0 test
(`test/stage-0-backstop-delete-guard.test.js`) extracts `backstopFullSave()`
into its own isolated sandbox and didn't know about the new shared
`verifyAndFilterDeletes()` helper it now calls — threw
`ReferenceError` until the test's extraction list was updated to include
it (one-line fix, test-infrastructure staleness, not a production bug).

**Isolation confirmed**: `git diff main --stat` — only `index.html` (the
guard extraction + two call-site changes) and the one-line Stage-0 test
fix; `vercel.json` byte-identical (empty diff). No `diffMode` flip
anywhere in the diff.

**Status: MERGED and DEPLOYED, 2026-08-16.** Reviewed and approved same
day. Merged `feat-cd-guarded-syncchangeddocs` → `main`
(`db1dbcd`), bumped `APP_VERSION`/`version.json` to `2026-08-16.1`
(`abc9ceb`). Pre-merge: full regression suite re-run clean on `main`
post-merge (157 checks, 0 failures), `vercel.json` confirmed
byte-identical against pre-merge `main`. Post-deploy:
`https://squareoffice.vercel.app/` returns `HTTP 200`, embedded
`APP_VERSION = '2026-08-16.1'` confirmed, response size 493197 bytes
(in line with prior deploys). `diffMode` still `'shadow'` in

## Stage C+D — diff-based backstop + diffMode primary flip, built 2026-08-16

Built on `feat-cd-primary-diffmode`, off `main` (post Stage B merge).
Both decisions from the C-spec review (transaction wrapping deferred,
self-heal loss accepted with mandatory mitigation) carried forward
unchanged — nothing revisited.

**Stage C** — `backstopFullSave()` converted from its unconditional
full-array rewrite (`commitInChunks()` × 3 + its own inline delete
block) to the same `computeCollectionDiffs()` + `syncChangedDocs()`
cycle `saveData()` uses when `diffMode` is `'primary'`. Both callers now
share one guarded write/delete implementation, on different cadences
(this one every 5 minutes, independent of `saveData()`'s own tick) —
this is the change that gives up the unconditional-rewrite self-heal,
exactly as scoped in the C-spec review.

**Found and fixed while building C, not before**: `syncChangedDocs()`
only ever maintained `docSnapshots` on a successful write/delete, never
`backstopContentSnapshots` (the delete guard's own "last known content"
baseline). This was harmless through Stage B because
`backstopFullSave()`'s unconditional rewrite rebuilt
`backstopContentSnapshots` from scratch every single cycle regardless —
once that rebuild is gone (this stage), any doc ever written via
`syncChangedDocs()` would carry a **permanently stale** content hash.
Not unsafe (the guard still fails closed — it would just skip that
doc's eventual delete forever, never actually completing it), but a
real functional regression this build would otherwise have shipped
silently. Fixed: `syncChangedDocs()` now refreshes
`backstopContentSnapshots` on every successful write (to the new
content's hash) and clears it on every successful delete. Also seeded
`backstopContentSnapshots` at `loadData()` time (previously only ever
populated by the now-removed unconditional rebuild), closing a
one-cycle-delay gap for any doc loaded but never itself written or
deleted in a given session.

**Stage D** — `diffMode` flipped `'shadow'` → `'primary'`
(index.html:1323). `saveData()` now writes via `syncChangedDocs()` as
the real persistence path, not the full-array rewrite.

**Tests**: `test/stage-0-backstop-delete-guard.test.js` removed —
it tested the delete-guard logic as it existed INLINE inside
`backstopFullSave()`, which no longer exists there at all (moved to the
shared `verifyAndFilterDeletes()` helper in Stage B, already covered by
`test/stage-b-guarded-syncchangeddocs.test.js`'s 21 checks via its real
call site). Replaced with `test/stage-cd-backstop-diff-wrapper.test.js`
(9 checks): every guard clause (`staleVersionLockout`, `dataLoaded`,
`currentUser`, `window._saveInProgress`), the `_saveInProgress`
lifecycle including the exception path (reset in `finally` even when
`computeCollectionDiffs()` throws), and two end-to-end cases confirming
the wrapper actually reaches the real guarded delete path (not just
that the right function names appear in source). Added CASE 9 to
`test/stage-b-guarded-syncchangeddocs.test.js` (5 checks, a genuine
two-cycle scenario: write a doc, then delete it in a later cycle)
specifically proving the `backstopContentSnapshots` write-refresh fix —
without it, this exact case would fail (the delete would be
incorrectly skipped). **Full regression suite: 164 checks, 0 failures
across 9 files.**

**Real Firestore round-trip** (scratchpad
`stage-cd-real-firestore-primary-cycle-test.js`, `TESTCD-` prefixed
throwaway docs in `timeLogs`, cleaned up after, independently
re-verified clean): run twice — once calling `syncChangedDocs()`
directly, once via the `backstopFullSave()` wrapper — each proving,
against genuine server reads/writes: an edited doc's new content lands;
a genuinely-deleted doc (hash-confirmed) is actually removed; and
**an untouched doc is never rewritten at all** — its `updatedAt` stays
byte-identical after the cycle. That last point is the actual
correctness guarantee this whole migration exists to deliver, proven
against real Firestore rather than asserted from mock call logs.
**10 checks, 0 failures.**

**Isolation confirmed**: only `index.html` and `test/` changed;
`vercel.json` byte-identical against `main`.

**Two confirms answered** (asked before this deploy is allowed to
proceed):
1. **Production check that `diffMode` reads `'primary'`**: two
   independent methods. (a) Source-level, same pattern already used for
   every post-deploy `APP_VERSION` check this whole engagement:
   `curl -s https://squareoffice.vercel.app/ | grep -o "let diffMode = '[^']*'"`
   — the authoritative check, confirms what's actually deployed and
   served, not just one tab's in-memory state. (b) Runtime, on a live
   tab: open DevTools Console and type the bare identifier `diffMode`
   (no `window.` prefix — top-level `let`/`const` in a classic script
   never attach to `window` as object properties, but ARE visible to
   code evaluated in the page's global lexical scope, which is exactly
   what the DevTools console REPL does; the file's own Stage-3/4 design
   comment already documents this as the reason it's "live-flippable
   from the DevTools console... no extra plumbing needed"). Use (a) to
   confirm the deploy itself is correct; use (b) to additionally confirm
   a specific open tab has actually loaded the new code (catches a
   stale-cache tab that (a) alone wouldn't).
2. **First ground-truth spot-check to run immediately post-deploy**:
   given the office is closed today (zero live user activity to
   cross-check against), Day 1's instance of the mandatory spot-check
   (DELIVERY-ACCEPTANCE-CRITERIA.md criterion 1) is necessarily a
   staticity check, not a content-correctness check — the same
   freeze-check shape already used for the pre-C/D safety-net gate:
   pull recent docs directly (`source:'server'`) across all three
   collections, capture doc counts and `updatedAt` values immediately
   before the deploy, then re-pull the same sample a few minutes after
   and confirm both counts and `updatedAt` are unchanged (correct,
   since nothing local actually changed — this is precisely what the
   real Firestore test above already proved in miniature, now run once
   for real in production), plus confirm the reconciliation queue is
   still 0. Once staff return and real activity resumes, subsequent
   days' spot-checks become genuine content-correctness comparisons
   (displayed UI totals vs. server truth) rather than pure staticity
   checks.

**Status: MERGED and DEPLOYED, 2026-08-16.** Approved after a full
hash-consistency audit (every `docSnapshots`/`backstopContentSnapshots`
write site traced individually — `snapshotCollection()`,
`snapshotBackstopContent()`, `diffCollection()`'s `toWrite` hash,
`syncChangedDocs()`'s write/delete handlers, `verifyAndFilterDeletes()`'s
mismatch branch — confirmed `docSnapshots` only ever receives
`computeDocHash` values and `backstopContentSnapshots` only ever receives

## C/D soak checklist — dated, REQUIRED daily through 2026-08-22

**Why this exists**: converting `backstopFullSave()` to diff-based
removed the unconditional full-rewrite that used to self-heal a silent
hash-logic bug automatically every 5 minutes. Post-C+D, a bug that makes
the hash comparison wrongly report "unchanged" produces **zero** signal
in `toWrite`/`toDelete` counts, logs, or the reconciliation queue — it
is invisible to every other mechanism this system has. The daily
ground-truth spot-check is the only thing that catches it. Confirmed
2026-08-16: PITR and the static export are both recovery mechanisms for
data already written badly; neither detects an edit that silently never
got written at all. **Skipping a day during this window is a real gap
in coverage, not a formality.**

| Day | Date | Day of week | Office status | Requirement |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 2026-08-16 | Sun | Closed | **DONE** — staticity check passed (see Stage C+D entry above) |
| 2 | 2026-08-17 | Mon | **Staff back** | **DONE — CLEAN** (see "C/D soak — Day 2" entry below) |
| 3 | 2026-08-18 | Tue | Open | **DONE — CLEAN** (unattended-stability check, see entry below) |
| 4 | 2026-08-19 | Wed | Open | **DONE — CLEAN** (2 consecutive clean unattended day-ends, see entry below) |
| 5 | 2026-08-20 | Thu | Open | **DONE — CLEAN** (3 consecutive clean unattended day-ends, see entry below) |
| 6 | 2026-08-21 | Fri | Open | **DONE — CLEAN** (4 consecutive clean unattended day-ends, see entry below) |
| 7 | 2026-08-22 | Sat | Closed | **NOT REQUIRED — soak closed early, 2026-08-21.** See "SOAK CLOSED" entry below. |

**Day 2 (2026-08-17, Monday) is the most important day in this entire
window, flagged specially.** Every check run so far — the pre-deploy
freeze-check, the post-deploy staticity check — has been against a
closed, empty office: zero concurrent writers, nothing to actually
exercise the diff engine's write path under real conditions. Monday is
the **first genuine test**: real multi-user activity, real concurrent
writes across `timeLogs`/`planEntries`/`projects`, and — critically —
the exact profile that exposed both the original Bug A backstop gap and
the mid-August clobber incident in the first place: staff opening a tab
in the morning and leaving it open all day without reloading. If the
diff-based primary path has a problem that only manifests under real
concurrent load (a race the vm-sandbox and single-writer real-Firestore
tests couldn't reproduce), Monday is where it would first show up. Treat
Monday's check as higher-scrutiny than the rest of the week, not a
routine repeat of Day 1.

**Per-day pass criteria** (every day, not just Day 2):
1. **Sample docs across all three collections** (`timeLogs`,
   `planEntries`, `projects`) via `source:'server'` reads — on office
   days, include specifically the docs of whoever's actively working
   that day, not just a random sample.
2. **Compare server truth against expected/displayed values** — on
   closed days (staticity check, like Day 1): confirm the sample is
   byte-identical to the previous check, nothing moved. On office days
   (real content check, starting Day 2): compare what the app UI
   actually displays for a few active users against what's genuinely on
   the server — the real point of this whole check, not reachable until
   staff are back.
3. **Per-collection doc counts stable**, accounting only for expected
   growth from real activity that day — no unexplained drops.
4. **Reconciliation queue still 0** (`reconciliationNeeded:true` count).
5. **Zero unexplained `toDelete` entries** — every delete traceable to a
   real, known action (plan-entry delete, leave-request cancel, admin
   user-delete); anything else is exactly the "roll back now" tripwire
   from `ROLLBACK-PLAN-CD-MIGRATION.md` §2.

**Any anomaly on any day → STOP, investigate, rollback plan ready.**
Do not wait to see if it resolves itself, do not attribute an unexplained
finding to "probably fine" — `ROLLBACK-PLAN-CD-MIGRATION.md` §1 (Vercel
Instant Rollback + git revert) and §3-4 (PITR primary, export secondary)
are both in place specifically for this window. A rollback here resets
the soak clock — the next attempt starts back at Day 1, per
`DELIVERY-ACCEPTANCE-CRITERIA.md` criterion 1's existing fail condition.

**Status**: Days 1-6 done, all clean. **Day 7 formally waived — see
"SOAK CLOSED, 2026-08-21" below.**

## Rollback git-revert dry run — verified 2026-08-16, before Monday

Closes half of the open item flagged in `DELIVERY-ACCEPTANCE-CRITERIA.md`
criterion 5 (git revert mechanics never actually exercised, only
described). Wanted proven before Day 2's high-scrutiny window, not
discovered to be broken under pressure if a real rollback were ever
needed.

**Method**: local-only throwaway branch (`throwaway-rollback-dry-run-
20260816`, created from `main` at `705665e`, never pushed to origin),
running the exact command sequence from `ROLLBACK-PLAN-CD-MIGRATION.md`
§1b against the real C+D commits:
```
git revert 705665e --no-edit      # the version-bump commit
git revert -m 1 22d5c96 --no-edit # the C+D merge commit
```

**Results**:
- Both reverts applied with **zero conflicts** — clean, automatic merge.
- `git diff abc9ceb -- index.html version.json test/ vercel.json` —
  **empty**. The reverted tree is byte-identical to the exact commit
  `main` was at immediately before the C+D merge, not merely similar.
- `APP_VERSION`/`version.json` back to `2026-08-16.1`, `diffMode` back
  to `'shadow'` — confirmed directly in the reverted files.
- `vercel.json` empty diff throughout — confirmed untouched by the
  revert process itself, not just by the original deploy.
- Syntax check clean; full regression suite **157 passed, 0 failed** —
  the exact same count as when Stage B alone was live, independently
  confirming this is genuinely that known-good historical state, not a
  coincidentally-similar one.
- Cleanup: switched back to `main` (untouched throughout, confirmed via
  `git log`/`git status`), throwaway branch deleted
  (`git branch -D`) — never pushed, so no remote residue to clean up
  either.

**What this proves**: if `git revert 705665e && git revert -m 1
22d5c96 && git push origin main` is ever needed for real, the commands
will apply cleanly and produce exactly the pre-C+D state — no merge
conflicts to resolve under pressure, no surprises.

**What's still open**: the Vercel Instant Rollback / Promote-to-
Production dashboard walkthrough — requires the user's own dashboard
access, not something exercisable from this environment. Remains the
one item in `DELIVERY-ACCEPTANCE-CRITERIA.md` criterion 5 not yet
verified.
