**Orphan-recovery-modal fix (the Taskiya self-report bug, "Glitch 3") —
FIXED and DEPLOYED, 2026-08-15.** Confirmed independent of the C/D
migration batch at scoping time — `showOrphanRecoveryModal()`/
`restoreTimerState()` is purely `localStorage`-based, zero coupling to
`diffMode`/`backstopFullSave()`/the delete path — so it shipped
separately, ahead of C/D, as a genuinely quick isolated win.

Root cause: `restoreTimerState()` decided whether to prompt for a
self-report purely from localStorage elapsed time, with zero awareness
of whether the session it referred to was already resolved server-side
by something else in the meantime (EOD-close, Bug B, a manual repair)
— exactly what happened to Taskiya's 12 Aug day (a genuine 420m doc
already correctly closed, then a spurious 510m self-report created on
top of it by a stale browser tab that never got the memo).

Fix, built on `fix-orphan-recovery-duplicate-guard`: before prompting,
`restoreTimerState()` now checks the SPECIFIC session the local state
refers to (via `sessionLogId`, already present on every `timerState`
record) against server truth. Only suppresses the prompt on POSITIVE
confirmation (doc exists AND `inProgress:false`) — any other outcome
(doc missing, fetch fails, no `sessionLogId` at all) falls through to
the existing prompt behavior unchanged, same fail-closed principle as
the Stage 0 delete guard: only act on a confirmed signal, default to
asking a human rather than guessing when uncertain either way.

Confirmed the read only fires in the genuinely rare orphan case, not on
every page load — two early returns in `restoreTimerState()` (no saved
state at all; saved state not `running:true`) sit before the
`hours >= MAX_SESSION_HOURS` (12h) check that gates the new guard, so
a normal login, a cleanly-stopped timer, or an ordinary few-hours
session never reaches it.

**Explicitly does NOT address Glitch 4** (Bug-B-truncated sessions
under-logging, entangled with the flag-don't-truncate recovery
architecture) — a session Bug B closed short still reads
`inProgress:false`, so this guard correctly suppresses the duplicate
prompt there too, but the underlying short duration stays exactly as
wrong as before. Glitch 4 stays logged, untouched, for a dedicated
session — not a quick pre-C/D win.

10 fixture cases in `test/fix-orphan-recovery-duplicate-guard.test.js`:
already-resolved session discarded with no modal; genuinely-open
session prompts exactly as today; a deleted/missing session doc falls
through to the prompt without crashing; a legacy state with no
`sessionLogId` falls through without crashing; a bonus case proves a
verify-fetch failure also fails closed to the prompt. All 8 existing
test suites re-run clean (136 checks total, 0 failures). Isolation
confirmed: 25 lines added, only inside `restoreTimerState()`'s own
scope, `vercel.json`/`api/` untouched. Merged to `main`, deployed as
`2026-08-15.5`. Post-deploy: `HTTP 200`, correct version string,
`vercel.json` confirmed byte-identical (empty diff pre-merge).

## Biometric anomaly-check — replaced with a session-marker design (2026-08-13)

**Supersedes an earlier threshold-based approach** (an "implausible-total"
guard that would flag sessions exceeding some fuzzy ceiling and guess at
exceptions) — that version isn't itself written down in this file (it
was discussed but never logged), so this entry stands as the current and
only backlog record for this item going forward.

**New design, from Ranit**: removes the ambiguity at the source instead
of detecting around it. Add a session marker — Office / Site Visit / WFH
— defaulting to Office, one tap to change only when it differs. Then the
biometric check becomes unambiguous instead of probabilistic:
- Office-tagged time → must be ≤ biometric presence (Work+OT).
- Site Visit / WFH time → real work, **excluded** from the biometric
  ceiling entirely (biometric can't capture work that didn't happen in
  the office, so it shouldn't be compared against it).

No fuzzy thresholds needed — the tag itself tells the reconciliation
logic definitively whether biometric applies to a given session, instead
of inferring it from whether a total merely looks "too high."

**Bonus value**: real visibility into the office/site/remote work split
(feeds a future KPI/quality layer), and nightly biometric reconciliation
(`api/biometric-sync.js`) can handle off-site sessions correctly instead
of misreading them as anomalies.

**Design constraint, explicit**: default MUST be Office (the common
case, zero action required) with Site/WFH a single quick tap — if
tagging requires more friction than that, low adoption defeats the
purpose entirely.

**Scope**: a real feature — new field on session/timeLogs data, UI for
the tap-to-change control, and updated check logic in the biometric
reconciliation path. Not a Thursday-plan item — scoped for the
OMS-v2/monitoring phase, after Stage 3/4 lands, pairing the marker and
the anomaly-check logic together as one unit rather than shipping the
field without the logic that uses it (or vice versa).

**Status**: design decided, not built. Read-only/planning only.

## Glitch 5 — miss-punch fallback stretch-to-now (label established 2026-08-15)

**Established name, confirmed by Ranit 2026-08-15**: "Glitch 5" =
`closeReconciliationNeededSessions()`'s fallback-routing behavior in
`api/biometric-sync.js` when a session has no biometric OUT (`--:--`).
Today it auto-closes by **stretching to `nowAtJobRun`** — whatever
wall-clock time the script happens to execute at — which fabricates
hours if the job runs late (a 22:00 run would credit someone through
22:00 for a day they may have actually left hours earlier).

**Real-world evidence, already logged above**: this exact bug is what
forced the manual split-workaround on both the 13 Aug close (Ridhi/
Taskiya) and the 14 Aug close (Rai) — each had to be hand-capped at
their real last OMS heartbeat *before* the broad `confirm=true` run,
specifically to keep the broad run's fallback path from stretching
their hours to job-run time. Confirmed recurring, not person-specific:
"the fallback-routing gap isn't specific to any one person, it recurs
for whoever has a bad punch on a given day."

**Agreed fix, scoped 2026-08-15, not yet built**: replace stretch-to-now
with **cap-at-last-activity-bounded-19:30** — the fallback should close
at the user's actual last OMS activity/heartbeat for that day (the same
value that's currently being computed manually for the workaround),
hard-bounded at a ceiling of ~19:30 using the operational rule that
people wrap up by 7:30pm. This removes both failure directions at once:
no more fabricated hours from a late job run, and no incorrect
under-crediting either, since last-activity is normally well inside the
19:30 ceiling anyway — the ceiling only matters as a backstop against a
heartbeat that's itself anomalously late or missing.

**Status**: label established, root cause and fix both fully scoped,
nothing built yet. Not part of C/D — this lives in the EOD-close/
biometric-sync fallback path, independent of the diff-engine/backstop
migration. Candidate for its own small, isolated fix-and-fixture cycle
(same pattern as the Angana cause-fix and Glitch 3), separate from the
C/D migration unit — not blocking C/D, and C/D doesn't block it either.

## Backgrounded-tab heartbeat survival gap — diagnosed 2026-08-20 (system-side, not a Suravi behavior issue)

**Reclassified from Suravi's under-logging pattern.** What was originally
logged as a device/habit pattern specific to Suravi (her 1/13/14 Aug
gaps, the "ask her what's different about her setup" item above) has a
confirmed root cause: her OMS tab is backgrounded over lunch, and the
session heartbeat dies during that interval — invisible to her, since
she's in another app. This is a system robustness question, not
discipline or device-specific behavior on her part.

**Read-only code trace, 2026-08-20** (no changes made — investigation
only, deliberately sequenced after the C/D soak completes so a code
change doesn't muddy the soak's clean signal):

1. **Heartbeat architecture**: a genuine dedicated `Worker`
   (`initReliableTickWorker()`, index.html:1936) ticks `heartbeatSession()`
   every 60s, correctly avoiding the main-thread `setInterval` throttling
   Chrome applies to backgrounded tabs. But the only verification on
   record is **"Fix 1's 30-min hard-background test"** (V14.3.21/28/31,
   July) — Suravi's actual gaps (142-190min) are 5-6x longer than that
   tested window. Beyond ~30min, whether the worker itself survives, or
   whether OS-level tab-discarding/machine-sleep kills it regardless of
   architecture, is unverified either way.
2. **Stage E does not cover this.** Stage E (2026-08-15) fixed the
   version-gate reload countdown getting stuck on a stalled main-thread
   interval — a different bug riding the same shared worker
   infrastructure, not the heartbeat/staleness path. The two-stage
   watchdog (2min/5min, desktop notification) that DOES exist is built
   to alert a person who's backgrounded-but-present (e.g. working in
   Revit/AutoCAD) — it does nothing for someone genuinely away at lunch.
3. **Fix options, not yet built, ranked**: (a) re-run the hard-background
   test at lunch length (60-190min) FIRST to confirm whether the worker
   itself is the limit or whether this is OS/machine-sleep specific,
   before building anything; (b) if tab-discarding specifically, a
   Service Worker (Background Sync) has stronger persistence guarantees
   worth investigating, bigger change; (c) if genuine machine sleep, no
   client-side fix can prevent the gap itself — the improvement there is
   a better recovery UX (prompt the person to backfill the dead window
   on return, instead of a passive toast). **Explicitly NOT recommended**:
   loosening `STALE_HEARTBEAT_MS` (5min) to be "more forgiving" of long
   background gaps — that threshold is load-bearing for the whole
   clobber-prevention architecture; loosening it slows detection of
   genuinely-abandoned sessions everywhere, the wrong direction.
4. **Cross-check against other recent events — does not fully
   generalize (yet)**: Ridhi's Tuesday event (18:20→19:22 IST) is
   evening, not lunch — doesn't match this signature. Tasmin's Wednesday
   event is already confirmed as a one-off PC hardware failure,
   unrelated. **The recovery mechanism (Bug B) is general and correctly
   caught all three; the specific trigger (lunch-backgrounding) is so
   far only evidenced in Suravi's data**, not corroborated elsewhere.
   Could still be a general code limitation everyone's exposed to
   (nobody else may have happened to background their tab that long),
   but the data doesn't currently prove it's universal.

**Deeper trace, 2026-08-21 (post-soak-closeout)**: revisited with two
new questions — does the data split into one cause or two, and can
tab-discard be distinguished from machine sleep without a live test.

- **Two distinct causes, not one pattern, confirmed by checking recovery
  tags on the doc following each gap**: **Group A** (1/13/14 Aug) shows
  `recovered:true`/"auto-closed on load" — a session genuinely started,
  died, and was later found orphaned by `checkForOrphanedSessionOnLoad()`.
  **Group B** (18 Aug 45min, 20 Aug both Suravi's and Souvik's) shows NO
  recovery tag at all — the doc after the gap is a plain fresh entry,
  meaning no timer was ever running during that window. Group B isn't a
  heartbeat-survival question at all; it's "no timer was started,"
  closer to the original habit-pattern hypothesis than the backgrounding
  theory. Only Group A (3 of 6 total gaps found) bears on the
  backgrounded-tab theory.
- **Tab-discard vs. machine sleep, reasoned from existing code branching
  (no new instrumentation existed to check this directly)**: pure sleep
  preserves the page's JS state exactly — no reload occurs, so
  `timerRunning` stays true and `checkForOrphanedSessionOnLoad()` (gated
  on `!timerRunning`) never fires; `reArmHeartbeat()` handles it silently
  instead, no `recovered` tag produced. Group A's actual "auto-closed on
  load" signature only fires on a genuine fresh page load — meaning
  Group A was NOT simple sleep-and-resume. Confirmed via grep that the
  code has zero Page Lifecycle instrumentation (`document.wasDiscarded`,
  `freeze`/`resume` events) and zero device/browser info recorded on any
  session doc (`navigator.userAgent` unused anywhere) — so this
  reasoning is the only signal available for past occurrences; nothing
  distinguishes tab-discard from an actual browser/computer restart
  without asking directly.

**Confirmed by Ranit, 2026-08-21: Suravi does NOT close her laptop lid
at lunch.** This rules out the remaining alternative explanation for
Group A. Combined with the code-trace finding above (not simple sleep,
since that path produces no `recovered` tag) and now her own confirmation
(not lid-close), **the most likely Group A cause is the browser silently
discarding her long-backgrounded tab under memory pressure** — laptop
stays open the whole time, nothing visibly wrong to her, intermittent
(consistent with discard being memory-pressure-heuristic-driven, not a
fixed timer), fresh-load-on-return signature matches exactly.

**This is not user action and not preventable at the source** — a web
page has no reliable API to opt out of browser tab-discarding.
**Confirms the fix direction already reasoned out: recovery/nudge, not
worker-hardening.** No version of a more robust heartbeat worker
survives its own page being discarded and reloaded.

**Status**: root cause narrowed to browser tab-discard (Group A) +
forgot-to-start (Group B), both pointing away from "harden the worker"
and toward better recovery/nudge UX plus retroactive-diagnosability
instrumentation. Page Lifecycle instrumentation now being built
(read-only telemetry only) — see next entry. Build only, not merged —
deliberately not deployed until Ranit is back to watch it land.

## Page Lifecycle telemetry — BUILT, tested, held on a branch (2026-08-21)

Built on `feat-page-lifecycle-telemetry`, off `main` at `705665e` (the
live C+D production commit). Commit `1e1abed`. Adds the direct signal
the deeper trace above found missing: `document.wasDiscarded` (checked
once at load, tagged onto whichever session
`checkForOrphanedSessionOnLoad()` recovers or `restoreTimerState()`
resumes that cycle) and `freeze`/`resume` Page Lifecycle events (tagged
onto the currently-active session, if any). Writes
`lifecycleEvent`/`lifecycleEventAt` onto the relevant `timeLogs` doc via
a new, isolated `recordLifecycleEvent()` — a small `.update()` call,
never read back anywhere in this file, never gating any existing
behavior.

**Isolation, confirmed**: `index.html` diff is **64 insertions, 0
deletions** — every line is a pure addition, nothing removed or
modified anywhere in production code. Grepped the diff for
`diffMode`/`syncChangedDocs`/`verifyAndFilterDeletes`/`backstopFullSave`/
`saveData`/`commitInChunks`/`timerRunning =`/`startTimerWithPlan`/
`closeCurrentSessionNow` — zero non-comment matches. Both hook call
sites are added strictly AFTER each function's existing logic (the
toast/banner block in `checkForOrphanedSessionOnLoad()`, the
`heartbeatSession()` flush in `restoreTimerState()`), not interleaved
with it. `diffMode` still `'primary'`, `APP_VERSION` still
`2026-08-16.2`, `vercel.json` byte-identical against `main` — all
confirmed via diff, none touched.

**Fail-safe, confirmed**: `recordLifecycleEvent()`'s own write is
wrapped in try/catch, console-warns only on failure, never throws
outward — proven by fixture (a simulated write failure doesn't
propagate or affect anything else). The two new hook call sites are
`if (pageWasDiscarded) ...` appended after each function's real work is
already done, so even a total failure of the new telemetry write cannot
change what `checkForOrphanedSessionOnLoad()`/`restoreTimerState()`
already did.

**Fixtures — `test/stage-lifecycle-telemetry.test.js`, 21 checks**,
proving both directions: lifecycle events record correctly (freeze →
`'frozen'`, resume → `'resumed'`, discard-on-load → `'discarded'` on the
recovered/resumed doc, no-op when no session is active), AND existing
behavior is provably unchanged (`wasDiscarded=false` vs `=true` produce
byte-identical `recovered`/`durationMins`/`endTime` fields on the
underlying orphan-recovery write — the lifecycle tag only ever lands as
a genuinely separate write). One real flaky-timing bug caught and fixed
during development: two of the fixture's own timestamps used live
`Date.now()`, which — via `extractLastSeenMs()`'s whole-second flooring
— could occasionally shave `durationMins` from 60 to 59 depending on
sub-millisecond test-run timing, unrelated to the actual production
code. Fixed with deterministic fixed timestamps; confirmed no
timing-sensitive `Date.now()` usage remains anywhere assertions depend
on (the two remaining `Date.now()` calls, in the `restoreTimerState()`
resume cases, feed only boolean/identity checks, not numeric
comparisons, so they carry no flakiness risk).

**Regression suite: 185 passed, 0 failed across 10 files.** One real
regression caught and fixed along the way:
`test/bugb-orphan-autoclose.test.js` (predates this change entirely)
extracts `checkForOrphanedSessionOnLoad()` into its own isolated
sandbox that didn't know about the new `pageWasDiscarded` reference —
same test-infrastructure-staleness pattern as every prior stage in this
engagement (Stage 0's test needing `verifyAndFilterDeletes` added is
the closest precedent), not a production bug. Fixed by seeding
`pageWasDiscarded: false` + a no-op `recordLifecycleEvent` stub in that
test's sandbox.

**Status: held, not merged, not deployed** through the weekend, exactly
as planned — confirmed via a dedicated post-weekend check 2026-08-24
(local/origin `main` both at `705665e`, branch confirmed not an
ancestor of `main`, live production confirmed still serving
`2026-08-16.2`/`diffMode:'primary'` with zero trace of
`recordLifecycleEvent` in the served HTML).

## Overnight admin-tab redundant write on projects/p1, p2 — investigated, closed for now, 2026-08-24

**Event**: `projects/p1` (Villa Interior – Phase 1) and `projects/p2`
(Office Fit-Out) — 2 of the 15 fixed control docs tracked since the C/D
soak's Day 1 — got a write overnight, both stamped identically
(`updatedAt: 2026-08-24T05:18:01.809Z`, `updatedBy: "Admin User"`).
First control-doc movement in 10 days of tracking. Coincided with
Ranit's admin OMS tab being left open overnight.

**Confirmed via PITR time-travel read** (Cloud Shell, `readTime` just
before the write, diffed against current): **content byte-for-byte
identical** — name, client, dates, every `phases` value unchanged. Only
`updatedAt`/`updatedBy` moved. **No data loss, no real content change.**

**Cause investigated, NOT confirmed — logged as such, not guessed.**
Ranit's own hypothesis (the raw PITR output showed `phases` map keys in
different order between before/current, so maybe `computeDocHash` is
key-order-sensitive) was tested directly against the real extracted
`stableStringify`/`computeDocHash` (not a reimplementation) with p1's
exact content in two different `phases` key orders: **hashes matched
identically both times (`611cc2c0`).** `stableStringify` recurses into
nested objects and re-sorts keys at every level, so it's genuinely
order-insensitive at any depth — **ruled out, not the cause.** The
actual trigger remains unconfirmed. Plausible candidates, explicitly
labeled as unverified: the admin project-edit UI mutating the in-memory
`projectsData` object for p1/p2 without a completed edit, round-tripping
to identical values; or a snapshot-bookkeeping edge case not yet
isolated. **Not escalating further on a one-off** — see disposition.

**Impact — confirmed the safe direction**: this is a **false-positive**
diff (hash said "changed," content wasn't) → an unnecessary but
harmless write. The dangerous direction would be a **false-negative**
(hash says "unchanged" when content genuinely changed → a real edit
silently never saved) — that's exactly what the mandatory daily
ground-truth spot-check (logged during the C-spec review) exists to
catch, and this event is not an instance of it. No evidence anywhere in
this investigation of the dangerous direction ever occurring.

**Disposition: low-priority, watch-if-it-recurs.** Not building
anything for a benign, PITR-confirmed-harmless one-off. Escalate to
Stage F-style diff telemetry only if this recurs — especially if it
touches `timeLogs` (the high-volume collection) or happens without an
admin tab open overnight (which would rule out the current leading,
still-unconfirmed hypothesis).

**Operational note, for the eventual runbook**: don't leave the admin
OMS tab open overnight — under `diffMode='primary'`, it's a live write
participant (via `backstopFullSave()`'s 5-minute diff-based cycle), not
a passive viewer. This event is the concrete example of why.

## Glitch 5 — miss-punch fallback BUILT on branch, held for review, 2026-08-25

Final spec (finalizes the scoping at line 1906, refined twice same day:
first by the Taskiya stalled-session live case — see the "My admin
laptop was left open overnight" thread — then by a deliberate
error-direction decision on the ceiling, below). Built on
`feat-glitch5-miss-punch-fallback` (off `main` @ `c792ed9`), **not
merged** — held for Ranit's review before deploy, per explicit
instruction.

**Change, scoped to `api/biometric-sync.js`'s `closeAtGenuineFinal()`,
no-biometric-OUT branch only** (the has-biometric-OUT branch, all
client-side/save/delete/`diffMode`/heartbeat-client code untouched —
confirmed via full `git diff main` review, `vercel.json` byte-identical):

- **PRIMARY anchor**: `previousDoc.data.endTime` (`combined[i-1]`,
  passed in from the caller) — used unconditionally when it resolves
  and is genuinely after the flagged doc's own start.
- **FALLBACK anchor (the Taskiya refinement)**: when no previous doc
  exists, or its end is at/before the flagged doc's own start (meaning
  it says nothing about *this* session) — falls through to the flagged
  doc's own last heartbeat, via a new `extractLastSeenMs()` (verbatim
  port of `index.html`'s function of the same name; did not exist in
  this file before).
- **CEILING, settled at `20:30`** after two earlier drafts: `19:30`
  (read-only scoping phase, line 1906 — too tight, would truncate
  genuine late work) → `22:00` (first correction, same day — avoided
  truncation but risked silently OVER-crediting a real 9-10pm worker,
  with no signal anyone would notice) → **`20:30`, the final call,
  made on a deliberate error-direction principle**: for hours feeding
  budgets, an error that's *visible and self-correcting* beats one
  that's silent. Capping at 20:30 means a rare genuine late-worker sees
  LESS than they worked — people notice being shorted and say so.
  Under-credit is self-reporting; over-credit isn't. Its own guard
  (`ceiling <= start` → manual review) is unchanged throughout.
- **Flag-on-clamp (new)**: whenever the real anchor is later than
  20:30, the session still closes (at 20:30) — but is now also pushed
  to `needsManualReview` with the real anchor time and reason, so the
  rare "actually worked past 8:30" case gets proactively surfaced for
  review instead of relying on the person to notice and complain. The
  doc itself carries a new `ceilingClamped:true` field when this fires.
  Applies uniformly whether the pre-clamp anchor came from the
  previous-doc or own-heartbeat path, and regardless of whether the
  real value looks like plausible late work or an implausible stray —
  the system doesn't try to tell those apart itself; that judgment is
  left to whoever reviews the flag.
- **FAIL-SAFE**: neither anchor resolves → `needsManualReview.push(...)`,
  same discipline as `resolveStartMsStrict()` elsewhere in this file —
  never fabricates a close time.
- `nowAtJobRun` (the old fallback's shared close-point) removed
  entirely — its only two usages were both replaced.

**Fixture results — `test/glitch5-miss-punch-fallback.test.js`, run
directly against the real extracted (not reimplemented) function
source: 36/36 passed.** Covers: (a) PRIMARY anchor from a genuine
previous-doc end; (b) the Taskiya case (own-heartbeat fallback,
producing a small positive duration, never negative); (h) last activity
before the 20:30 ceiling (19:45) → caps there, no flag, no
`ceilingClamped`; (c) last activity past the ceiling (21:00, plausible
late work) → capped at 20:30 **and** flagged, doc carries
`ceilingClamped:true`, flag names the real anchor and the
under-crediting risk; (c2) the same clamp+flag path via the
own-heartbeat anchor with a clearly implausible stray value (23:30) —
proving both anchor paths and both "plausible" and "stray" values are
treated identically; (d) ceiling-at-or-before-start → manual review, no
write; (e) no anchor at all → manual review, no fabrication; (f) a
recovered previous doc's endTime used as-is, no special-casing; (g) the
never-reaches-this-branch guard for a genuinely-live session confirmed
architecturally (by construction — `combined`/`next` wiring unchanged
by this diff), not re-tested. Plus a write-failure case: non-fatal,
reported via `needsManualReview`, matching every other branch's
pattern.

**Regression check**: full existing suite (all 10 pre-existing
`test/*.test.js` files, 185 checks) plus this file's own 36, re-run
clean, 0 failures across all 11 files (221 checks total) — this change
introduces no regressions elsewhere.

**Status: held on the branch, unmerged, awaiting Ranit's review of the
diff + fixtures before deploy.**

## Glitch 5 — MERGED and DEPLOYED, 2026-08-25

Reviewed and approved (20:30 ceiling + flag-on-clamp confirmed matching
the decision above, fixtures/isolation/regressions all accepted).
Merged `feat-glitch5-miss-punch-fallback` → `main` (`--no-ff`, commit
`6b71790`, on top of `c792ed9`), pushed, Vercel auto-deployed.

**Deliberately did NOT bump `APP_VERSION`/`version.json` for this
deploy** — caught and confirmed with Ranit before merging, not just
assumed. `APP_VERSION` is a client-side constant baked into
`index.html`; `checkForNewVersion()` (index.html:2171) force-reloads,
then hard-locks, any open tab whose loaded `APP_VERSION` doesn't match
`/api/version`. That mechanism exists to catch stale CLIENT JS after an
`index.html` deploy. This change touches only `api/biometric-sync.js` —
a serverless function no browser tab ever loads — so there is no client
staleness to reconcile. Bumping `version.json` without also bumping
`index.html`'s `APP_VERSION` (which would have broken the "index.html
byte-identical" requirement) would have force-reloaded every open admin
tab on a false mismatch, then hit the hard version-gate lockout
permanently, for a change with zero actual client impact. Confirmed
with Ranit via explicit question before proceeding; skipped the bump.

**Post-deploy verification, all confirmed**:
- HTTP 200, zero downtime across the deploy (polled every 5s through
  the rollout).
- `vercel.json`: empty diff vs. pre-merge `main`, confirmed via `git
  diff` (repo-level check, not HTTP-observable).
- `diffMode` still `'primary'` in the live served `index.html` — C/D
  backstop intact.
- Live `index.html`: fetched pre-push and post-deploy, diffed
  byte-for-byte (CRLF-normalized) — **identical**, confirming zero
  client-bundle impact as required.
- `/api/version`: unchanged at `2026-08-24.1`, as expected given the
  deliberately-skipped bump.
- `api/biometric-sync.js` itself: hit read-only (no `confirm=true`,
  matching the file's own dry-run default) — returned a clean typed
  `401 {"status":"unauthorized"}` (the file's own `CRON_SECRET`
  bearer-auth gate, `api/biometric-sync.js:736-741`), not a crash/500 —
  confirms the new code loaded and ran without error. Did not attempt
  an authenticated call: `CRON_SECRET` lives only in Vercel env vars
  (see [[reference_cron_secret_location]]), not fetched or guessed.

**Glitch 5 is live.** The daily cron (`vercel.json`, 16:30 UTC / 22:00
IST) still runs dry-run only — `confirm=true` is not baked into the
cron path (unchanged by this deploy, per the file's own header). A
manual `confirm=true` run is required to actually close a flagged
session.

**Watch item — the real-world validation**: the next genuine miss-punch
(biometric IN, no OUT, no biometric data all day) is this feature's
first live test. Confirm it behaves as designed, not as the old
behavior: closes at the user's real last previous-session-end or own
last-heartbeat (NOT stretched to whenever the job happened to run), and
if that real anchor is past 20:30, confirm it (a) still closes at 20:30

the doc — don't let a clamped-and-flagged case go unreviewed the first
time it fires for real.

## Taskiya's Aug 24 duplicate self-report — systemic Glitch 3 race, found and fixed 2026-08-26

**The watch item above found something on its very first morning** —
not the miss-punch validation itself (that one self-healed cleanly, see
below), but a second, recurring bug surfaced while checking on it.

**Taskiya's stalled 24 Aug session** (`mt7ccb47bmjujh`, started 20:08,
died 11 seconds later, flagged for reconciliation) sat untouched
overnight simply because she hadn't reopened the app — not a Bug B
failure. The moment she did, at **11:10:04 IST on 2026-08-25**, Bug B's
own orphan-autoclose correctly capped it at its own last heartbeat
(20:08), matching exactly what Glitch 5's fallback path would have
chosen had it run instead (previous session ended 20:00, this one
started 20:08 — after — so the own-heartbeat fallback applies,
uncapped, well under the 20:30 ceiling). **Confirms Glitch 5's logic
is sound**, even though it never got the chance to actually run here.

**But 29 seconds later (11:10:33 IST), a duplicate landed**:
`mt88kmkdjvssv6`, `desc: "Self-reported (timer left running — 9h 15m
logged by user)"`, `durationMins: 555`, no `startTime`/`endTime` at
all — inflating Taskiya's Aug 24 total from the correct 554 min to a
false 1109 min (18h29m, not a plausible single day).

**Root cause, traced through the actual code, not inferred**: this is
the SAME class of bug as the original Glitch 3 fix
(`fix-orphan-recovery-duplicate-guard`, 2026-08-15), but a hole that
fix's own fixtures never modeled. `restoreTimerState()`'s guard
(`index.html:8109-8120`) does exactly one point-in-time
positive-confirmation check before showing the self-report modal. The
init sequence at `index.html:3729/3734` and `3827/3831` runs
`await restoreTimerState(); await checkForOrphanedSessionOnLoad();` —
strictly sequential. Bug B's own auto-close (`checkForOrphanedSessionOnLoad()`)
never starts until `restoreTimerState()` has already finished its
check and shown the modal (`showOrphanRecoveryModal()` isn't awaited,
so `restoreTimerState()` returns immediately after opening it). Right
after any crash, the affected doc is *always* still `inProgress:true`
server-side — nothing else has touched it yet — so the guard's
"already resolved" check can **never** fire for the exact session that
caused the crash. It's structurally guaranteed to miss, not a rare
timing fluke: Bug B closed the doc at 11:10:04, while Taskiya was
still looking at (and about to submit) the modal that the guard had
already, correctly-per-its-own-logic, decided to show moments earlier.
Confirmed the coverage gap directly: all 10 fixtures in
`test/fix-orphan-recovery-duplicate-guard.test.js` mock a static
server-doc state fixed at call time; none model a second, concurrent
writer changing that doc between the modal showing and being submitted.

**Fix — DELETED, verified, 2026-08-26.** Confirmed via a fresh
`source:'server'` freeze-check immediately before acting (both docs
unchanged since discovery) that `mt6sa4oqcas508` (553min, clean
`startTime`/`endTime`, heartbeat-computed) is the real session and
`mt88kmkdjvssv6` is the spurious one (no start/end, human-typed
estimate landing within 2 minutes of the already-correct 553min
figure — consistent with Taskiya estimating her whole day from memory,
unaware the system had already captured it). Deleted
`mt88kmkdjvssv6` via `deleteDoc()`, then verified: doc confirmed gone
(`source:server`); `mt6sa4oqcas508` byte-for-byte identical
pre/post-delete (every field diffed, not spot-checked); Taskiya's Aug
24 total recomputed from server = **554 min**, correct. Global
`reconciliationNeeded:true` queue checked: 1 unrelated item (Souvik,
2026-08-25, today's date — not investigated, flagged separately, not
part of this cleanup).

**Status: the systemic fix (closing the race itself, not just this one
duplicate) is not yet built** — scoped next, so this class of bug stops
recurring on every future crash-then-reopen. See below.

## Glitch 3 race fix — BUILT on branch, held for review, 2026-08-26

**Two candidate fixes scoped, compared, one rejected before building
anything.**

**(b) Reorder `checkForOrphanedSessionOnLoad()` before
`restoreTimerState()` — rejected.** Both of Bug B's own defensive
guards (`if (timerRunning) return;` and `if (doc.id ===
currentSessionLogId) return;`) depend entirely on `restoreTimerState()`
having already run and set those values for a legitimately-resumed
session (confirmed via the explicit comment at `index.html:3731-3733`:
"after restore has had its chance..."). `STALE_HEARTBEAT_MS` is 5
minutes; `MAX_SESSION_HOURS` (the resume-vs-prompt threshold) is 12
hours — meaning **any ordinary reopen after more than 5 minutes away
(a lunch break, a laptop sleeping) has a heartbeat Bug B considers
stale, while still qualifying for `restoreTimerState()`'s normal-resume
path.** Reordering would let Bug B auto-close that legitimate session
before `restoreTimerState()` ever protects it — a regression far more
common (every day, every break) than the bug being fixed (one crash).

**(a+) Re-check at submit-time, reading the LOCAL `timeLogs` array —
approved and built.** Ranit's refinement of the original (a) proposal
(server round-trip): `tryAutoCloseOrphan()` already mirrors its own
Firestore write into the in-memory `timeLogs` array synchronously right
after the write succeeds (`index.html:8014-8015`), so a local re-check
sees Bug B's resolution with no network round-trip and no new async gap
of its own — same "re-verify at the moment of mutation" principle as
the Stage B delete guard. Confirmed the local-mirror premise directly
in the code before building on it, not assumed.

**Built**: `orphanSaveBtn`'s click handler now re-reads
`timeLogs.find(l => l.id === state.sessionLogId)` immediately before
writing, deliberately INSIDE the handler (not a value captured when
the modal opened — the stale-closure trap Ranit flagged). Only
suppresses on positive confirmation (doc found locally AND
`inProgress` strictly `false`); doc not found, no `sessionLogId`, or
`inProgress` anything other than exactly `false` all fall through to
the existing write, unchanged — same fail-closed principle as the
original 2026-08-15 guard. Added `recordDuplicateDiscarded()`
telemetry (additive `.update()` on the session doc, mirrors
`recordLifecycleEvent()`'s exact discipline — never read back, never
gates behavior, failure-tolerant) so this race's fix is observable
going forward instead of just assumed.

**Fixture results — `test/fix-glitch3-race-guard.test.js`: 22/22
passed.** The critical one, matching Ranit's explicit requirement:
models the actual race with TWO-STEP timing — a local `timeLogs`
array entry starts `inProgress:true` (matching modal-open reality),
then gets mutated to `inProgress:false` mid-test (simulating Bug B's
own synchronous write, the same code shape as the real
`timeLogs[idx] = {...}` line) — BEFORE the save handler runs. This is
the exact scenario the original 10 static-doc fixtures couldn't model,
which is why the bug shipped uncaught the first time. Confirms: no
duplicate written, telemetry recorded once on the right doc,
`localStorage` cleared, user informed. Paired with a still-genuinely-open
case (writes normally, completely unaffected) and four fail-closed edge
cases (doc not found locally, no `sessionLogId`, `inProgress` merely
`undefined` rather than strictly `false`, and the pre-existing `mins
<= 0` validation still firing first) — all matching the original
guard's established fail-closed shape.

**Regression check**: full existing suite (all 11 pre-existing
`test/*.test.js` files) plus this file's own 22, re-run clean, 0
failures across all 12 files (253 checks total).

**Isolation confirmed**: `git diff main` touches only `index.html` (57
lines added, 0 removed) — the new telemetry helper next to
`recordLifecycleEvent()`, and the new guard inside `orphanSaveBtn`'s
handler. `api/` and `vercel.json`: empty diff. `restoreTimerState()`,
`checkForOrphanedSessionOnLoad()`, `tryAutoCloseOrphan()`, the init
order, Glitch 5, and every save/delete/diffMode path — completely
untouched.

**Status: held on `feat-glitch3-race-fix`, unmerged, awaiting Ranit's
review — especially the race-modeling fixture, which is what proves
this is actually fixed rather than just plausible.**

## Glitch 3 race fix — MERGED and DEPLOYED, 2026-08-26

Reviewed and approved (race-modeling fixture, isolation, regressions all
accepted). Merged `feat-glitch3-race-fix` → `main` (`--no-ff`, commit
`2864fe9`, on top of `6b71790`).

**Unlike Glitch 5, this deploy correctly DOES bump `APP_VERSION`** —
this is a genuine client-side (`index.html`) change, so the
version-gate reload is doing exactly its intended job: any tab still
running the old orphan-recovery code (with the race still live in
memory) gets refreshed onto the fix. Bumped `version.json` and
`index.html`'s `APP_VERSION` together, as a separate commit (`7a6a2ae`)
on `main` after the merge, matching the established convention from
every prior client-changing deploy. Pushed, Vercel auto-deployed.

**Post-deploy verification, all confirmed**:
- HTTP 200, zero downtime across the deploy (polled every 5s through
  the rollout).
- `/api/version` → `{"version":"2026-08-26.1"}`, matching the bump.
- Live `index.html`'s `APP_VERSION` constant → `2026-08-26.1`, matching
  `/api/version` (so the version-gate has nothing to flag on the very
  next tab that loads it).
- `diffMode` still `'primary'` in the live served `index.html` — C/D
  backstop intact.
- `vercel.json`: empty diff vs. pre-merge `main`, confirmed via `git
  diff` (repo-level check).
- `recordDuplicateDiscarded` and the submit-time guard's own log
  message both confirmed present in the live-fetched `index.html` —
  the actual fix is what's being served, not just what's committed.

**Glitch 3's systemic hole is now closed.** The next crash-then-reopen
where Bug B and the orphan self-report modal target the same session
will no longer duplicate — and `orphanDuplicateDiscarded`/
`orphanDuplicateDiscardedAt` on the affected `timeLogs` doc will show
it happening (or not), turning "we think it's fixed" into something
actually observable.

## Rai's stuck reconciliationNeeded flag — traced, cleared, systemic gap found and fixed, 2026-08-28

**Checking the queue on 28 Aug found nothing newly stalled that day** —
two carryover flags from the 26th (Rai) and 27th (Suravi), neither a
Glitch 5 case. Suravi's (`mtbal3y7aklytk`, Lunch Break, 27 Aug) turned
out to be Bug C's de-overlap path (`deoverlapped:1` in the dry-run) —
she has a real 15:30 NIAVARA CLUB session starting after her stalled
lunch-break's own last heartbeat, so `closeAtGenuineFinal()` (Glitch 5)
is structurally never reached for it (only invoked when the flagged doc
is the LAST item that day) — a clean demonstration of that guarantee,
still not a validation case. Left open, unresolved, three real-world
candidates in now (Souvik, Neha, Suravi), zero Glitch 5 hits.

**Rai's (`mt9mpvqci1g3dm`, Kredent office, 26 Aug) was the more
interesting one — already `inProgress:false` with a real `endTime`
("19:04") and correct `durationMins` (510), but `reconciliationNeeded`
stuck at `true`.** Traced to a genuine, confirmed gap, not guessed at:

**Root cause**: the 2026-08-15.4 "Angana cause-fix" added
`reconciliationNeeded:false` to `closeCurrentSessionNow()` (the shared
close function behind task-switch and logout) — but three *other*
client-side write sites that also perform a genuine close were never
updated the same way:
- `timerCompleteBtn`'s handler ("Complete Task", `index.html:7562`) —
  confirmed as the actual path that produced Rai's doc, via an exact
  fingerprint match: its `autoDesc` has no suffix in parens in the
  uncapped case, and Rai's `desc` reads bare `"Final Design – Detailed
  Drawings"` — every other close path (task-switch, logout, Bug B,
  8pm auto-complete, server EOD-close) always stamps a suffix.
- `runEndOfDayCheck()`'s 8pm force auto-complete branch
  (`index.html:~8420-8429`).
- `finalizeCurrentSession()`'s capped (12h-ceiling) branch
  (`index.html:~7268`) — a much rarer trigger.

**Corrected a specific hypothesis along the way**: it's not about
*when* a session was flagged relative to closing —
`closeCurrentSessionNow()`'s own comment already explicitly covers
"flagged mid-session, then resumed and closed normally." The actual
variable is *which close mechanism fired* — three of them never got
the field at all, regardless of timing.

**Clearing scope was checked, not assumed**: re-read the full
`reconciliationNeeded:true` queue — Rai's was the only doc showing
this "closed but still flagged" shape right now. Not evidence the gap
is rare, just that this specific coincidence (flagged mid-day, then
closed via Complete Task/8pm-auto-complete rather than switch/logout)
hadn't landed on anyone else recently.

**Rai's flag cleared, 2026-08-28**: freeze-checked immediately before
(baseline matched exactly what was traced), targeted single-field
`updateDoc({reconciliationNeeded: false})` — same shape as the
pre-existing "wake-verify" mechanism (`index.html:2059-2060`), no other
field touched. Verified `source:'server'`: `reconciliationNeeded` now
`false`, every other field (`inProgress`, `endTime`, `durationMins`,
`desc`, all of it) byte-for-byte identical pre/post. Global queue
dropped from 2 to 1 (just Suravi's de-overlap case) as expected.

**Systemic fix — BUILT on branch, held for review.** Branch
`fix-reconciliation-flag-clear-gaps` (off `main` @ `7a6a2ae`). Adds
`reconciliationNeeded: false` at the three sites, each mirroring
`closeCurrentSessionNow()`'s existing fix exactly — no new logic.
`finalizeCurrentSession()`'s addition is deliberately scoped to the
**capped branch only** (`...(capped?{autoCapped:true,
reconciliationNeeded:false}:{})`), since that same write also fires on
every ordinary live heartbeat checkpoint (uncapped) — clearing the
flag unconditionally there would be wrong, not just unnecessary.

**Fixtures — `test/fix-reconciliation-flag-clear-gaps.test.js`: 27/27
passed.** Covers all three sites closing a session and clearing the
flag (the Rai shape); a regression proving `finalizeCurrentSession()`'s
uncapped branch does NOT write `reconciliationNeeded` at all (correctly
scoped to capped-only); a regression proving a short, never-flagged
Complete Task close is otherwise completely unaffected (the write
doesn't branch on prior flag state — it unconditionally clears on any
genuine close, same principle `closeCurrentSessionNow()` already used);
and a direct source-text check that `closeCurrentSessionNow()` itself
is genuinely unchanged, not just "still contains the string somewhere."

**Regression check**: full existing suite (12 pre-existing files) plus
this file's own 27, clean across all 13 files (285 checks total).

**Isolation confirmed**: `git diff main` touches only `index.html` (27
insertions, 3 modified lines at the three sites) — `api/`, `vercel.json`
untouched. No save/delete/diffMode logic changed anywhere.

**Deploy note**: this is a client change (`index.html`), so unlike
Glitch 5 it needs the version bump on deploy — same reasoning as the
Glitch 3 fix.

**Status: held on the branch, unmerged, awaiting review.**

## Studio Health — Layer A (Project & Estimation Health) BUILT on branch, held for review, 2026-08-28

Full design blueprint scoped read-only across three sessions (submissions
data model + safety, summation-site risk, insight-not-verdict UI framing,
"don't box in B/C" mechanics) before any code — this entry covers the
Layer A build itself, approved to go first per that blueprint.

**Built on `feat-studio-health-layer-a`** (off `main` @ `5d880e0`). New
admin-only "Studio Health" nav item + page, reusing the exact
`admin-only` class/toggle pattern already trusted for Appraisal/Manage
— zero new visibility infrastructure. Page is a **tabbed shell from day
one**: "Project Health" (Layer A, populated), "Submission Log" and
"Individual Insight" present as empty placeholders — so B and C slot in
later without restructuring a shipped page.

**Layer A, confirmed pure-read**: two new pure aggregation functions —
`sumHoursByProjectPhase(logs)` and `computeEstimationBias(projects,
byProjectPhase)` — take their data as arguments, touch no globals,
deliberately don't reuse the existing global-array-bound
`phaseLoggedMins()`/`projectLoggedMins()` (index.html:3420-3421) since
those read the cached `timeLogs` array, which this view specifically
avoids. `renderStudioHealth()` fetches `projects`+`timeLogs` **fresh via
`source:'server'`** on every open, not the cached in-memory arrays —
deliberate, per the projects/p1-p2 admin-tab-staleness lesson (an admin
tab can sit open for hours; a cached snapshot here would misreport
project health). Computes: actual-vs-budget per phase, cross-project
estimation bias per phase type (>10%/<70% thresholds — the 10% figure
reused from the existing Appraisal budget-adherence tolerance,
index.html's own precedent, not a new arbitrary number), and live-burn
flags for currently-open phases at ≥90% of budget.

**Insight-not-verdict enforced in the CSS itself, not just described**:
one neutral flag marker (`.sh-flag-mark`, tan/amber) used identically
regardless of severity — deliberately NOT the red/green
`.ht-badge.at-risk`/`.behind` pattern this file already uses elsewhere
for per-person Hours Tracker targets. Flags render as plain-language
sentences (`.sh-flag`), never table cells. The reference table is
grouped by project in whatever order they come back — never sorted by
overrun%, which would read as a ranking regardless of caption.

**Fixtures — `test/feat-studio-health-layer-a.test.js`: 27/27 passed.**
Includes the central safety claim as a direct source-text check: no
`.set(`/`.update(`/`.add(`/`.delete(`/`.batch(` anywhere across all
three functions. Plus: aggregation correctness (non-project entries
correctly excluded, budgets/actuals summed right), fresh
`source:'server'` reads confirmed on both collections, bias/live-burn
flags fire and don't fire correctly, a closed (not live) near-budget
phase correctly produces no live-burn flag, empty-state messaging when
nothing is notable, a fetch failure fails visibly instead of crashing
or hanging, and a non-admin guard that attempts zero reads.

**Regression check**: full existing suite (13 pre-existing files) plus
this file's own 27, clean across all 14 files (312 checks total).

**Isolation confirmed**: `git diff main` touches only `index.html` (222
insertions, 0 deletions) — `api/`, `vercel.json` empty diff. No schema
change, no new collection, never enters
`computeCollectionDiffs()`/the backstop/diffMode system (nothing to
register).

**Visual preview**: ran the real extracted `renderStudioHealth()`
against realistic sample data (not a hand-mockup) via the same
vm-sandbox technique as the fixtures, captured its actual output HTML,
and wrapped it in the real page CSS for Ranit to review directly —
sent as a file rather than a live in-app screenshot (Browser-tool
preview of a local file wasn't cooperating in this environment; the
app also requires login, which a static preview sidesteps cleanly).

**Deploy note**: client change (`index.html`) — will need the version
bump on deploy, same reasoning as the Glitch 3 and reconciliation-flag
fixes.

**Status: held on the branch, unmerged, awaiting review.**

## Studio Health — Individual Insight (Workload + Estimation-vs-actual) BUILT on branch, held for review, 2026-08-28

Scoped read-only first (data sourcing per insight, the multi-person-
phase attribution answer, fairness handling for workload, the
structural anti-scoreboard UI mechanism) — this entry covers the build
itself. **Attendance (#2) explicitly held out of this build entirely**
— confirmed via repo-wide search that biometric IN-time is never
persisted anywhere today (`fetchInOutPunchData()` in
`api/biometric-sync.js` fetches live per-date and discards after use) —
so it needs new server-side plumbing (a small addition to the existing
daily cron, plus a one-time backfill) before an insight view could read
anything real. Decided separately, later.

**Built on `feat-studio-health-layer-a`** (same branch as Layer A,
second commit `d2284ad` — Individual Insight extends the same tabbed
page shell Layer A introduced, so kept together rather than branching
separately). Same pure-read shape: fresh `source:'server'` reads
(`projects`, `timeLogs`, `leaveRequests`, `users`) on tab open, zero
writes, no schema change.

**Workload**: rolling 7/30-day window (not calendar-month — avoids a
partial current period reading unfairly light against full prior ones)
compared to the average of 3 equal-length prior windows — **self-baseline
only**, never a shared target (confirmed no part-time/employment-type
field exists anywhere to build a fair absolute one). Approved leave from
`leaveRequests` annotates the raw view. Requires at least one prior
window with real data before flagging at all.

**Estimation-vs-actual**: a phase is attributed to a person only when
they contributed ≥20% of that phase's logged hours (no drive-by
dilution) — and even then, **never a "share of the overrun" number**,
only their raw unmodified hours next to the phase's real total and
budget. The pattern itself compares their 5 most-recent
meaningfully-contributed phase-instances' over-run rate against their
own historical rate (≥3 historical samples required) — never against
anyone else's.

**The anti-scoreboard mechanism is structural, proven by fixture, not
just described**: flag sentences can name multiple people at once
(sorted alphabetically, never by magnitude), but a fixture confirms they
carry no numbers at all. A second fixture seeds a person with 999h of
real data alongside the selected person and confirms that number is
**absent from the rendered output entirely** — raw numbers render for
at most one selected person, structurally, not by convention.

**Fixtures — `test/feat-studio-health-individual-insight.test.js`:
37/37 passed** (one real bug caught and fixed before shipping: an
off-by-one in `approvedLeaveDaysInWindow` undercounted a leave request's
own last day by treating an inclusive `endDate` as an exclusive window
bound — caught by the fixture, not found later). Covers: source-text
proof of zero writes, self-baseline flagging (above/below/insufficient-
history), leave-day counting, the ≥20% threshold enforced both ways,
raw-hours-not-attribution-math on a shared phase, the recent-vs-
historical rate matching the "4 of 5 vs 1 of 5" shape from scoping,
fresh `source:'server'` reads confirmed, and the two structural
one-person-at-a-time checks above.

**Regression check**: full existing suite (14 pre-existing files) plus
this file's own 37, clean across all 15 files (349 checks total).

**Isolation confirmed**: `git diff main` (both Studio Health commits
combined) touches only `index.html` — `api/`, `vercel.json` empty diff.

**Preview against REAL production data, not synthetic — the Layer A
lesson applied**: ran the real extracted render functions against a
live `source:'server'` read (30 projects, 3524 `timeLogs`, 290
`leaveRequests`, 10 users) and captured three real screens. Two
genuinely useful real findings surfaced immediately:
- **Sarbani**: workload down to 18.4h from her own 137.9h normal —
  correctly annotated with 28 of 30 days approved leave, so it reads as
  explained rather than alarming. Confirms the leave-annotation feature
  does its job on real, not constructed, data.
- **Taskiya**: workload up 4x (167.2h vs. her own 40.4h normal), and the
  drill-down shows why — she's a 51% contributor on **Kredent office's
  Schematic Design phase, which is real data sitting at 288% of its
  budgeted hours.** The estimation *pattern* itself isn't flagged yet
  (only one phase-instance on record, needs 5) — but the raw number
  alone is already a legitimate "worth a conversation" case, which is
  the actual argument for showing raw data underneath even before a
  recency pattern exists.

**One real usefulness issue found, not yet fixed — flagging for a
decision**: "Admin User" appears in the flags list (workload well above
their own normal) alongside real team members. That's noise, not
signal — the admin account isn't a team workload data point. Small fix
if wanted: exclude `isAdmin:true` users from the `peopleWithData` filter
in `renderIndividualInsightBody()`. Held, not applied, pending Ranit's
call.

**Status: held on the branch, unmerged, awaiting review — including the
Admin User exclusion decision above.**

**Retuned, 2026-08-28, after Ranit reviewed the rendered view against

1.3x/0.7x → 2.0x/0.4x — the old band flagged Souvik at 1.09x his own

`significantlyOverBudgetInstances()`, a new PRIMARY signal (meaningful

## Studio Health Individual Insight — FULL RESHAPE (Margin Risk leads), built, 2026-08-28

Scoped read-only first (margin-risk ranking basis, versatility, leave-
near-holidays data sourcing, graphics without becoming a ranking
surface) — this entry covers the build. Commit `b70271c`, same branch.

**The reframe**: estimation moves from person-level "worth a
conversation" to project-level economics. **Margin Risk** (projects/
phases consuming far more hours than budgeted) is now the primary
card, ranked by **absolute hours over budget**, not percentage —
confirmed the right basis (a small phase at 288% can be less real cost
than a large one at 130%) and confirmed ranking *projects* by severity
was never what the anti-scoreboard rule protected against (only
ranking *people* against each other is restricted). The BURDWAN-RD
clustering from the previous pass now surfaces once, at the project
level, with contributors listed underneath as staffing/"why" context —
not three separate person-level flags.

**New signals**: Versatility (distinct active-project count,
self-baseline, shown only as a drill-down stat — deliberately not a
proactive flag, matching its "pattern to interpret" framing). Leave-
near-holidays (adjacency to a weekend/2nd-4th-Saturday/holiday, ported
faithfully from this file's own `is24Sat()` rule) — **drill-down only,
never a flag sentence anywhere**, strictly neutral coverage-planning
language, confirmed by fixture that it never appears until a person is
explicitly selected.

**New structure**: two independent, one-at-a-time drill-down axes —
"Look at One Project" (margin risk) alongside the existing "Look at One
Person." **One deliberate, explicit exception to the no-side-by-side
rule**: the project drill-down's contributor list intentionally shows
multiple people's raw hours together (Ranit's own "who's on this, for
the why conversation" ask) — reasoned through and fixture-tested as a
positive case, not silently allowed to slip through.

**Graphics, hand-built inline SVG (no library)**: a workload trend
sparkline and a leave-heatmap, both single-person only. Margin-risk
burn bars reuse Projects & Budget's existing `.phase-bar-fill`
red/amber/green pattern verbatim — deliberate: at the project level,
color is a quantified economics fact, not a judgment about a person,
so it doesn't conflict with the neutral register kept everywhere else
in this feature.

**A real bug found and fixed while building, not just a new one
introduced**: four date-formatting call sites — including
`getWindowBounds()`, already shipped since V16.11/12 — used
`d.toISOString().slice(0,10)`, which silently rolls the calendar date
back one day for any local time between IST midnight and 05:29
(`toISOString()` converts to UTC first). This exact pitfall was already
identified and avoided in this file's own pre-existing `fmt()` helper;
missed it when writing the new pure functions. Added `toLocalDateStr()`
mirroring `fmt()`'s approach, routed all four sites through it, and
added a regression fixture anchored at an early-IST-morning instant so
it can't silently return.

**Fixtures — fully rewritten,
`test/feat-studio-health-individual-insight.test.js`: 56/56 passed.**
Covers margin-risk ranking by absolute hours (not percentage), the real
`is24Sat()` sandwich-rule cases (2nd/4th Saturday flagged, 1st/3rd/5th
correctly not), leave-near-holiday drill-down-only enforcement, the
two-axis drill-down working independently, the no-side-by-side
extension to both new visuals, the explicit deliberate-exception test
for the project contributor list, and the timezone-bug regression.

**Regression check**: full existing suite clean, 368/368 across 15
files.

**Isolation confirmed**: `index.html` + the one test file only —
`api/`, `vercel.json` empty diff. No schema change, no writes anywhere
in this feature (source-text checked).

**Preview against REAL production data (not synthetic), three
screens** — sent directly as a file: (1) the default flags view —
**BURDWAN RD's Project Management phase is at 2256%, 604h over a 28h
budget**, the standout real case, now correctly surfacing once at the
project level; (2) "Look at One Project" for BURDWAN RD — real burn
bars, and Neha/Ridhi/Rai shown together as that phase's real
contributors; (3) "Look at One Person" for Taskiya — her real
versatility stat (2 vs. her own 1.7 average, unremarkable), her real
12-week workload trend (a genuine climb, visible in the sparkline), and
her leave-heatmap, which found 2 real leave periods adjacent to a
weekend.

**One honest real-data gap worth flagging**: `companyData/squareDB`'s
`holidays` field is currently **empty** in production — the leave-
near-holiday feature is fully wired and already finding real weekend-
adjacency signal (as seen with Taskiya above), but the holiday-specific
half of it won't surface anything until someone actually populates that
list. Structurally ready; needs real data entered to be fully useful.

**Status: held on this branch, unmerged, pushed to `origin`, awaiting
review.**

## Existing Appraisal system — read-only deep-dive, 2026-08-28

Investigated before scoping Employee Review, since the two systems'
goals turned out to be in direct tension. Full detail, all confirmed
against real code and real Firestore data, not assumed:

**What it scores**: 8 weighted components into one composite 0–10 —
Attendance (15–20%), OMS logging discipline (15–20%), Productive hours
vs. 170h/month target (18–20%), Weekly planning discipline (12–15%),
Project budget discipline (8–10%), Leave application behaviour (7–8%),
Work breadth (5–7%), Admin scorecard (0–20%, a single manual 1–10 star
rating). **The weights themselves shift** — entering the admin star
rating reduces every other weight and adds a 20% slice for it, invisible
in the UI. Two components collide directly with lessons already learned
elsewhere in this engagement: *Project budget discipline* scores a
person for their phases running over budget — precisely the BURDWAN
trap Margin Risk was built to avoid. *Work breadth* rewards juggling
more projects as a flat positive, the opposite of Versatility's honest
"could be positive, could be spread thin" framing.

**What employees see**: nothing, and cannot reach it. The nav item
itself is `admin-only` (`index.html:494`); the function has its own
redundant admin gate on top of that. Vestigial "visible from Q2
onwards" staggered-reveal code exists inside the function but is
unreachable — both gates stop a non-admin before it would ever run.

**Has it been used**: queried `appraisalData` directly, `source:server`
— **zero documents, ever**. No admin has entered a single star rating.
No composite score has ever been persisted (the whole computation is
ephemeral, recalculated fresh on every page load from existing
`timeLogs`/`leaveRequests`/`planEntries`/`projectsData` — only the
admin's manual rating would ever be stored, and that's never happened).
**Fully dormant. Never seen by anyone, never fed with real input.**

**Write path**: single `appraisalData` collection, one doc per quarter
(`appraisalScores_{quarterLabel}`), each admin rating stored as a field
keyed `{userId}_{quarterLabel}`, written via `.set({[key]: entry},
{merge:true})`.

**Dependencies**: none. Every reference to `appraisalData`/
`apAdminScores`/`adminScore` lives entirely inside `renderAppraisal()`
itself — no other page, calculation, or export reads from it.

**Assessment, acted on**: a clean, direct replacement is safe — there's
no real data to migrate and no one who's already seen a number that
would change. Decision made: Employee Review replaces this
methodology; retiring `renderAppraisal()`/the nav item/`appraisalData`
is a deliberate separate follow-up, not bundled into the Employee
Review build itself (confirmed untouched — see below).

## Studio Health: Submission Log (Layer B) — BUILT on branch, held for review, 2026-08-30

The 5th Studio Health tab (`sh-submissionlog`, previously a placeholder)
and the second write-feature. The delivery-tracking log: what packages
went out, when they were promised, when they actually shipped, and how
many times each one got reworked — the keystone data source for the
objective project/business KPIs, deliberately NOT computed in this pass.

**Data model — `submissions` collection, one doc per delivery EVENT**
(not one mutating doc per package): `{id, projectId, projectName,
packageName, revisionNumber, parentSubmissionId, committedDate,
actualDate, status ('pending'|'submitted'), notes, createdAt,
createdBy, updatedAt}`. `parentSubmissionId` is `null` on revision 1
and always points at the chain's **ORIGINAL** doc id for every later
revision — never at the immediately-prior revision — so "every cycle
for this package" is one flat query (`id===originalId ||
parentSubmissionId===originalId`), not a chain-walk. Proven with a
real 3-revision sequential-write fixture reviving from a MIDDLE
revision, not just rev1→rev2.

**Built**: `saveSubmission()` (the only write, mirrors `saveReview()`/
`saveLeaveRequest()` exactly), `computeNextRevision()` (pure — finds
the chain's true max revisionNumber, not just the doc-being-revised's
own +1), `groupSubmissionsByProject()` (pure — groups the flat list by
project then by chain, revisions ascending), `renderSubmissionLog()`
(fresh `source:'server'` fetch of `projects`+`submissions`),
`renderSubmissionLogBody()` (entry form + Revise action + grouped list
view). Form handles three modes off one shared UI (`new`/`edit`/
`revise`) — "fast to fill" per the ask, no separate revise-only screen.

**KPI-schema confirmation (explicit ask, not built this pass)** — code
comment above `saveSubmission()` in `index.html` confirms all 5 deferred
KPIs need zero schema change when built: on-time rate and slippage
both fall out of `committedDate` vs. `actualDate` directly; rework rate
is a count over the `parentSubmissionId` link already described above;
delivery rhythm groups by `projectId` + date, no new field; hours-per-
submission is flagged as NOT precisely attributable (no `timeLogs`
entry is tagged to a specific package) — when built it must be labeled
"approximate hours in this delivery window" (project's logged hours
between the previous submission's `actualDate` and this one's), never
presented as precise per-submission cost, same no-fake-precision rule
as Estimation Reliability's raw-hours approach.

**Storage safety**: same proven pattern, third time — `{merge:true}`,
`source:'server'` verify immediately after every write, throws rather
than silently succeeding on a failed verify. Confirmed `submissions` is
**not** registered in `computeCollectionDiffs()` (still hardcoded to
exactly `timeLogs`/`planEntries`/`projects`).

**Fixtures — `test/feat-studio-health-submission-log.test.js`: 39/39
passed.** Covers: source-text-only-`saveSubmission`-writes,
`computeNextRevision()`'s chain-max + always-points-at-original logic
(including the middle-revision case), `groupSubmissionsByProject()`'s
grouping/ordering, the write-safety shape (merge/verify/throw), a real
3-write sequential revise-chain against a mock db, the render
(entry form + grouped list + Revise button only on the chain tip,
empty state).

**Regression**: full existing suite clean, all 17 test files
(`node test/*.test.js` run individually, every file exit 0).

**Isolation confirmed**: `index.html` + the one new test file —
`api/`, `vercel.json` empty diff. `renderAppraisal()`/`appraisalData`
untouched (only referenced in comments, as before).

**Preview against REAL data** — sent as a file: the entry form plus a
grouped list, real project picker (30 real projects, including
BURDWAN RD and Kredent office), with BURDWAN RD's DD Package shown at
2 revisions (rev 2 resubmitted early after a client-requested layout
change) and its still-pending GFC Package, plus one on-time Kredent
Schematic Design delivery. **No real write was made** — `submissions`
confirmed empty in production (0 docs, fresh server read) immediately
before generating the preview; the seeded rows are realistic
hand-authored data run through the real extracted render functions,
not a claim that these deliveries already exist in Firestore.

**Status: held on this branch, unmerged, pushed to `origin`, awaiting
review.**

## Versatility fix — meaningful-contribution threshold, built 2026-08-30

Follow-up on the read-only Versatility investigation earlier the same
day (real data showed Angana's "11 projects" over a quarter was mostly
drive-by help — only 3 were a meaningful chunk of her own time). Built
exactly as scoped and approved.

**The fix**: `distinctProjectCount()` — the old raw "any project with
>=1 logged minute, no threshold, no productive check" — is retired and
replaced by `meaningfulProjectCount(logs, userId, start, end, minPct)`:
a project counts only if this person's PRODUCTIVE minutes on it are
`>= SH_VERSATILITY_MIN_PCT` (0.10, i.e. 10%) of their own total
productive project minutes in the window. Self-baselined against the
person's OWN total — not the project's total, and deliberately a lower,
separate constant from the estimation metric's 20%-of-the-PHASE's-hours
threshold (real data showed 20% would over-punish genuinely multi-
project people; see the constant's own comment in `index.html` for the
Suravi-shaped counter-example that set the number at 10%, not 20%).

**Both sides of the ratio are productive-only now**: a non-productive
entry tagged to a project no longer counts as involvement, and no
longer dilutes the denominator either — closing the second gap the
owner flagged (the old function had no `productive` check at all).

**Current count and own-average now share the same threshold** —
`computeVersatilityStat()` calls the new thresholded function on both
the current window AND every prior window before averaging, so the fix
can't be undone by comparing a thresholded "now" against an
unthresholded "usual." A dedicated fixture proves this directly (a
case that would have averaged 3 touched-projects/window under the old
function now correctly averages 1/window, matching the current side).

**One function, both call sites** — `computeVersatilityStat()` itself
didn't fork; both `renderIndividualInsightBody()` (Individual Insight)
and `computeObjectivePanelFacts()` (Employee Review's Objective Panel)
already called the same shared function, so fixing it once fixes both
call sites automatically — confirmed by a dedicated Employee-Review-side
fixture, not just assumed from the Individual Insight fix.

**Framing genuinely unchanged**: `computeVersatilityStat()` still
returns no `flagged`/`direction` field — a fixture asserts this
explicitly. Only the NUMBER changed; "could mean versatile utility...
or spread thin — worth understanding why" reads exactly as before.

**Fixtures**: `test/feat-studio-health-individual-insight.test.js` now
64/64 (was 56/56) — 8 new checks: the exact >=10% boundary (9%
excluded, 10% included, 11% included), the productive-only filter on
both sides of the ratio, current/avg-same-threshold consistency, an
Angana-shaped case (8 touched -> 3 meaningful), a Suravi-shaped case (9
touched -> 5 real commitments, not collapsed to 1), and the no-flag/
no-direction framing check. `test/feat-studio-health-employee-review.test.js`
now 46/46 (was 44/44) — 2 new checks: a source-text check that
`computeObjectivePanelFacts()` passes `SH_VERSATILITY_MIN_PCT` through
(not a second, unthresholded definition), and a structural render
proving the Objective Panel's Versatility fact reflects the same
5-touched/2-meaningful shape.

**Regression**: full existing suite clean, all 17 test files.

**Isolation confirmed**: `index.html` + the two existing Studio Health
test files — `api/`, `vercel.json` empty diff.

**Version bump**: deliberately NOT bumped this pass — matches this
branch's own precedent (every Studio Health commit so far, all client-
side changes, has deferred the `APP_VERSION`/`version.json` bump to
actual deploy time; none has ever touched it while held on this
unmerged branch). Will bump at merge/deploy, not before.

**Preview against REAL data** — sent as a file, both places Versatility
appears, using the real fixed code end-to-end: Suravi's Individual
Insight panel (30-day window, the feature's own default) now shows
**5** projects (was ~9, unthresholded) — her real month-long
commitments survive intact. Angana's panel at a 91-day/quarter window
(matching Employee Review's own window) now shows **3** (was ~11) —
matching the investigation's finding almost exactly. Her own-average at
that window came back low (0.3) because logged history that far back
is genuinely sparse for her — real data, not an artifact of the fix.

**Status: held on this branch, unmerged, pushed to `origin`, awaiting
review.**

## Estimation Reliability threshold fix — count relaxed 5+3 → 3+2, built 2026-08-31

Follow-up on the read-only Estimation investigation earlier the same
day, triggered by Angana (senior, many phases) showing "not enough
history" — turned out to be a real, team-wide bottleneck, not an
Angana-specific artifact. Built exactly as scoped and approved.

**Trace confirmed, and it's a different root cause than Versatility's**:
`computeEstimationPattern()` needs `SH_EST_RECENT_COUNT` +
`SH_EST_MIN_HISTORICAL_COUNT` qualifying phase-instances (old: 5+3=8
total) after the `>=SH_EST_MIN_CONTRIBUTION_PCT` (20%) filter, over an
**unbounded** lookback — `computePhaseContributions()` receives the
full `logs` array with no date window at all, so "not enough history"
was never about a short window. Swept the 20% contribution filter in
isolation (count held fixed at 5+3): identical insufficient-count at
20%/15%/10% — **confirmed NOT the bottleneck**, unlike Versatility.
Angana specifically: 2 phase-instances touched ever, 1 qualifying
(the other at a real 6.6% share, nowhere near even a relaxed
threshold) — genuinely short, not narrowly missing.

**Team-wide count — the actual diagnostic**: **8 of 9 people (89%)
showed "not enough history"** at the old 5+3 threshold; only Suravi
cleared it. Swept count combinations with 20% held fixed: 4+2 (6
total) → 5/9 insufficient; 3+2 (5 total) → 4/9; 2+2 (4 total) → still
4/9, rescuing nobody further — confirming 3+2 as the real floor of who
has genuine multi-phase history in this data, not an arbitrary
relaxation stopped short.

**The fix**: `SH_EST_RECENT_COUNT` 5→3, `SH_EST_MIN_HISTORICAL_COUNT`
3→2 (index.html, both new named constants documented in place — see
the comment block above them for the full sweep). `SH_EST_MIN_CONTRIBUTION_PCT`
deliberately **kept at 20%**, not lowered to match Versatility's 10% —
confirmed above it isn't the bottleneck, and it's correctly doing real
work here (excludes drive-by touches like Angana's 6.6% phase) since
Estimation asks "was this person responsible enough for this phase's
over/under-run," a genuinely different question from Versatility's
"was this a meaningful chunk of THEIR OWN time."

**One shared function, both call sites** — `computeEstimationPattern()`
itself didn't fork; `renderIndividualInsightBody()` (Individual
Insight) and `computeObjectivePanelFacts()` (Employee Review) already
called the same shared function with the same constants by name, so
fixing the constants once fixes both — confirmed by a source-text
fixture, not assumed.

**Verified sane, not noise**: the 5 newly-qualifying people's actual
computed patterns are real and legible, not thin-data artifacts —
Ridhi 33%/33% (flat), Suravi 33%/29% (flat), **Tasmin 100%/0% and
flags** (driven by real 243%/112%/120% recent overruns vs clean
historical phases — a genuine signal), Sarbani 0%/0% (flat), Rai
67%/100% (flat, historical already high). Framing genuinely
unchanged: still no verdict/grade field on the result, `flagged`
stays a plain boolean — a fixture asserts this directly.

**Budget-completeness check (read-only, as requested) — a separate,
larger finding, not fixed here**: `computePhaseContributions()` only
sees phases with a `budgetHours` value set on the project. Checked
across all 30 projects: **95 distinct (project, phase) combinations
have logged hours; only 35 (37%) have a budget set.** The other 60
(3,025 of 6,307 total logged phase-hours — **48%**) are entirely
invisible to this metric, independent of any threshold. **20 of 30
projects (67%) have zero budgeted phases at all** — budget entry looks
all-or-nothing per project (0 projects found partially budgeted: a
project either got its phases fully filled in or not touched at all).
Per person, 4-19 phases each sit on unbudgeted projects. This
directly explains part of why Angana still shows "not enough history"
even after this fix: she has 11 more phases with real logged hours
that this metric can never see, on top of her 2 budgeted ones — a
data-entry gap, not a threshold one. **Not fixed in this pass** (data
entry, not code) — reported so the threshold change isn't mistaken for
the whole picture.

**Fixtures**: `test/feat-studio-health-estimation-threshold.test.js`
(new, 19/19) — the exact 3+2 boundary (5 qualifying → sufficient, 4 →
still short), recent/historical sliced from one list (can't disagree),
a real-data-shaped team fixture reproducing the actual 8/9→4/9
transition AND the natural-floor claim (2+2 rescues nobody beyond
3+2), Tasmin's real over/under pattern reproduced from synthetic data
shaped to match (100%/0%, flags), the shared-constants/shared-function
source-text check, and the framing-unchanged check. Existing
Individual Insight (64/64) and Employee Review (46/46) suites re-run
unchanged and still green — the constant change alone didn't break
either.

**Regression**: full existing suite clean, all 17 test files on this
branch.

**Isolation confirmed**: `index.html` + the one new test file —
`api/`, `vercel.json` empty diff.

**Version bump**: deliberately NOT bumped this pass — same precedent
as every other Studio Health commit on this branch; will bump at
merge/deploy.

**Preview against REAL data** — sent as a file: Tasmin's, Ridhi's, and
Angana's Employee Review objective panels, rendered through the real

historical, real 243/112/120% overruns). Ridhi now shows a sufficient,
flat pattern (1/3 · 1/3). Angana still correctly shows "not enough
history yet · need 3 recent + 2 historical" — genuinely, not a
leftover artifact.
