## Recovered-doc-overlap quirk — precision cleanup, not urgent (found 2026-08-15)

Found during the same full-history audit. Three occurrences on Tasmin's
5 Aug (each ~1 minute): a session starts, goes stale almost immediately
and gets "recovered" down to a near-zero-duration artifact doc (e.g.
`10:36→10:36`, `durationMins:1`), and a subsequent gap-fill entry starts
at that *same* timestamp to cover the real gap.

**Exact cause**: `getLastLoggedEnd()` in `api/biometric-sync.js` (the
function that decides where a gap-fill's start boundary should be)
doesn't exclude near-zero-duration "recovered"/"reopenCorrected"
artifact docs when computing a user's last logged point for the day —
so it fills the gap right up against the artifact rather than treating
the artifact's own sliver as already covered, double-counting roughly
1-2 minutes per occurrence.

**Impact**: tiny — 1-2 min per occurrence, a handful of occurrences
found across 2 weeks of full-history data. Not a correctness issue at
any meaningful scale, just numeric noise.

**Status**: logged for a someday-fix, not urgent, not blocking Stage
3/4 or anything else. No code changed.

## Weekly Health Check — repeatable data-integrity sweep template

Logged 2026-08-15, generalized from the full-history read-only audit
run that day (2026-08-01 through 2026-08-15, all users, 368 docs). This
is the recurring admin check — run this same sweep periodically (weekly
suggested) so drift gets caught early instead of accumulating for weeks
before being noticed. Intended to eventually become the automated
biometric-anomaly-check (session-marker design, logged above) — until
that's built, this is the manual/on-demand equivalent, and any future
session (fresh context or not) should be able to run it from this
description alone.

**The 7 checks, run together as one sweep:**

1. **Implausible daily totals** — any user-day total over ~13h that
   hasn't already been explained/caught. Group `timeLogs` by
   `userId`+`date`, sum `durationMins`, flag anything over ~780min.
2. **Overlapping sessions** — same user, same day, time ranges that
   overlap (the double-count signature). Sort each user-day's docs by
   `startTime`, flag any doc whose `startTime` falls before the
   previous doc's `endTime`.
3. **Orphaned/stuck sessions** — any doc still `inProgress:true` that
   should have closed by now (i.e., not from today).
4. **Malformed docs** — blank `startTime`/`endTime` on entries that
   aren't gap-fill/self-report/lunch-break shaped; missing
   `userId`/`date`/`id`; `durationMins` that doesn't match computed
   `endTime - startTime` on a closed doc (allow ~15min slack for the
   lunch-deduction feature, which legitimately produces this pattern on
   purpose — don't flag it as an error without checking for that first,
   exactly the false-positive this audit ran into on its first pass).
5. **Lingering `reconciliationNeeded:true`** — should be zero at all
   times now that the Angana cause-fix is live; anything found here is
   worth investigating immediately, not filing away.
6. **Duplicate-doc signature** — self-reported entries (blank
   start/end, `desc` containing "Self-reported") alongside a normal doc
   for the same user+date — the Taskiya shape. Should be zero post-
   Glitch-3-fix; anything found here means the fix has a gap.
7. **OMS-vs-biometric reconciliation, BOTH trailing and mid-day gaps**
   — two sub-checks, not one: (a) run `api/biometric-sync.js` dry-run
   for each date in range to confirm trailing gaps to biometric OUT are
   covered (its own "already logged through OUTTime" signal does this
   part automatically); (b) separately check for **mid-day** gaps —
   time between two consecutive logged segments, same user+day, with no
   doc covering it — the dry-run's trailing-gap check does NOT catch
   these on its own, they need their own pass (find consecutive
   same-day docs sorted by `startTime`, flag any gap over ~20min
   between one doc's `endTime` and the next doc's `startTime`).

**What "clean" looks like**: zero on checks 1, 3, 5, 6; check 2 near-zero
(a handful of 1-minute recovered-doc artifacts is known, tiny, logged
above — not a red flag on its own); check 4 should show only the
15-min-lunch-deduction pattern, nothing else; check 7 should show
gaps only for already-explained cases (Suravi/Rai, tracked above) —
any NEW unexplained gap is worth a direct conversation with that
person, same as Suravi's pattern was escalated once it hit a third
occurrence.

**Result of the 2026-08-15 run**: clean at the scale that matters — see
the two items logged just above (Suravi's third-occurrence gap, Rai's
7 Aug gap, and the recovered-doc-overlap code quirk) as the only
findings; everything else came back clean.

### Code fixes — built on `fix-selfreport-supersede-and-sweep-race`, held for review

Two structural gaps, the actual root causes behind Souvik's and Rai's
cases above — fixed so they don't recur. On a fresh branch off `main`
(unrelated to Studio Health, which stays on its own branch).

**Fix 1 — self-report supersede** (Souvik's root cause): the
orphan-recovery modal's Save handler created a new doc for the honest
self-reported total but never closed the ORIGINAL session doc it was
replacing — the happy-path branch had no `.update()` on
`state.sessionLogId` at all, only the duplicate-discard branch (a
different case entirely) touched it. Now, right before the self-report
doc is pushed, if `state.sessionLogId` exists the original doc gets an
immediate targeted close: `inProgress:false`, `reconciliationNeeded:false`,
`durationMins:0` (zeroed for the same double-count reason as the manual
cleanup above), desc noting superseded, `supersededBySelfReport:true`.
Same "don't defer to autosave" immediacy as `closeCurrentSessionNow()`,
same synchronous local-array mirror.

**Fix 2 — sweep re-flag race** (Rai's root cause): the three
stale-heartbeat sweep functions (`cleanupStaleTimers`,
`finalizeStaleHeartbeatSessions`, `finalizeStaleHeartbeatSessionsAllUsers`)
each read an initial snapshot, decide "stale," then write
`reconciliationNeeded:true` per doc with no re-verification immediately
before that write — if a session closes cleanly in the gap between the
snapshot read and a specific doc's write landing, the sweep's write can
silently re-flag a doc that was just correctly resolved (sweep writes
never touch `inProgress`, only the flag fields). New shared
`isStillFlaggableOnServer(id)` — a fresh `source:'server'` single-doc
re-read immediately before each write, mirroring the Glitch 3 fix's own
"re-check at write time" principle applied to the other side of the same
race class. Fails closed (skips flagging) on a negative confirmation OR
an unverifiable read. Applied identically to all three sweeps.
`finalizeStaleHeartbeatSessionsAllUsers()` gained a `closedSinceRead`
counter in its result/log line, matching its own existing
always-log-every-run discipline.

**Fixtures**: `test/fix-selfreport-supersede-original.test.js` (new,
25/25) — central fixture proves the real Souvik shape (new doc AND
original closed), plus discard-branch non-interference, write-failure
resilience, and missing-sessionLogId/missing-local-doc edge cases.
`test/fix-sweep-reflag-race-guard.test.js` (new, 17/17) — central
fixture models the actual race against all three sweep functions
directly (re-verify read returns `inProgress:false`, flag write must not
fire), plus the genuinely-still-stale case (must still flag, unaffected),
fail-closed-on-read-error, and the two pre-existing short-circuits
(fresh heartbeat, already-flagged) confirmed to still skip the new check
entirely (no wasted read on the common healthy path).
`test/fix-glitch3-race-guard.test.js` updated (23/23, was 19/23 broken
by the new expected supersede write) — 3 assertions that predated Fix 1
were checking "no telemetry write at all" in cases where Fix 1 now
correctly writes a supersede update; updated to distinguish
discard-shaped writes (still absent, as before) from supersede-shaped
writes (now correctly present) rather than asserting total absence.

**Regression**: full existing suite clean, all 15 test files on this
branch (no Studio Health test files here — this branch is off `main`).

**Isolation confirmed**: `index.html` + 3 test files (1 new-behavior
update, 2 new files) — `api/`, `vercel.json` empty diff; zero
Studio-Health references touched; zero `diffMode`/`saveData`/
`computeCollectionDiffs`/`commitInChunks` touched.

**Version bump**: deliberately not applied yet — held on branch for

## Budget-distribution feature — scoped, then built on branch, held for review, 2026-08-31

Read-only scope first (see the scope conversation for the full write-
safety confirmation), then built exactly as approved. Speeds up
backfilling the 20 projects with missing budgets, plus every future
project.

**Templates — pulled from real repeated project allocations, not
invented**: `DEFAULT_BUDGET_TEMPLATES`, two templates —
**Standard Design-Build** (SD 15% · FD 20% · CD 30% · MSC 5% · SS 20% ·
PM 10%, matching CLUB ONE WORLD/BURDWAN RD/BELGACHHIYA EXP CENTRE's
byte-identical real percentages) and **Design-Heavy** (SD 10% · FD 25%
· CD 20% · MSC 25% · SS 10% · PM 10%, matching AVANA/Kredent office).
Both sum to exactly 100%, both map only to the app's real `PHASES`
names.

**Storage — `companyData/squareDB`, same pattern as `holidays`**: a new
`budgetTemplates` field on the existing shared config doc, loaded/saved/
backup-restored at all three of `holidays`' own sites (confirmed
matching, not assumed). Explicitly **not** a new collection, and
explicitly **not** a new field on project docs — verified empirically
before building anything: all 30 real project docs currently have zero
fields beyond `{id, name, client, startDate, endDate, phases, updatedAt,
updatedBy}`, and this feature keeps it that way.

**Write path — `saveProject()` reused verbatim, confirmed unmodified**:
Distribute only fills the existing phase `<input>` elements in the
project modal; it never touches `projectsData`, never calls
`saveProject()`/`autoSave()`, never writes anything. The existing
`#saveProjBtn` click handler (byte-for-byte untouched — a fixture
confirms zero diff hunks touch `saveProject()` itself) is still the
sole write path, same full-overwrite `.set()` as every project edit
today. `computeCollectionDiffs()`/`syncChangedDocs()`/`diffMode` all
confirmed untouched too — this is a normal project edit through the
already-proven backstop-safe path, not a new write mechanism.

**Rounding — largest-remainder method**: `distributeBudgetByTemplate(totalHours,
templatePcts)` floors each phase's raw share, then hands the leftover
hours (lost to flooring, always fewer than the 6 phases) one-by-one to
the phases with the largest fractional remainder — sums to EXACTLY the
entered total every time, deterministic tie-breaking via `PHASES`' own
fixed order.

**UI — inside the existing project modal, structurally prevents "distribute
→ blind save"**: a "Distribute from total" control (template picker +
total-hours input + Distribute button) sits above the existing phase
inputs. Distribute fills those SAME inputs; the owner reviews/adjusts
them exactly as they would any manual edit, then clicks the existing,
unchanged Save Project button. A new "Manage Templates" modal lets the
owner edit percentages and add templates — edits a DRAFT copy, only

## Budget-distribution "missing picker" report — investigated, NOT a code bug, 2026-08-31

Reported: the template picker (Standard Design-Build vs Design-Heavy)
appeared to be missing from the Distribute control in the preview sent
above -- only a total-hours field and a Distribute button seemed
usable.

**Root cause: a preview-delivery bug, not a code bug.** The original
preview's `<select id="pDistributeTemplate">` was populated by a
client-side `<script>` (`populateDistributeTemplateSelect()`) that ran
on page load -- and the surface that displayed that file rendered it
as a static HTML snapshot, so the script never executed and the select
appeared empty. Confirmed directly, not assumed: copied the file into
a real local static server (`npx http-server`) and loaded it in an
actually-executing browser tab. Real result, read straight from the
live DOM:
- `#pDistributeTemplate` had exactly 2 `<option>`s: "Standard
  Design-Build" and "Design-Heavy", both selectable.
- Switching to Standard Design-Build and clicking the real Distribute
  button produced a genuinely different result at the same 3317h
  total: SD 498h/15% · FD 663h/20% · CD 995h/30% · MSC 166h/5% ·
  SS 663h/20% · PM 332h/10% -- the CD-heavy shape, distinct from
  Design-Heavy's FD/MSC-heavy 332/829/663/829/332/332.
- Design-Heavy still reproduced AVANA's real stored budget exactly on
  all 6 phases, as already confirmed at build time.

**No change made to `index.html`** -- `git status`/`git diff` confirm
zero diff on this branch after the investigation. The shipped feature
already satisfies every point in the "check and fix" list: the picker
exists, lists templates dynamically from `budgetTemplates` (not
hardcoded), is visible and selectable, and produces genuinely
different results per template.

**Fixed instead**: the preview file itself. The corrected version
bakes both templates' `<option>` tags and both distributions directly
into the static HTML at generation time (Node), so the core content is
visible with zero JavaScript execution required -- the live `<script>`
wiring stays too, for real interactivity if opened in an actual
browser, but is no longer load-bearing for what needs to be seen.

**Status: no code change. Corrected preview sent, showing both
templates' distinct results side by side.**

## August team-report data check — clean, one real artifact found and corrected, 2026-08-31/09-01

Read-only sweep across all of August before the monthly report went out
to the team. Checked: per-person totals, unresolved stale/flagged
sessions, duplicate/double-count patterns, the shipped reconciliation
fixes' own telemetry, and Suravi's manual gap-fills.

**Per-person August totals** (productive minutes, 1-31 Aug): 8 active
staff landed in a plausible 143-178h band. **Sarbani: 0h, fully
explained** — approved leave requests cover literally every day of
August (last real work day 31 July) -- not a data gap.

**Clean**: zero `reconciliationNeeded:true`/`inProgress:true` docs
anywhere in August. Zero stuck flags (Rai-pattern). Zero new orphaned
stubs (Souvik-pattern) beyond the one already resolved and fixed.
`orphanDuplicateDiscarded` telemetry: still exactly the one known hit
(Angana, 28→29 Aug) -- nothing new fired, the Glitch 3 fix hasn't had
to catch anything else this month. Suravi's 4 manual gap-fill days
(Aug 1/13/14/18) all verified doc-by-doc: sequential, zero time-range
overlap, correctly reflected in her total, not double-counted.

**One real artifact found: Rai, 28 Aug** -- a session (`mtcm0o6cuhk7r1`)
that went stale and wasn't reopened until 31 Aug hit the 12h
`MAX_SESSION_HOURS` cap (`autoCapped:true`, `durationMins:720`)
instead of Glitch 5's smarter last-activity/ceiling logic, because the
12h cap fired first (see the refinement note below). This inflated his
28 Aug day to 13.48h and his August month total to 173.52h -- both
would have looked odd, and been wrong, in the team report.

### Rai's 28 Aug correction -- targeted, verified, 2026-09-01

Confirmed genuine miss-punch (no biometric OUT that day). Applied
Glitch 5's exact logic by hand: previous doc's end (12:37) doesn't
qualify as an anchor (not strictly after this session's own 12:37:54
start); own last heartbeat resolves to **29 Aug 11:19** -- past the
20:30 same-day ceiling -- so the correct close is the **20:30 IST
ceiling clamp**, giving a real duration of **472min (7.87h)**, not the
720min (12h) artifact.

**Targeted write** (`mtcm0o6cuhk7r1`): `durationMins` 720→472,
`endTime` "11:19"→"20:30", `desc` rewritten to explain the correction,
`estimatedClose:true` and `ceilingClamped:true` added, `autoCapped`
removed (`deleteField()` -- it was never a genuine 12h session).
Freeze-checked immediately before the write (doc re-read, confirmed
byte-identical to the investigation turn); `source:'server'` verify
after.

**Verified precisely, not assumed**: a full field-by-field diff
between the freeze-check snapshot and the post-write server read
confirmed exactly the 7 intended fields changed and nothing else --
`sessionStartMs`, `lastHeartbeat`, `startTime`, `userId`, `date`,
`reconciliationNeeded`/`Since`, `productive`, `ts`, `updatedAt`,
`updatedBy`, `activity`, `projectId`/`projectName`/`phase`/`typology`,
`appVersion`, `recovered`, `id` all byte-identical before/after.
Corrected totals recomputed **fresh from server, twice, matching**:
28 Aug = **561min (9.35h)**, August month = **10,163min (169.38h)** --
both exactly matching the pre-approved plan. Queue confirmed still 0
after the write.

**Status: correction complete and verified. Rai's August total is now
accurate for the team report.**

### Refinement logged (not urgent) -- the 12h cap can pre-empt Glitch 5's smarter logic

When a session goes stale and stays open across multiple days, the
12h `MAX_SESSION_HOURS` cap can fire and close it (`autoCapped:true`,
capped at exactly 12h) *before* Glitch 5's own last-activity/ceiling
logic ever gets a chance to run on it -- the 12h cap is a simpler,
earlier-firing safety net, and it wins the race by construction
whenever a stale session survives long enough to hit it. Glitch 5's
20:30-ceiling-with-flag-on-clamp design would have produced a more
honest number here (7.87h vs a flat 12h) had it been the one to close
this session.

**Future refinement (not urgent, logged for later)**: when the EOD-close/
miss-punch pass (`api/biometric-sync.js`'s `closeReconciliationNeededSessions()`)
finds a session that's already been closed via the 12h cap rather than
genuinely resolved, it could re-evaluate and apply Glitch 5's
ceiling-cap logic instead of leaving the flat 12h value in place --
same honest-number principle Glitch 5 was built for, just extended to
cover this one sequencing edge case. No action taken this pass;
today's fix was a targeted, manual correction of the one real instance
found, not a code change.

## Lunch Break / Non-Productive / "Unlogged" reconciliation check — read-only, 2026-09-01

August report claimed: Souvik's 7h51m and Neha's 7h44m "Unlogged"
entries originally appeared as giant Lunch Break sessions reclassified
by OMS; and the report's own Lunch Break total (~35h55m) vs its own
Non-Productive headline (~32h18m) don't reconcile (~3h37m gap).
Checked both claims against real production data (3,574-doc `timeLogs`
snapshot, same one used for the Data Quality build above). No writes.

**Claim 1 (Souvik/Neha) — factually wrong on classification.** Both
flagged entries are real docs (`gapfill-mmllimboc39-2026-08-04-1127`,
471min; `gapfill-mmllpsq6rih-2026-08-12-1058`, 464min) with
`productive:true`, `projectName:"Unlogged"`. Neither is tagged Lunch
Break, in code or in the data — they're biometric-presence gap-fills
(`desc: "...auto-filled from biometric presence/OUT..."`), a
completely different code path from Lunch Break logging. The exact
durations match the report's numbers, but the "started as a giant
Lunch Break, got reclassified" story doesn't — there's no Lunch Break
doc at those durations/times to reclassify from.

**Claim 2 (the 3h37m gap) — real, but not a bug: the two headline
numbers are answering different questions.** Real August figures:
Lunch Break (by project/activity label) = **2,155min = 35.92h**,
matching the report's ~35h55m almost exactly. Non-Productive headline
(`total − prodMins`, i.e. `productive===false`) = **1,938min =
32.30h**, matching the report's ~32h18m exactly. Gap = **217min =
3h37m — exact match to the report's claimed discrepancy.**

Root cause, found directly in the data: **10 of the 87 Lunch Break
entries (217min total) are tagged `productive:true`** — real work
logged during the lunch window, `desc:"Work on project"`, spread
across Aug 5/10/13/14/20/24/25/29/29/31 (Neha, Souvik ×6, Tasmin,
Ridhi). "Lunch Break" (35.92h) is a sum by project label; "Non-
Productive" (32.30h) is a sum by the `productive` flag. They overlap
97.5% but aren't the same set by construction — 217min of
label="Lunch Break" is flagged productive and correctly excluded from
Non-Productive. Confirmed this is not a dedupe artifact either: ran
the real `dedupeSessionLogs()` against raw August data — Lunch Break
totals are bit-identical raw vs. deduped (2,155min both ways; dedupe
drops 9 unrelated tiny-starter/duplicate entries elsewhere, 14min
total, none of them Lunch Break).

**Does it affect anyone's actual hours?** No. `Non-Productive = total
− prodMins` is a pure arithmetic identity inside `runTimeReport()` —
it cannot diverge from a direct sum by construction, and it doesn't:
Souvik's and Neha's 471min/464min "Unlogged" hours are correctly
counted productive regardless. The 217min gap is a **category-label
vs. productive-flag presentation ambiguity in how the report presents
"Lunch Break,"** not a computation error and not a hit to anyone's
total/productive minutes — OMS's own totals reconcile internally with
zero gap either way this is sliced.

**Read-only. No code, no data changes.**

## Lunch Break productive:true fix — 10 docs, data write, 2026-09-01

Followed the reconciliation check above with a targeted data fix.

**Case-read, all 10 candidates (Lunch Break label, productive:true,
217min total)** — fresh server pull, full raw docs: Neha 08-05 (27m),
Souvik 08-10/13/14/20/24/25/29 (18/25/25/13/19/38/23m), Tasmin 08-29
(11m), Ridhi 08-31 (18m). Every one: `desc:"Work on project"` (byte-
identical generic string across all 10 — not a person-typed work
description), `projectId:null`, `phase:""`, `typology:""`, all inside
a 13:22–15:41 window. Contrasted against the shape of a real
productive-work doc (always carries a real `projectId`+`phase`+
`typology`+project-specific `desc`) and against the full 2,848-doc
productive:true population: only 53 entries anywhere have this empty
shape, 51 of them are these Lunch Break docs, the other 2 are the
unrelated "Unlogged" gap-fills from the check above. **All 10 = Case
1 (genuine lunch, mis-flagged) — zero Case 2 (no real work
mislabeled).** Nothing held back for relabeling.

**Freeze-check**: the 4 affected people (Neha, Souvik, Tasmin, Ridhi)
each had one unrelated in-progress session live at write time (today,
Sep 1, different doc ids/dates — normal mid-workday state, not the
docs being touched). Re-pulled all 10 targets `source:'server'`
immediately before write — byte-identical to the case-read, none
in-progress. Safe to proceed.

**Write**: `updateDoc(ref, {productive: false})` per doc, targeted
single-field change, per-item try/catch, no batch/transaction.
**10/10 verified after write** — `source:'server'` re-read of each
doc confirms `productive` flipped `true→false` and every other field
(duration, times, desc, project, ids, timestamps) byte-identical to
the pre-write snapshot.

**Reconciliation, before → after** (fresh `source:'server'` pull,
real August data):

| | Lunch Break total | Non-Productive (`total−prodMins`) | Gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| Before | 2,155min (35.92h) | 1,938min (32.30h) | 217min |
| After | 2,155min (35.92h) | **2,155min (35.92h)** | **0min** |

Gap closes cleanly, exactly as predicted — Lunch Break and
Non-Productive are now identical, matching the finding above. No
other total moved: this only reclassifies 217min from productive to
non-productive for 4 people; nobody's `date`/`duration`/`project` data
changed.

**Forward fix, logged for later (not built this pass)**: the
underlying gap is that logging a session under the "Lunch Break"
project doesn't force `productive:false` at write time — it's
possible to end up with `projectName:"Lunch Break"` + `productive:
true` today. A small logging-side change (auto-set `productive:false`
whenever `projectName==='Lunch Break'`, client-side at save) would
close this permanently. Deliberate, separate pass — not done here.

**Read-only investigation, then a deliberate, verified, targeted
write — 10 docs, 1 field each, on the user's explicit instruction.**

**v2 idea, logged for later (not scoped or built)**: a
**data-trustworthiness grade** — combine the confidence-mix (Feature
A) and contradiction% (Feature B) into a single A/B/C grade per
person/period. Must be framed explicitly as **DATA QUALITY** (how
clean/verifiable someone's own logging habits are), **never as a
performance or productivity grade** — same care as Feature A/B's
original scope. Build as a v2 layer on top of the now-shipped Data
Quality panel, not a replacement for it.

## Data-trustworthiness grade (v2) — read-only scope, 2026-09-01

Scoped combining the two shipped signals (confidence-mix, phase/
typology contradiction%) into one A/B/C per person. No code. Real
all-time per-person numbers, fresh pull:

| Person | Verified% | Contradiction% | Grade |
|---|---|---|---|
| Sarbani | 94.0% | 0.8% | A |
| Ridhi | 89.2% | 2.5% | A |
| Angana | 92.2% | 2.6% | A |
| Neha | 90.7% | 3.0% | A |
| Rai | 92.7% | 5.5% | A |
| Souvik | 95.1% | 18.7% | B |
| Suravi | 95.7% | 25.5% | B |
| Tasmin | 87.7% | 69.5% | C |
| Taskiya | 85.6% | 90.0% | C |

**Key honest finding: verified% barely discriminates (85.6–95.7%
across everyone, a 10-point band) — contradiction% carries almost all
the real signal (0.8–90%, a 90-point band).** For this real
population, no one's grade is actually decided by the verified-%
half of the formula; it's a safety net for a future person whose
verified% genuinely craters (self-report-heavy), not doing live work
today. Disclosed rather than hidden.

**Cutoffs placed in the real data's own empty gaps, not round
numbers**: contradiction% clusters naturally into 0.8–5.5% (5
people), 18.7–25.5% (2 people), 69.5–90% (2 people) — two clean gaps
at ~6–19% and ~26–70%. A/B/C boundaries (contra ≤10% / ≤40% / >40%,
verified ≥85% for A, <60% forces C regardless) sit inside those gaps,
so no one is a coin-flip away from a different grade. Result: **A:5,
B:2, C:2 — not everyone A, not everyone C, and the split tracks a
real qualitative break in the data (Tasmin/Taskiya are genuinely
different from everyone else), not an artifact of where the cutoff
line was drawn.**

**No-black-box**: the grade always ships paired with both
underlying numbers, never a bare letter — same principle as every
other Studio Health panel this engagement has built.

**UI framing (concrete, not "we'll be careful")**:
- Section label: **"Log Reliability"**, not "person grade" — same
  register as "Data Quality."
- Permanent banner, unmissable, same structural role as Employee
  Review's insight-not-verdict framing: *"This grades the DATA, not
  the person. A low grade means these specific logs need a second
  look before you rely on them for reporting — it says nothing about
  how someone is performing at their job. Two people can do equally
  strong work while one logs it less precisely than the other."*
- Per-grade copy, always shown with the letter:
  - A: *"Clean & verifiable — most hours are directly confirmed or
    cross-checked against biometric time."*
  - B: *"Mostly reliable — a meaningful share is reconstructed or
    doesn't fully reconcile."*
  - C: *"Needs review — reconstructed, self-reported, or
    contradictory entries make up a large share. Worth checking
    before trusting these numbers, not a comment on the work
    itself."*

**Honest read on per-person vs. per-period/per-project**: the real
risk isn't the grade itself, it's a UI affordance — a sortable
leaderboard-style table next to names invites exactly the "who's
worst" reading no caption fixes. Per-project would dodge that
entirely but doesn't answer what was actually asked ("whose data can
I trust") — that question is inherently person-indexed; a project
grade can't answer it. Recommendation: **keep it per-person** (it's
the honest answer to the actual question), but: (1) never a sortable/
rankable grade column — render as an individual card per person, not
a table row; (2) grade always co-displayed with its two numbers,
never a bare letter, everywhere it appears; (3) add a minimum-sample
floor before assigning a grade at all (an "insufficient data yet"
state below some checkable-entry count) — same statistical-noise
guard as Estimation Reliability's existing count threshold — none of
today's 9 people need it (94–348 checkable entries each) but a
new/part-time person could otherwise get a noisy grade off a handful
of entries; (4) admin-only, same gate as the rest of Studio Health,
never shown to the graded person as a self-view.

**Pure-read confirmed**: derived entirely from the two already-
shipped pure-read functions (`gradeTimeLogConfidence`,
`TYPOLOGY_TO_PHASE`/contradiction check) against the same
`timeLogs`+`users` fetch the Data Quality panel already makes — no
new collection, no new field, no write path of any kind.

**Not built. Scope only, awaiting review.**

## Firebase cost investigation — read-only code analysis, 2026-09-02

Bill timeline: July ₹7,789 → August ₹6,575 → September ₹5.17 (owner
pulling the real Console breakdown separately; this is CODE-side
evidence only). Re-diagnosis: old clobber architecture vs C/D
migration, not PITR. **Confirmed, directly from the commit history and
current code, all 6 points:**

**1. Pre-C/D (July, "shadow" diffMode) — confirmed exactly as
characterized.** `saveData()`'s own comment on the shadow-mode branch:
*"PRIMARY write path in shadow mode: the ORIGINAL unconditional
full-array rewrite, byte-for-byte the same mechanism that shipped
before this diff engine existed."* Triggered by `periodicSaveInterval`
every **30s** (confirmed by name and by a V15 comment on the primary-
mode branch contrasting it: *"periodicSaveInterval now calls
saveData() unconditionally every 30s"*). Separately,
`backstopFullSave()` did its own **unconditional full rewrite of all
three collections every 5 minutes, regardless of whether anything had
changed** — pre-Stage-C comment: *"forces a COMPLETE rewrite of all
three collections... every cycle."* Two independent full-rewrite
paths, each `commitInChunks()`-writing the entire `timeLogs`
(3,000+ and growing) + `planEntries` + `projects` arrays. Back-of-
envelope for the backstop alone, zero actual edits: ~48 cycles/day ×
~4,000 docs ≈ **190,000 writes/day per open tab**, before counting the
30s tick's own full rewrites on top whenever `hasUnsavedChanges` was
true (likely most of a working day, across ~10 users' tabs). Millions
of writes/month is the right order of magnitude, not hyperbole.

**2. C/D migration (merged `22d5c966`, 2026-08-16) — confirmed, exact
mechanism.** Stage C converted `backstopFullSave()` from the
unconditional full-array rewrite to the same
`computeCollectionDiffs()`/`syncChangedDocs()` cycle `saveData()`
already used in shadow (observe-only). Stage D flipped
`diffMode: 'shadow' → 'primary'`, making the diff-based write the real
path. `syncChangedDocs()` writes only the docs the in-memory hash
comparison found changed/deleted — a handful per cycle, not the whole
collection. An idle tick (nothing changed) now costs **zero**
Firestore reads or writes — confirmed by the early-return guard in
`saveData()` before any network call.

**3. Timeline match — confirmed, dates line up exactly.** C/D merged
2026-08-16 (mid-August, mid-billing-cycle) — matches "August =
transition" precisely. July predates it entirely (full clobber cost
all month). September is the first full month entirely under
`diffMode: 'primary'`. The magnitude (₹7,789 → ₹5) is consistent with
going from "rewrite ~4,000 docs every 30s-5min, per open tab, all day"
to "write only what actually changed, a handful of docs a day."

**4. C/D also solved a cost problem, not just a data-integrity one —
confirmed.** The commit message for `22d5c966` frames it entirely as a
delete-safety fix (the clobber's actual documented risk was silent
data loss, not billing) — cost was never the stated motivation. But
the SAME full-rewrite pattern that risked clobbering data was
mechanically the SAME thing burning ~₹7k/month. Fixing one structurally
fixed the other as a side effect, not a coincidence — they were the
same code path.

**5. Confirmed real, still-live waste — NOT solved, C/D never touched
it (it's a READ, not a write).** `startDashboardAutoRefresh()`
(`index.html:7180`) — while the Dashboard page is open, every 30
seconds, unconditionally: `db.collection("timeLogs").get({source:
'server'})` with **no filter at all** — the entire collection, every
cycle, for as long as the tab stays on Dashboard. Dashboard is the
default post-login landing page (`nav-item active" data-page=
"dashboard"`) and starts this loop via `renderDashboard() →
startLiveTimerUpdates() → startDashboardAutoRefresh()`; correctly
torn down on navigating away (`stopLiveTimerUpdates()` on any other
page), so it's not an unbounded leak, but it is real and sizable
whenever Dashboard is left open — the two other 30s pollers in the
same function (`fetchActiveTimersFromFirebase()`,
`loadReconciliationQueue()`) are both properly filtered
(`where('inProgress','==',true)` / `where('reconciliationNeeded','==
',true)`, ≤10-15 docs); only this one reads everything. Unlike the
diff-based write cost (flat, stays cheap forever), this read cost
**grows every month as `timeLogs` grows** — the one thing in the
current architecture that will quietly get more expensive over time.
Second, smaller instance of the same pattern: every Studio Health tab
(`renderIndividualInsight`/`renderEmployeeReview`/`renderDataQuality`/
`renderLogReliability`) does its own fresh, unfiltered
`timeLogs.get({source:'server'})` **on every tab click** (by design,
so admins never see a stale snapshot) — admin-only and click-bounded
rather than a standing 30s loop, so much lower volume, but the same
shape of waste. `loadData()` (once per login) is NOT part of this
finding — it already scopes `timeLogs` to the last 90 days.

**6. PITR — ruled OUT as a driver, confirmed by the dates alone.**
Enabled 2026-08-16, the SAME DAY as the C/D merge (per
`ROLLBACK-PLAN-CD-MIGRATION.md` and `FINDINGS-2026-08-10.md`'s own
"Stage C+D" section) — as the migration's rollback safety net, not
related to cost. It came ON exactly when writes became cheap, so it
never saw the old clobber's write volume at all, only the tiny
diff-based writes from day one. Firestore PITR bills on versioned-
write storage over its retention window, so its cost necessarily
tracks write volume — and write volume was already low the entire
time PITR has existed. Can't be the explanation for either the July
number (predates PITR) or the September number (PITR was on for all
of it and the number is still ₹5). Not a meaningful line item either
way, on the dates alone.

**Answer to the direct question: yes, confirmed — July's ₹7,789 was
the old clobber architecture's unconditional full-collection rewrites
(both the 30s primary tick and the independent 5-min backstop sweep),
C/D fixed exactly that, and PITR is not part of the story. Not fully
solved, though: the Dashboard's unfiltered 30s `timeLogs` re-read
(#5) is real, live today, and — unlike everything else here — gets
worse every month as the collection grows. That's the highest-value
remaining fix.**

**Ranked wins, cheapest/highest-impact first:**
1. **Date-filter Dashboard's 30s refresh** (`index.html:7194`) — same
   pattern `loadData()` already uses one screen away
   (`.where("date",">=",cutoffDate)`, e.g. today or last 7 days
   instead of all-time). One line. Kills the only cost driver in this
   codebase that scales with collection size instead of activity.
2. **Cache Studio Health's per-tab fetch across tab switches within a
   session**, or at minimum date-scope it the same way — currently
   every click re-reads the entire collection by design; admin-only
   and low-frequency today, but grows with usage and with the
   collection.
3. **Nothing else stands out** — cron reads (`biometric-sync.js`,
   `finalize-stale.js`) are all `where()`-filtered to a user+date or a
   status flag, never a full scan; the two other 30s dashboard pollers
   are filtered; `loadData()` is already 90-day-scoped; the two
   `onSnapshot` listeners are a single doc and a small collection
   (`leaveRequests`), not `timeLogs`. Read-side, #1 and #2 above are
   the whole remaining list.

**Read-only. No code, no data, no Firebase settings changed.**

## Dashboard auto-refresh cost fix — built on branch, held for review, 2026-09-02

Fixed win #1 from the cost investigation above:
`startDashboardAutoRefresh()`'s 30s `timeLogs` re-read was unfiltered
(entire collection, every cycle, for as long as Dashboard stayed
open) — the one cost driver that grows every month as the collection
grows. Branch `fix-dashboard-refresh-cost`, cut from `main`.

**The fix**: added `.where("date", ">=", cutoffDate)`, 90 days back,
computed identically to `loadData()`'s own cutoff (same `new Date()` +
`setDate(getDate()-90)` + `fmt()`).

**Why 90 days, not shorter (checked what Dashboard actually needs
first, per instruction)**: Dashboard's own rendered content — this-
month stat cards (`monthLogs` filters on the current calendar month),
the current-week calendar grid, the live "who's working now"/recent-
logs feed — only ever needs "this month" at most, genuinely a
current-activity view as guessed. But this refresh replaces the
**shared global `timeLogs` array** other pages read from
(`phaseLoggedMins()` for Projects & Budget's phase-budget totals, Time
Report), and that array is already, by design, only ever 90-day-
scoped (`loadData()`'s own established baseline — Time Report already
has its own UI warning when a requested range exceeds it: *"Data older
than X is not loaded"*). Matching 90 days exactly means no page loses
data it could see before this change. Going shorter (e.g. "today
only") would have silently starved Projects & Budget and Time Report
of days 8-90 they already expect to have — a real regression, not a
free win. (Worth noting: today, pre-fix, the unfiltered refresh
accidentally gave those other pages ALL-TIME data whenever Dashboard
had been opened that session, wider than `loadData()`'s own 90-day
promise — an unintended, inconsistent, and expensive side effect this
fix also removes, not just the read cost.)

**Confirmed safe against the delete-guard**: `diffCollection()` only
flags a doc as a delete candidate when it's in `docSnapshots` but
missing from the current array. `snapshotCollection('timeLogs',
timeLogs)` already runs immediately after this refresh and rebuilds
`docSnapshots` from exactly the filtered array — a doc outside the
window is never in `docSnapshots` to begin with, so nothing can look
"missing" and become a false delete candidate. Traced through
`diffCollection()`'s actual logic line by line to confirm this before
writing the fix, not assumed.

**Studio Health's per-tab full-collection reads (win #2) — flagged,
NOT fixed, per instruction ("if it's more involved, flag it
separately").** Not the same clean pattern as Dashboard: these panels
(`renderIndividualInsight`/`renderEmployeeReview`/`renderDataQuality`/
`renderLogReliability`, plus the dormant `renderStudioHealth`)
explicitly want all-time data by design — Data Quality's own UI copy
says *"All logged history, not just one period,"* and Log Reliability's
minimum-sample floor exists specifically to need enough history to be
stable. Date-scoping them would degrade their own correctness, not
just their cost. The alternative (cache across tab switches) would
undo an explicit, deliberate freshness guarantee already documented in
that code: *"re-fetches fresh on every click... same 'don't trust a
snapshot' reasoning as Project Health's own refresh link."* Admin-only
and click-bounded (not a standing timer) — real, but far smaller and
non-growing compared to the Dashboard's loop. Left alone.

**Confirmed exhaustive (instruction #4)**: reviewed every `timeLogs`
read site in `index.html` and `api/` (grep across the whole file, ~30
call sites). Everything else is either a single-doc get/update, a
`.limit()`-bounded bloat check, or filtered by
`userId`/`inProgress`/`reconciliationNeeded`/`date`. The Dashboard
refresh (now fixed) and the Studio Health tab family (flagged, not
fixed) were the only two unfiltered full-collection reads anywhere in
the app.

**Fixtures — `test/fix-dashboard-refresh-date-scope.test.js`, new,
17/17**: source-text (where-clause present and chained before `.get`,
cutoff math byte-identical to `loadData()`'s), a mock Firestore that
*actually filters* by the requested where-clause (proves in-window
docs load, out-of-window docs are excluded, the stale array is
genuinely replaced not appended to, `docSnapshots` stays consistent
with the filtered array), the timer-running guard still protects a
live in-progress session unchanged, and a sanity check that "this
month" can never fall outside the 90-day window for any calendar
month. **Regression: all 25 test files green** (was 24 before this
branch).

**Isolation**: `index.html` (one function) + one new test file only.

**Version bump**: deliberately not applied — held for review, same
precedent as every prior branch-only build.

**Status: held on this branch, unmerged, pushed to `origin`, awaiting
review.**

## Dashboard refresh cost fix — MERGED and DEPLOYED, 2026-09-03

Real billing data confirmed the fix was worth shipping: September
running ~₹4/day (~₹120/mo projected), and the actual driver is **read
overage** — 167K reads, exceeded the 50K/day free tier on 2026-09-02.
Writes are fine (C/D already fixed those). Shipped
`fix-dashboard-refresh-cost` → `main`.

**Pre-merge**: `main` unchanged since the branch was cut (merge-base
== `main`'s HEAD == `origin/main`'s HEAD — a straight fast-forward,
not a real 3-way merge, despite it having felt like a lot of time had
passed). Dry-run (`--no-commit --no-ff`) clean, zero conflicts.
Isolation confirmed: exactly `index.html` (one function) + one new
test file. Full regression green on the merged-but-uncommitted state
before committing (25/25 test files) — then committed for real.

**Version bump**: `2026-09-01.1` → **`2026-09-03.1`**. Pushed to
`main`, deployed.

**Post-deploy verification, all confirmed**:
- HTTP 200, `/api/version` → `2026-09-03.1`, live `APP_VERSION` in the
  served HTML matches.
- The date filter is live in the bundle: `.where("date", ">=",
  cutoffDate)` present (both `loadData()`'s original and the fixed
  refresh's).
- Main's prior live work survives: Studio Health tab bar exactly as
  before (3 live tabs — individualinsight/employeereview/dataquality
  — Project Health/Submission Log still held back in the comment;
  `logreliability` correctly absent — that branch is still separate,
  unmerged, not part of this ship), `distributeBudgetByTemplate`
  (budget-distribution) present, Data Quality's confidence-grade/
  contradiction functions present, reconciliation-flag logic present.

**CRITICAL check — Projects & Budget / Time Report totals, verified
against real production data, not assumed**: pulled the live
`timeLogs` (3,617 docs) + `projects` collections and directly compared
every budgeted phase's all-time total vs. its 90-day-scoped total.

**Honest result, not a simple "confirmed fine": of 99 budgeted phases
across 17 projects, 41 show *some* difference between all-time and
90-day totals (expected — most work is recent), and 4 phases flip
their over-budget status specifically because their over-budget hours
were logged more than 90 days ago with little-to-no recent activity**:

| Project | Phase | All-time | 90-day | Budget | All-time over? | 90-day over? |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BURDWAN RD | Material Selection & Coordination | 15.7h | 4.3h | 14h | yes | no |
| CHETLA SAMPLE FLAT | Material Selection & Coordination | 59.1h | 5.4h | 14h | yes | no |
| TWIN COTTAGE | Material Selection & Coordination | 24.6h | 14.0h | 14h | yes | no |
| BELGACHHIYA EXP CENTRE | Schematic Design | 243.2h | 0.0h | 203h | yes | no |

**This is NOT a new regression this fix introduces** — `loadData()`
has capped the shared `timeLogs` array to 90 days since V14.2.24, well
before this session's work, and `renderProjectsBudget()`/the
Dashboard's own "Phases Over Budget" stat already read that same
capped array on every fresh login, before Dashboard's 30s timer had
ever ticked once. These 4 phases were **already** silently
under-counted at the start of every session — the OLD unfiltered
refresh only accidentally "corrected" them back to all-time truth
*if and only if* Dashboard happened to stay open long enough for its
first 30s tick that session, an undocumented, expensive, and
inconsistent side effect. This fix removes that inconsistency rather
than causing it: the app's "Phases Over Budget" count is now always
exactly what `loadData()` and Time Report's own existing 90-day
disclaimer already promise, in every session, consistently — not
sometimes-wider depending on which pages happened to be visited.
Time Report itself is unaffected in the way that matters: it already
shows its own *"Data older than X is not loaded"* warning for any
custom range reaching past 90 days, so an admin pulling Belgachhiya's
or Chetla's full history there sees an honest warning, not a silent
wrong number — only the Dashboard stat card and Projects & Budget's
phase bars lack an equivalent disclaimer today.

Belgachhiya's Schematic Design phase shows 0.0h recent activity — almost
certainly a long-finished phase (the project has moved on), so its
over-budget flag quietly aging off a *current-activity* dashboard is
low-stakes. The other three (Burdwan Rd, Chetla, Twin Cottage) show
non-zero but below-budget recent Material Selection activity —
worth a look if the owner wants "Phases Over Budget" to stay
permanently all-time-accurate regardless of recent activity; that
would need its own small, separate, all-time-scoped query (cheap on
its own — the `projects` collection is small — since it wouldn't need
to run on a 30s timer, just once per Dashboard/Projects Budget open).
**Flagged for a decision, not treated as a blocker — not fixed in
this pass.**

**Status: merged, version-bumped, deployed, verified live. Read count
to be watched over the next few days against this fix (owner's own
next step, not something to check from here).**

## All-time project/phase budget tracking — built on branch, held for review, 2026-09-03

Fixes the exact regression the Dashboard cost fix's own FINDINGS entry
flagged and left for a decision: `phaseLoggedMins()`/
`projectLoggedMins()` read the shared `timeLogs` array, which is now
(correctly) 90-day-scoped for cost reasons — right for activity, wrong
for budgets, since a phase over budget 2 years ago is still over
budget today. Branch `feat-alltime-budget-tracking`, cut from `main`.

**The fix — a separate all-time data source, not a wider shared
array**: `loadAllTimeBudgetLogs()` queries only the projects that
actually have a budgeted phase — one `.where('projectId','==',id)`
query per budgeted project, in parallel (`Promise.all`, same pattern
used throughout this file). Not the whole `timeLogs` collection, not
even every project.

**Cost discipline — cached, not looped, proven structurally**:
`ensureAllTimeBudgetLogsLoaded()` is the only thing render functions
call. After the first successful load, every subsequent call — from
anywhere, however many times — fires **zero** network calls; this
isn't a coding convention, it's a hard `if (allTimeBudgetLogs !==
null) return` gate at the top of the loader, proven directly in the
fixture by calling it repeatedly against a call-counting mock and
asserting the query count never moves past the first round.
Concurrent callers before the first fetch resolves share one in-flight
promise (also proven directly — 3 concurrent calls fire 2 queries
total, not 6). The only way stale data ever updates is
`refreshAllTimeBudgetLogs()`, wired to an explicit "🔄 refresh accurate
budgets" link on Projects & Budget — never a timer, never automatic.
Confirmed via source-text fixture that none of this machinery appears
inside any `setInterval(...)` anywhere in the file.

**Scope kept precise, per instruction — only budget math widened**:
wired into exactly two call sites — `renderDashboard()`'s
`overBudgetCount` stat and `renderProjectsBudget()`'s project%/phase%
bars, both now reading `phaseLoggedMinsAllTime()`/
`projectLoggedMinsAllTime()`. Everything else is byte-for-byte
untouched: the Dashboard's 30s activity refresh (still date-scoped),
`fetchActiveTimersFromFirebase()`, `loadReconciliationQueue()`,
`renderLiveTimeLogFeed()` — all confirmed unmodified in the fixture.
A dormant, out-of-scope third call site was found and deliberately
left alone: the legacy Appraisal scoring function's "Project Budget
Discipline" component also calls `phaseLoggedMins()` — that feature is
already established as dormant (superseded by Employee Review,
retirement a separate follow-up), not part of what was asked, not
touched here.

**Verified against real production data — the 4 flagged phases,
recomputed the way `loadAllTimeBudgetLogs()` actually computes it**
(projectId-scoped, no date filter), pulled fresh, not asserted from
memory:

| Project | Phase | All-time hours | Budget | Over? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BELGACHHIYA EXP CENTRE | Schematic Design | 243.2h | 203h | **yes** |
| CHETLA SAMPLE FLAT | Material Selection & Coordination | 59.1h | 14h | **yes** |
| BURDWAN RD | Material Selection & Coordination | 15.7h | 14h | **yes** |

## Neha + Taskiya Sept 3 data fixed; new overlapping-orphan race scoped, 2026-09-04

Read-then-fix, both verified before any write.

**Neha's duplicate — confirmed, deleted, verified.** Fresh re-pull
showed the same 4-doc shape as the sweep. `mtl8l78v6fzd3g` (13:31–19:23)
starts *inside* the still-open window of the normal 11:01–14:02 entry
(that entry didn't close until 14:03 IST, per its own `updatedAt`) —
structurally can't be additional real work; the other three entries
form one unbroken, millisecond-continuous chain (each doc's
`sessionStartMs` lands within ~40ms of the prior doc's `ts`).
Freeze-checked (doc unchanged, her only live session was an unrelated
Sept 4 entry), deleted, `source:'server'` verified: doc gone, the
other 3 byte-identical to before, Sept 3 total **11.95h → 6.10h**.

**Taskiya's stuck session — understood, closed, verified.** Genuine
stale-tab artifact: last heartbeat 18:54 IST Sept 3, correctly flagged
`reconciliationNeeded:true` ~7 minutes later — the flagging logic
worked. **Corrected a premise along the way**: `finalize-stale.js`
never closes sessions at all (deliberate V16 "flag-don't-truncate"
design) — closing is `biometric-sync.js`'s job. Its close logic for
exactly this case (no prior session that day → fall back to own last
heartbeat) is pre-built and tested — its own comments cite *"the
Taskiya refinement (2026-08-25)"* by name. It didn't fire because
`vercel.json`'s cron carries no query string, so the automated run is
always `confirm=false` — computes the close, writes nothing. Same root
pattern as an earlier engagement finding (the gap-fill writer), now
confirmed on a second call site. Enrollment isn't the issue — empcode
`0025` is correctly mapped. Did not query the live eTimeOffice API
(no verified-current local credentials, and a bigger step than this
warranted) — flagged rather than guessed. Closed using the codebase's
own established fallback formula (own last heartbeat, no capping
needed at 483min): `inProgress:false`, `recovered:true`,
`estimatedClose:true`, `reconciliationNeeded:false`, `recoveredAt`,
desc extended — `endTime`/`durationMins` unchanged since the
heartbeat mechanism already kept them current. Freeze-checked, wrote,
`source:'server'` verified — diffed before/after, only the 6 intended
fields changed, nothing else.

**New race scoped (read-only, not built): two independent orphans,
overlapping reconstructed windows.**

- **Mechanism, confirmed**: `tryAutoCloseOrphan()` closes ONE orphan
  doc in place (same id) — it has no awareness of any OTHER orphan or
  already-closed session for that user/day. Two separate stale
  `inProgress:true` docs, each legitimately auto-closed on its own
  merits, can end up with reconstructed windows that overlap each
  other — nothing checks, so both get counted, double-counting real
  time.
- **Confirmed distinct from Glitch 3** (2026-08-26): Glitch 3 guards
  ONE session's self-report-submit racing against that SAME session
  already having been auto-closed (re-checks live state at submit
  time). This is two DIFFERENT original orphan docs, neither going
  through the self-report path at all — a different code path
  entirely, with no equivalent guard today.
- **Frequency — checked exhaustively, not estimated**: scanned all
  3,634 `timeLogs` docs / 1,003 person-day groups in history for two
  different-id "auto-closed on load" docs with overlapping windows.
  **Exactly one hit, ever: Neha's Sept 3.** A genuine one-off so far,
  not a recurring pattern — but the code path has no defense against
  it recurring.
- **Fix approach recommended**: when `tryAutoCloseOrphan()` is about
  to close an orphan, check its proposed `[startMs, hbMs]` window
  against every OTHER already-closed session for the same user+date
  (already in the in-memory `timeLogs` array — no new fetch). On
  overlap, don't silently auto-close it — fall through to the
  existing `stillNeedsBanner` path (defer to the human), matching the
  same fail-closed/flag-don't-fabricate philosophy already used
  throughout this race-fix family (V16, Glitch 5, biometric-sync's own
  `needsManualReview`). Truncating one side automatically was
  considered and set aside — deciding *which* overlapping
  reconstruction is the spurious one isn't always obvious (could
  legitimately be a multi-device double-login needing a human look),
  so defer rather than guess, consistent with precedent.
- **Fixture requirement noted for when this gets built**: two orphan
  docs, overlapping windows, both individually eligible for auto-close
  — prove today's code would silently double-close both (the bug,
  demonstrated not asserted), prove the fix detects the overlap and
  defers instead of double-writing, and prove a genuinely
  non-overlapping pair of orphans still both auto-close normally (no
  regression).

**Not built — scope only, as asked.**

## Known edge case, deliberately not fixed: overlapping-orphan double-count — decision logged, 2026-09-04

**Mechanism**: `tryAutoCloseOrphan()` closes one orphan in isolation,
with no awareness of any other orphan or already-closed session that
day. Two independently-legitimate auto-closes for the same person/day
can produce reconstructed windows that overlap each other — nothing
checks, so both get counted (double-count). **Distinct from Glitch 3**
(which guards ONE session's self-report-submit racing against that
SAME session already having been auto-closed) — this is two separate
original orphan docs, neither going through the self-report path at
all.

**Frequency**: checked exhaustively, not estimated — scanned all 3,634
`timeLogs` docs / 1,003 person-day groups in history for two
different-id "auto-closed on load" docs with overlapping windows.
**Exactly 1 occurrence, ever: Neha, 2026-09-03.** A genuine one-off.

**Decision (2026-09-04): NOT building the fix.** Too rare, on the
evidence so far, to justify touching the reconciliation-close path.
If it recurs: clean up manually — verify the overlapping doc is
genuinely spurious (not separate legitimate work), delete it, same
read-then-fix discipline used for Neha's case above. **Escalate to
actually building the fix only if this recurs / becomes a pattern**,
not preemptively.

**Fix, scoped and ready if escalated**: when auto-closing an orphan,
check its proposed window against every other already-closed session
for that user+date (already in the in-memory `timeLogs` array, no new
fetch needed); on overlap, don't guess which side is spurious — defer
to the existing manual-review banner instead of auto-closing, fail-
closed, consistent with this codebase's whole race-fix family (V16,
Glitch 5, biometric-sync's own `needsManualReview`). Fixture must
model the race directly: two orphan docs with overlapping windows,
both individually eligible for auto-close, proving (a) today's code
would silently double-close both, (b) the fix detects the overlap and
defers instead, (c) a genuinely non-overlapping pair of orphans still
both auto-close normally (no regression).

## Dry-run cron note — re-confirmed, relevant to a future cron-flip decision

`api/biometric-sync.js`'s EOD-close phase (`closeReconciliationNeededSessions()`)
computes the correct close for every flagged stale session every night
— but `vercel.json`'s cron entry carries no query string, so
`req.query.confirm` is always `undefined` → `confirm=false` on every
automated run. The function's own comment confirms this is deliberate:
*"writes are live behind the confirm=true query param (dry-run by
default)... confirm=true is deliberately NOT baked into the cron path
yet."* In practice: the cron computes and logs what it would close,
writes nothing, every night, for everyone. A stuck session (Taskiya's
Sept 3 case above is a direct example) does **not** get auto-closed by
the nightly cron — it sits flagged until someone closes it manually,
or the affected person's own next login triggers the client-side
`tryAutoCloseOrphan()` path instead.

This is by design today (a deliberate trust-building gate before
flipping automated writes on), not a bug — but it's the exact thing
that would need to change before any move toward an unattended,
monthly-or-less-frequent check-in model: as long as the cron stays
`confirm=false`, stuck sessions accumulate and wait for a human (or
the affected person's own return) rather than resolving themselves.
Relevant context for that future decision, not an action item now.

OUT HH:MM". **Reconciliation queue confirmed empty (0 items) right
after.**

**Discovery made mid-freeze-check, not asked for**: Taskiya's Sept 3
doc — closed once already (2026-09-04, see the fallback-close entry
above) — had reverted to `inProgress:true` and re-flagged
`reconciliationNeeded:true` **12 minutes after** that close, with a
fresh `lastHeartbeat`. Not touched until this pass; re-closed above.
Flagged as a real, previously-unknown mechanism gap, scoped below.

## Session-revival race — scoped, not built

**Mechanism, confirmed**: a server-side close (`inProgress:false` +
finalized fields) can be silently overwritten by a *later* client
heartbeat/resume write, if that client still holds local timer state
(`localStorage` / `currentSessionLogId`) pointing at the same doc id —
the client's heartbeat path writes `inProgress:true` + a fresh
heartbeat unconditionally, with no check for "has server state moved
since I last knew about this doc." Taskiya's Sept 3: closed 11:01:07
IST, revived by a heartbeat at 11:12:47 IST — 12 minutes later, almost
certainly her device waking from sleep/reconnect and resuming what it
still believed was its own live session.

**Distinct from the C/D clobber, confirmed**: C/D was an
*unconditional full-collection* rewrite (every doc, every cycle,
blind to whether anything changed) — fixed by diff-based writes. This
is a *single targeted doc write* from a client that has a legitimate
(if stale) claim to that specific doc — diff-based writes don't touch
this at all, since the client genuinely intends to write to that one
doc; the gap is that it never re-validates the doc's current server
state (specifically: has it already been closed) before doing so.
Same *family* (stale client-held state overwriting a server
correction, trusted blindly) but a different mechanism, different
blast radius, and a different fix.

**Frequency — scanned exhaustively, with an honest limit stated**:
searched all 3,660 `timeLogs` docs for the revival signature
(`inProgress:true` right now, but also carrying `recovered:true` /
`recoveredAt` / any `estimatedClose` value — evidence of a prior
close). **Zero other instances found** — Taskiya's Sept 3 case (now
re-closed) was the only one. Honest caveat: this can only detect a
revival still sitting reopened at scan time. A revival that got
silently closed again before anyone looked would leave no trace —
Firestore keeps no document history here, so "zero *currently
detectable* instances, one *directly observed* instance" is the
precise claim, not proof this has never happened before unnoticed.

**Fix direction, scoped, not built**: before a heartbeat/resume write
proceeds (specifically at the `freeze`/`resume` lifecycle wake point,
mirroring the hooks already in the file), the client should re-verify
via a fresh `get({source:'server'})` on its own remembered doc id that
the session is still genuinely open before writing another heartbeat
to it. If the server shows `inProgress:false` already, the client
should discard its stale local timer state instead of overwriting the
close — the exact same "re-check live server state before writing,
never trust what was true when this process started" principle Glitch
3 already established for the self-report-submit path
(`orphanSaveBtn`'s handler). A client-side guard, consistent with this
app's whole architecture (no backend authority beyond Firestore
itself).

**Fixture requirement for when this gets built**: (a) close a doc
server-side, (b) simulate a stale client heartbeat/resume attempt with
no re-check — prove today's code lets it silently overwrite the close
(the bug, demonstrated), (c) with the fix, prove the same attempt
re-checks server state, finds it already closed, and discards its
stale local session instead of writing, (d) prove a genuinely-still-
open session (never closed) still resumes/heartbeats normally — no
regression for the common, correct case.

**Not built — scope only, as asked. Decision on whether to build is
the owner's, informed by the frequency finding above.**

---

## Session-revival race: FIX SHIPPED (2026-09-06, APP_VERSION 2026-09-06.1)

Built, reviewed, and merged. This closes the exact gap scoped above —
same mechanism, same root cause, no parallel/duplicate system.

**Root cause, pinned precisely**: `awaitingWakeVerification` was only
ever *set* by `checkTimerHealth()`, but every wake entry point (the
`visibilitychange` "tab visible again" handler, both `online`
listeners, `focus`, and the reliable tick worker's own `onmessage`)
called `heartbeatSession()`/`finalizeCurrentSession()` **before**
`checkTimerHealth()` ran in that same handler — deliberately, per
V14.3.21's own comment (`reArmHeartbeat()` first, so `checkTimerHealth()`
reports "healthy" a moment sooner). That ordering meant the flag could
still read `false` on the very first write attempt after a genuine
long silence, letting `heartbeatSession()`'s existing V16 clobber guard
be bypassed exactly once per wake — enough to revive an already-closed
doc. A fully-suspended device (not just backgrounded) never ticks at
all until the wake instant, so there was no earlier tick that could
have tripped the flag in time.

**Fix**: new `tripWakeVerificationIfStale()` computes the same
staleness condition `checkTimerHealth()` already uses, directly and
self-sufficiently (no dependency on load-bearing call order), sets the
*same* flag, and calls the *same*, unmodified `verifySessionStillOpenOnWake()`.
Both `heartbeatSession()` and `finalizeCurrentSession()` — the two
chokepoints every wake path already funnels through before writing —
now gate on its return value instead of the bare flag. Zero listener
changes; the hole is closed at the one place all five paths already
converge, not by reordering five separate event handlers.

**Second bug found via fixture, not assumed correct**: the "genuinely
still open" branch of `verifySessionStillOpenOnWake()` never refreshed
`lastHeartbeatSuccessAt`. Without that one-line fix, a perfectly
healthy session that ever triggered verification would compute the
same stale gap again on the very next check and re-trip forever,
silently blocking all future heartbeats — this would have shipped as
a new, worse bug (permanently stuck timers) if the fixture hadn't
caught it (`calls.upserts.length` stalled at 0 on the second
`heartbeatSession()` call in the original build). Fixed with
`lastHeartbeatSuccessAt = Date.now();` in that branch; proven not to
regress via 3 consecutive `heartbeatSession()` calls all writing
normally after a triggered verification.

**Fail-safe direction, unchanged**: mirrors the Glitch 3 asymmetry —
only an affirmative `inProgress===false` from the server discards the
local session; a read failure/ambiguous result fails open (resumes
unverified, session preserved). `verifySessionStillOpenOnWake()`'s
try/catch/fail-open block was not touched.

**Isolation**: diff touches exactly 4 hunks, all inside
`verifySessionStillOpenOnWake()` / the new `tripWakeVerificationIfStale()`
/ the two guard lines in `heartbeatSession()` and
`finalizeCurrentSession()`. Confirmed via
`git diff main -- index.html | grep -E "saveData|computeCollectionDiffs|syncChangedDocs|diffMode|backstopFullSave|\.batch\(|commitInChunks"`
— zero matches, both pre-merge (branch vs. main) and post-merge
(merged main vs. pre-merge main).

**Testing**: `test/fix-session-revival-race.test.js` (new, 35/35) —
stale-client-revival-blocked for both write functions, the loop-
prevention "KEY SAFETY" fixture (3 consecutive heartbeats write
normally after a verified-open resume), fail-open on read failure,
no-double-trigger, and healthy/no-silence sessions untouched. Updated
`test/fix-reconciliation-flag-clear-gaps.test.js` (27/27, unchanged
assertions) for the new dependency. Full regression: 28/28 test files
green, run twice — once on the feature branch, once again on the
actual merged `main` state before push.

**Ship**: built on `fix-session-revival-race` (commit `a7f0489`),
merged into `main` as `fb0cbf0` (clean merge, no conflicts — `main`
had not moved since the branch was cut), version bumped to
`2026-09-06.1` as `e97d347`, pushed to `origin/main`.

**Post-deploy verification against the live bundle**
(`https://squareoffice.vercel.app/`), fetched fresh after push:
- `version.json` and `APP_VERSION` both read `2026-09-06.1`.
- `tripWakeVerificationIfStale` present in the live bundle.
- `verifySessionStillOpenOnWake()`'s fail-open catch and
  `checkTimerHealth()` confirmed unmodified beyond the one intended
  loop-prevention line.
- `diffMode` still defaults to `'primary'` (V16.5/Stage D untouched).
- Main's prior work survives intact: all 4 Studio Health tabs present
  (Submission Log, Appraisal, Data Quality, Log Reliability — legacy
  "Employee Review"/old Appraisal only remain as internal
  code-comment/id history, not user-facing labels), all-time budget
  tracking present, the Dashboard 30s-refresh date-scope cost fix
  (V19) present, legacy Appraisal removal intact.

## Phase-typology auto-sync: FIX SHIPPED (2026-09-06, APP_VERSION 2026-09-06.2)

Built, reviewed, and merged. This is the forward-fix scoped earlier in
this document ("phase-typology forward-fix scope") — AUTO-SYNC, the
gentlest of the three ranked options: when someone picks a typology,
auto-set phase to match, on both surfaces.

**What it does**: new `autoSyncPhaseFromTypology(phaseSelectEl, typologyValue)`
reuses the existing `TYPOLOGY_TO_PHASE` table (one definition, not
duplicated) to derive phase from typology at LOG TIME. Wired to four
change listeners: `logTypology` -> `logPhase` (Log Time modal, phase
options scoped to that project's own budgeted phases) and
`planTypology` -> `planPhase` (Planner's Assign Work modal, always all
6 canonical phases). Also re-applied from the `logProject`/`planProject`
change handlers, since picking a project after typology repopulates
the phase `<select>` and would otherwise wipe the already-implied
value — ordering doesn't matter on either modal.

**Graceful by design, not a strict enforcement**: only sets the value
when the implied phase is actually one of the select's current
options. When it isn't (the project isn't budgeted for that phase —
~44% of historical contradictions were exactly this case, per the
original scope), phase is left exactly as the user had it: no error,
no popup, nothing blocked. Zero-friction by construction — a plain
synchronous change-event side effect, nothing async, nothing that can
slow down or interrupt logging.

**Isolation**: zero write-path changes. `saveLog()`/`savePlanBtn` still
read `logPhase`/`logTypology`/`planPhase`/`planTypology` `.value` the
same way, unchanged shape — proven by source-text fixture assertions,
not just left untested. Confirmed via
`git diff <pre-merge main> -- index.html | grep -E "saveData|computeCollectionDiffs|syncChangedDocs|diffMode|backstopFullSave|\.batch\(|commitInChunks|heartbeatSession|finalizeCurrentSession|verifySessionStillOpenOnWake|checkTimerHealth|tripWakeVerificationIfStale"`
— zero matches, both pre-merge and on the final merged `main`. The
change touches only the four modal change-listeners and the one new
pure function; no Firestore `.set(`/`.update(`/`.add(`/`.delete(`/`.batch(`
call anywhere in the new code.

**Testing**: `test/feat-phase-typology-autosync.test.js` (19/19) —
both surfaces, graceful-fallback-when-phase-not-budgeted, typology-
before-project and project-before-typology ordering on both modals,
source-text checks (zero write calls in the new code, `TYPOLOGY_TO_PHASE`
defined exactly once and genuinely reused), and confirmation that
`saveLog()`/`savePlanBtn` are otherwise unchanged. Full regression:
29/29 test files green (28 prior + this one), run on the branch after
reconciling with `main`'s moved-forward state, and again on the final
merged `main` before push.

**Reconcile**: `main` had moved since this branch was cut (the
session-revival-race fix + its `2026-09-06.1` version bump shipped in
the interim). Dry-run merge of `main` into this branch was clean —
zero conflicts, `index.html` auto-merged — confirmed both features'
functions (`autoSyncPhaseFromTypology` and `tripWakeVerificationIfStale`)
present exactly once each in the reconciled file before committing.

**Ship**: built on `feat-phase-typology-autosync` (commit `e456c91`),
reconciled with `main` (`4eabff9`), version bumped to `2026-09-06.2`
(`828e2e6`), merged into `main` as `e2a85ca`, pushed to `origin/main`.

**Post-deploy verification against the live bundle**
(`https://squareoffice.vercel.app/`), fetched fresh after push:
- `version.json` and `APP_VERSION` both read `2026-09-06.2`.
- `autoSyncPhaseFromTypology` present; both `logTypology` and
  `planTypology` change listeners present.
- `diffMode` still defaults to `'primary'`, unchanged.
- The session-revival-race fix (`tripWakeVerificationIfStale`) survived

- Main's other prior work survives: all 4 Studio Health tabs

## HR policy externalised to Firestore (2026-09-10) — content prerequisite for the HR chatbot

**What:** the SQUARE HR policy, previously written down nowhere in OMS
(rules were only *encoded in app logic*, never readable as text), is now
a structured Firestore doc: `companyData/hrPolicies`.

**Where & why there:** a SIBLING document to `companyData/squareDB`, not
a field inside it. `squareDB` is on the app's constantly-written save
path (backstop saves, diffMode sync, the hardened save pipeline); policy
text changes rarely and has nothing to do with that path. A sibling doc
in the same collection needs no new Firestore rules and no new
read/write pattern — it is the exact shape of data the app already
trusts — while staying clear of the save machinery. Verified after the
write: `squareDB` still 15 fields, no `hrPolicies` field jammed in,
`holidays` array still present.

**Fields (13):** `casualLeave`, `medicalLeave`, `attendancePolicy`,
`sandwichRule`, `officeTiming`, `halfDay`, `latePolicy`, `wfh`,
`newJoiners`, `extraLeave`, `additionalHours`, `financialYear`,
`lastUpdated` (ISO string). Plain text, one topic per field — so the
future chatbot context assembler just concatenates non-empty fields, a
future admin editor is one `<textarea>` per field, and each field is
independently testable ("ask about X while X is empty → must say 'ask
HR', not guess").

**How written:** `scripts/seed-hr-policies.mjs` (committed on branch
`feat-hr-policies-doc`, NOT merged). Freeze-checks (refuses to run if the
doc already exists, so a re-run can never clobber later admin edits),
writes with `setDoc`, verifies with a `getDocFromServer` re-read + a
field-by-field value comparison. Independent second server read
confirmed 13 fields and `squareDB` untouched.

**NOT done (deliberately):** the chatbot is not wired to this doc. No
`index.html` code reads or writes `hrPolicies` yet. This turn was
capture-the-policy-text only.

**Related content gap found during verification:** `companyData/squareDB.holidays`
is currently an **empty array** (0 entries). The chatbot scope assumed
"the holiday list already lives in `companyData.holidays` — reuse that,"
but there is nothing there to reuse right now. "When's the next holiday"
answers have no data source until that array is populated (admin task,
separate from this).
