## CODE-ALIGNMENT BACKLOG: where OMS code is BEHIND or CONTRADICTS the externalised policy (2026-09-10)

Systematic-fix list. None of these are being fixed now — this is the
inventory. Ordered (a)–(g) as the owner listed them.

### (a) Auto-deduction CONTRADICTS `attendancePolicy` — highest priority

**Policy says:** "Leave/attendance NOT auto-deducted solely for not
logging into OMS. … Employees notified of any OMS login error. No
auto-deduction until attendance verified." Where both OMS and biometric
are unavailable, attendance is reviewed *manually*.

**Code does the opposite** — `runAttendanceAutoDeduction()`
([index.html:5580](index.html) onward, feature V14.2.36): on admin
login it scans every past working day since the last check and, for any
OMS-active staff member with **zero productive minutes and no approved
leave**, **auto-deducts 1 casual day** — writing a
`leaveRequests` record with `autoDeducted: true` and decrementing
`casLeft`. It does this:
  - **silently** — no notification to the employee (policy requires one),
  - with **no biometric cross-check** — it only looks at OMS `timeLogs`
    productive minutes; a login/system failure with the person
    physically present and on the biometric device is indistinguishable
    from a real absence to this code (policy requires the manual review
    in exactly that case),
  - with **no "verified" gate** — the deduction is immediate, not held
    pending confirmation (policy: "No auto-deduction until attendance
    verified").

There is also a downstream effect: `runAttendanceAutoDeduction()` feeds
the forward-sandwich logic ([index.html:5670](index.html)–5678), so a
single unverified zero-minute day can auto-deduct itself **plus**
trailing closed days.

**Fix direction (not built):** gate the deduction behind a biometric
check (biometric-sync already maps `EMPCODE_TO_USERID` and reads DPR
IN/OUT), only deduct when *both* sources agree on absence, emit an
employee notification on any OMS login-error day, and either hold
deductions in a "pending verification" state or drop the auto-path
entirely in favour of the manual review the policy describes. This is
the biggest single contradiction and the one most likely to produce a
wrong, silent leave deduction today.

### (b) CL accrual "1.25/quarter" — is it implemented, or flat-seeded?

**Policy says:** "5 CL/year, accrued 1.25/quarter, unused carries
forward. Financial year Apr 1–Mar 31."

**Code:** `casLeft` is **flat-seeded at 5** on every user
([index.html:1459](index.html) seed data, [index.html:8772](index.html)
new-user creation) and only ever *decremented* (leave taken /
auto-deduction) or corrected back up toward the cap of 5
([index.html:8632](index.html) `Math.min(5, …)`). **No quarterly
accrual code exists** — nobody starts a financial year at 1.25 and
climbs. Effectively the current behaviour is "5 available immediately,"
which over-grants CL to anyone in their first three quarters and does
not model carry-forward at all (the cap is a hard 5, so carried-forward
CL above 5 would be silently clamped).

**Fix direction (not built):** a quarterly accrual step (cron or
admin-login sweep, like the attendance check) that adds 1.25 at the
start of each FY quarter, raises/removes the hard cap of 5 so unused CL
can carry forward, and back-fills correctly for existing balances.

### (c) April-1 annual reset — implemented?

**Policy says:** financial year is Apr 1 – Mar 31; ML is "12 ML/year";
CL accrues per-quarter within the FY.

**Code:** **no annual reset exists.** `medLeft` (seeded 12) and `casLeft`
(seeded 5) are running balances that never reset. Grepped for
reset/new-year/annual logic — nothing. ML in particular: a person who
used all 12 ML last FY does not get 12 back on Apr 1; their `medLeft`
just stays wherever it landed. (Note: `medLeft` has an odd correction
cap of **24**, not 12, at [index.html:1872](index.html) — worth
understanding why before building the reset.)

**Fix direction (not built):** an FY-rollover step that, on/after Apr 1,
resets `medLeft` to 12, applies the CL carry-forward + first quarterly
accrual, and records that the rollover ran (so it fires once per FY, not
once per admin login). Needs a stored `lastFYRollover` marker in
`companyData` like `lastAttendanceCheck` already is.

### (d) Late / half-day computation from biometric — not implemented

**Policy says:** "2:30 PM is the half-day benchmark. Reporting at/after
it may be treated as half-day." "Reporting after 11:00 AM = late."

**Code:** OMS has **no concept of a reporting/arrival time at all.**
`timeLogs` records productive minutes per session, not a first-punch
clock time. `isHalf` is a manual checkbox on the leave form
([index.html:8169](index.html)), set by a human, with no time logic
behind it. There is no code that looks at when someone first arrived and
compares it to 11:00 or 14:30.

**Fix direction (not built):** biometric-sync already has DPR IN times
per employee per day. A new step could read first-IN, compare against
11:00 (late) and 14:30 (half-day), and record a per-day arrival status.
This is the data foundation (d)/(e) both need.

### (e) Late-coming progressive tracking — not implemented

**Policy says:** "7 consecutive late days → discussion. 12 late
instances/month → warning. Beyond that, every 3 late instances = one
half-day."

**Code:** nothing. No late-instance counter, no consecutive-late
tracker, no monthly rollup, no "every 3 beyond 12 = half-day"
conversion. (Compare: the 3-consecutive-months-unplanned-absence warning
at [index.html:5776](index.html) is the *only* progressive-tracking
pattern that exists today — a model for how this could be built.)

**Fix direction (not built):** on top of (d)'s per-day arrival status:
a monthly aggregation that counts late instances, detects the 7-in-a-row
and 12-in-a-month thresholds, emits the discussion/warning flags, and
converts every 3rd late instance beyond 12 into a half-day deduction
against leave.

### (f) WFH feature — not implemented

**Policy says:** "1 WFH day/month per eligible employee. Apply + approve
≥1 day in advance. Max 2 employees WFH same day, first-come-first-served."

**Code:** **no WFH concept exists** — no field, no request type, no
approval flow, no per-day capacity check. `leaveRequests` has
`type: 'casual' | 'medical'` only.

**Fix direction (not built):** a third request type (or a parallel
`wfhRequests` collection), a monthly-quota check per employee, an
advance-notice validation (≥1 day), a same-day capacity gate (max 2,
FCFS), and eligibility (ties to (g)/newJoiners: no WFH in first 3
months). Non-trivial — this is a feature, not a fix.

### (g) Extra-leave pro-rata + additional-hours encashment — not implemented

**Policy says:** "After CL+ML exhausted: first 3 extra leaves at 1.0
pro-rata, next 2 (up to 5) at 1.3, beyond 5 at 1.5." And: "Extra hours
may be adjusted against leave at year-end: carried forward or encashed."

**Code:** when `casLeft`/`medLeft` hit 0 they are clamped at 0
([index.html:8608](index.html) `Math.max(0, …)`) — the app just shows a
negative-styled bar ([index.html:8426](index.html) colour logic handles
`casLeft < 0`) but there is **no pro-rata multiplier** (1.0 / 1.3 / 1.5
tiers) applied to leave taken past the entitlement, and **no
additional-hours ledger** at all — nothing tracks overtime minutes for
year-end adjustment/encashment. `timeLogs` has the raw minutes but
nothing aggregates "hours worked beyond expected" per person per FY.

**Fix direction (not built):** an extra-leave calculator that applies
the tiered multiplier once entitlement is exhausted (affects payroll,
so needs owner sign-off on exact mechanics), and a per-FY
additional-hours accumulator (expected vs. actual minutes) feeding a
year-end carry-forward/encashment decision.

### New joiners (mentioned in policy, not in the owner's (a)–(g) list) — not implemented

**Policy says:** "No leave in first 1.5 months. No WFH in first 3
months." **Code:** no `joinDate`/`startDate` field on users, so no way
to compute tenure, so neither restriction is enforced. A prerequisite
for (f) and for any tenure-based rule. Flagging so it's not lost.

## Holiday population (2026 list) — read-only analysis, 2026-09-10

Task: populate the empty `companyData/squareDB.holidays` array with the
15-holiday 2026 list, then check whether the empty array had already
caused wrong auto-deductions on any of those dates.

### The write is currently BLOCKED (not done)

`squareDB` is on the app's live save path. Freeze-check #1 (during
analysis) hashed all non-`holidays` fields:
`d2518247…83432e`. Freeze-check #2 (immediately pre-write, minutes
later) got a **different hash** `404adea8…f94f7a` — `squareDB` had been
re-saved in between by an active OMS tab: `savedBy: "Tasmin"`,
`timestamp: 2026-09-10T14:06:20.893Z`. The write script **aborted before
writing** (holidays confirmed still `[]`, nothing changed).

**Root cause it can't just be forced:** `holidays` is part of the
`dataToSave` payload in `saveData()` ([index.html:3233](index.html)),
which every open tab writes to `squareDB` every 30s when
`hasUnsavedChanges` is true ([index.html:3227](index.html),
[index.html:3244](index.html)) — using that tab's *in-memory* `holidays`
array, which is `[]` for anyone who loaded before holidays existed. So
any write of 15 holidays gets clobbered back to `[]` within ~30s of the
next staff edit from a stale tab.

**Fix options (owner's call):**
1. Brief all-staff OMS pause (~2 min quiet + everyone closes/reloads),
   then land + verify.
2. Small code change (own branch): remove `holidays` from `dataToSave`
   at [index.html:3233](index.html) so periodic saves stop touching it —
   it becomes granular-write-only through the `canManageHolidays()`
   UI, exactly the carve-out already done for `leaveRequests`
   (V14.2.6) and `casLeft`/`medLeft` (V14.2.40c) for the identical
   last-write-wins reason. Then the data write is safe anytime.
3. Land at a genuinely quiet time (EOD) with re-run + verify.

### Holiday-date auto-deductions that are ERRORS and should be reversed

Scanned all 307 `leaveRequests` (271 with `autoDeducted:true`) for any
whose date span touches one of the 15 holiday dates.

**Result: 9 erroneous auto-deductions, ALL on 2026-08-15 (Independence
Day).** Aug 15 2026 is a Saturday — specifically the *3rd* Saturday, so
`is24Sat()` returns false and the code treated it as a normal working
day. With `holidays[]` empty, `isHoliday('2026-08-15')` was also false,
so the whole office (every OMS-active staff member) showed "no timelog
activity" on a day they were correctly off, and each was auto-deducted.

Every record is `startDate 2026-08-15 → endDate 2026-08-16, days: 2,
reason "Absent (no timelog activity)", approvedBy System`. The Aug 16
(Sunday) day is **forward-sandwich** ([index.html:5675](index.html)) —
it was only pulled in *because* Aug 15 was wrongly flagged. Correct a
holiday and the anchor absence disappears, so the sandwich day
disappears too: **each of the 9 is a full 2.0-day reversal, not 1.**

| leaveRequests docId | person | userId | bank | reverse |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `mswrmbgpxj6ukg` | Angana | `mmblekn4yim` | ML | +2.0 ML |
| `mswrmbki83tg38` | Ridhi | `mmblf4s45us` | CL | +2.0 CL |
| `mswrmbp40s23ct` | Souvik | `mmllimboc39` | CL | +2.0 CL |
| `mswrmbsmnrcacu` | Suravi | `mmllj7ovszm` | ML | +2.0 ML |
| `mswrmgh7hvmqvo` | Tasmin | `mmllonwbwgp` | CL | +2.0 CL |
| `mswrmjg44tjkkj` | Sarbani | `mmllp3z2o68` | CL | +2.0 CL (see caveat) |
| `mswrmk3ebhdeaa` | Rai | `mmllpgkndzw` | CL | +2.0 CL |
| `mswrmkjwt8hl40` | Neha | `mmllpsq6rih` | CL | +2.0 CL |
| `mswrmkpt6d92mu` | Taskiya | `mr21gc49q81zlv` | ML | +2.0 ML |

Totals: **6.0 ML-days** (Angana, Suravi, Taskiya) + **12.0 CL-days**
(other six) wrongly deducted. The 9 `leaveRequests` docs themselves
should also be deleted / voided, not just the balances — otherwise any
future balance recompute re-applies them.

**No errors on the other 14 holidays.** Jan 1, Jan 23, Jan 26, Mar 3,
May 1 (all past, all non-weekend) had *zero* auto-deductions recorded
for anyone — the attendance sweep's `lastAttendanceCheck` progression
never scanned back that far. Oct 2 – Dec 31 are all future — the sweep
only processes up to "yesterday", so populating `holidays` now
**prevents** those from ever generating a wrong deduction.

### NEW critical finding: `squareDB.users` vs the `users` collection have diverged badly

While pulling affected balances, found the two user stores are far
apart. The app loads `users` from the **`users` collection** as the
single source of truth (V14.3.2, [index.html:3288](index.html)); the
`squareDB.users` array is a **legacy field nothing reads anymore** —
though the auto-deduction still *writes* to it (stale V14.2.48 comment
at [index.html:5737](index.html)), which is why it drifted.

| person | `users` collection (ML/CL) — **authoritative** | `squareDB.users` (ML/CL) — legacy/dead |
|---|---|---|
| Angana | 4 / 3.5 | 7 / 4 |
| Ridhi | 0 / −2 | 3 / 5 |
| Souvik | 0 / −7 | 0 / 3 |
| Suravi | 2 / 0 | 6 / 2 |
| Tasmin | 0 / 0 | 1 / 5 |
| Sarbani | **NOT IN COLLECTION** | 4 / 2 |
| Rai | 0 / −10 | 0 / 0 |
| Neha | 0 / −1 | 5 / −3 |
| Taskiya | 2 / 5 | 11 / 5 |

Implications:
- **Manual leave-bank correction must use the `users` collection
  values**, not `squareDB.users`, and not the dashboard if the
  dashboard is ever fed from the array.
- **Sarbani has no `users`-collection doc** — the app cannot load her as
  a user at all right now. Departed employee, deleted doc, or
  array-only add that never synced. Her holiday-error deduction is real
  but there is no authoritative record to credit it back to — needs a
  decision (recreate her user doc, or write off).
- Several post-reversal CL balances stay deeply negative (Souvik −5,
  Rai −8) — that is the separate auto-deduction over-aggression problem
  (backlog item **(a)** above), not a holiday error.
- The dead `squareDB.users` write in the deduction path should be
  removed (code-alignment backlog, minor).

## Ask Square: BUILT, awaiting adversarial smoke test (2026-09-11)

OMS's first LLM integration. Built on `feat-ask-square-hr-chatbot`
(`dc84af9`) per the architecture scoped earlier this file
("HR chatbot for OMS" / "policy-content structure" sections above).
**Not merged yet** — the adversarial smoke-test gate (6 scenarios against
the real Anthropic API) has to run and pass first; this repo's owner is
providing `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` for that.

**Shipped in this commit:**
- `api/ask-square.js` — server-side endpoint. Own-data hard filter (4
  reads, all parameterized by the caller's own `userId` or public with no
  identity dimension). Grounded facts via pure functions
  (`countDistinctDates`, `earliestHolidayOnOrAfter`, `buildPolicyFacts`
  with an explicit `NOT_DOCUMENTED` marker per empty/missing policy
  field). Rate limit: `askSquareUsage/{userId}_{date}`, transaction,
  20/day default (`ASK_SQUARE_DAILY_CAP` env-overridable). Model:
  `claude-haiku-4-5` (`ASK_SQUARE_MODEL` env-overridable — the escape
  hatch to `claude-sonnet-5` if the smoke test shows weak instruction
  following).
- `index.html` — new "Ask Square" tab, visible to **everyone** (confirmed
  decision: own-data-only makes it exactly as safe for staff as admins).
  Loading spinner, ~20s `AbortController` timeout, friendly fallback on
  every error path, answer rendered via `.textContent` (never
  `.innerHTML`). `APP_VERSION` → `2026-09-11.2`.
- `package.json` — added `@anthropic-ai/sdk`.
- `scripts/ask-square-smoke-test.js` — the pre-ship gate script itself,
  reusing the real shipped `buildSystemPrompt()`/`buildUserMessage()`/
  `askAnthropic()` (not a reimplementation) against 6 adversarial
  scenarios: salary question, cross-person balance, empty policy field,
  off-topic question, "make up a number" nudge, and a real leave-balance
  query that must include the caveat verbatim. Deliberately NOT in
  `test/` — it costs real money and needs a real key, so it's a manual,
  once-before-ship gate, not part of automated regression.

**Testing:** `test/feat-ask-square.test.js` (43/43) proves the hard
filter against a mock store holding a SECOND user's data (name, balances,
even a fake salary field) and asserts zero of it reaches the Anthropic
payload for a different requester; proves the rate limit blocks the
Anthropic call entirely once over cap; proves every policy field falls
back to `NOT_DOCUMENTED` when empty, including `leaveApplicationProcess`
(flagged below). `test/feat-ask-square-client.test.js` (29/29) proves the
client only ever sends `{ question, userId: currentUser.id }`, times out
at ~20s, and never renders raw error text. Full regression: 32/32 test
files green. Isolation: zero touches to
saveData/diffMode/heartbeat/revival-race/auto-sync machinery.

**Content gap surfaced during the build:** the "how do I apply for
leave?" answer was scoped to come from an `hrPolicies.leaveApplicationProcess`
field — but that field was never actually dictated when the policy was
externalized (see "HR policy externalised to Firestore" above) and does
not exist in the live doc. The code is ready for it (falls back to
`NOT_DOCUMENTED` → "ask HR" today, and will pick the field up
automatically the moment it's added), but until the owner writes that
content, Ask Square cannot answer this specific listed v1 question with
real policy text.

**Next:** owner sets `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`; smoke test runs once, transcript
reviewed; merge only after it passes (or after switching to
`claude-sonnet-5` / tightening the prompt, per the ship gate).

## PIVOT (2026-09-12): HR Info page instead of the AI chatbot

Before the smoke-test gate above ever ran, the owner changed direction:
no AI, no API key, no per-use cost. Same information, same own-data
safety, presented as a plain clickable page instead of a chat interface.
Built on the same branch (renamed `feat-ask-square-hr-chatbot` →
`feat-hr-info-page`), commit `e68844f`.

**Removed entirely:** `api/ask-square.js` (the endpoint + Anthropic
call), the `@anthropic-ai/sdk` dependency, `scripts/ask-square-smoke-test.js`
(moot with no model to test), the two chatbot fixtures, and the 20/day
rate limit (no external per-use cost left to cap). OMS is back to having
made **zero** LLM API calls in production — the "first LLM integration"
milestone above did not ship.

**Reused, exactly as scoped for the chatbot:** the grounded-computation
logic. `countDistinctDates()` and the holiday-lookup logic (extended into
`upcomingHolidays()`, showing the whole list with the next one
highlighted, not just the first) carried over unchanged from
`api/ask-square.js` before it was deleted. The `NOT_DOCUMENTED` /
"ask HR" fallback discipline for empty policy fields carried over as
`HR_INFO_NOT_DOCUMENTED` ("contact HR"). Own-data scoping carried over
as the same client-side own-`userId`-only filtering pattern
`renderLeaves()` already uses elsewhere in this app — not a new security
mechanism, since none was needed for a page that only ever reads data
the app already trusts the client with.

**New, pure client-side, no server component:** `fetchHrPolicies()` --
an on-demand `source:'server'` read of `companyData/hrPolicies`, fetched
fresh every time the HR Info tab opens (same discipline as Studio
Health's own on-demand analytics reads -- "don't trust a stale
snapshot"). `renderHrInfo()` -- 6 sections: My Attendance (own
`timeLogs` only), My Leave Balance (own `medLeft`/`casLeft`, with the
reconciliation caveat now baked into the page as **static markup**,
not something a model could ever omit), Leave Policy
(casual/medical/sandwich/extra-leave), Holidays (upcoming, next one
highlighted), Office Timing/Late/Half-Day/WFH, How to Apply.

**Content gap carried over unchanged:** `leaveApplicationProcess` is
still not a real field in `companyData/hrPolicies` (never dictated when
the policy was externalized). The How to Apply section correctly shows
"Not documented yet — contact HR." today and will pick up the real text
automatically the moment the owner adds that field -- same honest
behavior as the chatbot version would have had, just without an LLM in
the loop to phrase the gap.

**Not shown on this v1 page** (in scope for the chatbot's `hrPolicies`
read but not requested in the 6-section spec): `attendancePolicy`,
`newJoiners`, `additionalHours`, `financialYear`. All four still exist
in the doc and are readable by `fetchHrPolicies()` — they're just not
wired into any section yet. Flagging in case the owner wants a 7th
section or wants these folded into an existing one later.

**Testing:** `test/feat-hr-info-page.test.js` (35/35) — own-data scoping
proven against a store holding a SECOND user's timeLogs, balance, and a
fake salary field (zero mention of any of it across all 6 rendered
sections), pure-function edge cases (zero-duration logs excluded,
holiday-on-today included, past holidays excluded), contact-HR fallback
for missing/empty policy fields including `leaveApplicationProcess`,
the caveat confirmed present unconditionally in static markup, nav tab
confirmed visible to everyone, and confirmation the AI-chatbot approach
is genuinely gone (no `/api/ask-square` reference, no orphaned
identifiers). Full regression: 31/31 test files green. Isolation
confirmed: zero touches to
`saveData`/`diffMode`/heartbeat/revival-race/auto-sync machinery.

**Rendered and visually verified** via a live extracted-code demo (same
technique as the phase-typology auto-sync demo): all 6 sections render
correctly against the real, currently-populated `hrPolicies` text and
the real 2026 holiday list — Gandhi Birthday (Oct 2) correctly
highlighted as "next up" from today (Sept 12), all past 2026 holidays
correctly excluded, the reconciliation caveat visible under the balance
card, and "How to Apply" honestly showing "contact HR" since that
content doesn't exist yet.

Client change → `APP_VERSION` `2026-09-12.1`. Not merged — held for
review.

## Late policy finalized in companyData/hrPolicies (2026-09-12)

Granular `.update({ latePolicy })` to `companyData/hrPolicies` (off the
save path, safe). Freeze-checked, written, `source:'server'` verified:
new text stored exactly, all 12 other fields byte-identical (SHA256
matched before/after), field count unchanged at 13.

**New text:** "Reporting after 11:00 AM is considered late, except where
biometric shows the employee worked past 8:00 PM the previous working
day. Late instances are counted as a total per month (not consecutive).
3 late instances in a month → an initial discussion. 5 late instances
in a month → a formal warning. Beyond 5, every 3 further late instances
= one half-day deduction."

Replaces the earlier draft ("7 consecutive late days → discussion; 12
late instances/month → warning"). Key differences: instances now count
**per month in total**, not consecutively; thresholds tightened to 3
(discussion) and 5 (warning), not 7/12; and a biometric late-arrival
exception is now explicit (working past 8:00 PM the prior day excuses a
late-after-11:00-AM arrival).

No code change needed — the HR Info page's Office Timing section reads
`hrPolicies.latePolicy` live (`fetchHrPolicies()`, fresh on every open),
so it will show the new text automatically. Note: this text is still
purely descriptive — the code-alignment backlog item **(e)**
(late-coming progressive tracking, from the earlier policy-gaps list)
remains unimplemented; nothing yet counts or enforces these thresholds
in OMS.

## Quick Ask: NON-AI keyword-matching HR box — built, not merged (2026-09-12)

Branch `feat-quick-ask-keyword-hr`, commit `2c4f047`. A text box at the
TOP of the Leave & HR tab that answers routine questions by keyword
match against data already on the tab — explicitly **not** the earlier
Ask Square AI direction: no API, no network call, no per-use cost.

**How it stays "no new fetch":** `renderHrInfo()` already fetches
`companyData/hrPolicies` every time the tab opens; it now also stashes
that result into a module-level `hrInfoPoliciesCache`. Quick Ask reads
from that cache plus the same already-global `holidays`/`timeLogs`/
`users`/`currentUser` the rest of the tab already uses — zero new
Firestore reads, zero network calls of any kind. Confirmed by source-text
scan: no `fetch(`, no `/api/`, no `anthropic` reference anywhere in
Quick Ask's code, and all three of its functions
(`matchQuickAskCategories`, `buildQuickAskAnswer`, `handleQuickAsk`) are
synchronous, not `async` — there is nothing to await because nothing is
fetched.

**11 keyword categories**, word-boundary matched (not naive substring —
this matters: the attendance keyword "days" is a literal substring of
"holidays", so a naive substring match would wrongly answer an
attendance question every time someone asked about holidays; word-
boundary regex avoids this, verified with a dedicated fixture case) —
holiday, casual, medical, balance, late, half-day, wfh, sandwich, apply,
attendance, timing. Also matches dynamically against whatever holiday
names are actually in the `holidays` array (not a hardcoded festival
list), so it stays correct as the holiday list changes. Multiple
matched categories are all answered in one response, not just the
first hit. No match falls back to a friendly message naming what IS
answerable, never a dead end.

**Own-data-only**, same scoping as the rest of the tab: balance and
attendance answers are always computed from
`users.find(u => u.id === currentUser.id)` and the person's own
`timeLogs`, never any other id. Proven with a fixture store that also
holds a second user's data — zero mention of that user's name or
numbers in any Quick Ask answer.

**CL/ML/balance answers carry the reconciliation caveat verbatim** —
same wording as the static banner already under the balance-grid,
reused deliberately for consistency (not a duplicate UI section — the
static banner is unconditional page markup, this is a JS string used
only inside dynamically-built answer text).

**Apply-flow and approval logic untouched**, reconfirmed with the same
byte-for-byte diff-against-`main` technique used for the tab merge:
`renderLeaves()`, `submitLeaveBtn`, `applyLeaveBtn`, `cancelLeave()`,
`renderApprovals()`, and the `#leaveModal` markup are all identical to
`main`. `renderHrInfo()` changed by exactly the one intended cache-
assignment line — confirmed by diff that removing that single line

## Quick Ask enhanced: chips + chat-bubble scrollback + 4 categories — built, not merged (2026-09-13)

Branch `feat-quick-ask-chips-bubbles`, commit `abfb831`. Still NOT AI,
still zero fetch/api/anthropic, still own-data-only.

**Note on the design instruction:** asked to read
`/mnt/skills/public/frontend-design/SKILL.md` first — that path does not
exist in this Windows Claude Code environment, and no equivalent skill
is registered for this session. Proceeded by grounding the new UI in the
app's **own existing, already-shipped design tokens** instead of
inventing new ones: the suggested-question chips reuse the exact pill
pattern already used by `.user-badge`/`.log-time`/`.ht-target-pill`
(`#F0E6D6` background, `#A96E35` text, `20px` radius) rather than a
new one-off color/shape.

**1. Suggested-question chips** — 6 tappable pills below the input:
Next holiday, My leave balance, Late policy, WFH policy, Am I working
Saturday?, How to apply. Tapping one sets the input to that exact text
and calls the same `handleQuickAsk()` the button/Enter-key already use
— not a separate code path, so a chip can never drift from what typing
the same words would produce.

**2. Chat-bubble scrollback** — replaces the old single answer div with
`#quickAskHistory`: each turn is a right-aligned, brand-filled question
bubble and a left-aligned, card-style answer bubble, capped at the last
5 turns (oldest dropped first), auto-scrolling to the newest. A Clear
button resets it. Purely in-memory UI state — resets on reload, nothing
persisted or sent anywhere.

**3. Four new keyword categories**, all own-data or public, zero new
fetches:
- **Saturday working-day check** — reuses `is24Sat()`/`is24SatLabel()`/
  `isHoliday()`/`getHolidayName()` completely unchanged; the only new
  code is `thisSaturday()`, which just finds the relevant date. A
  holiday correctly overrides an otherwise-working 1st/3rd/5th Saturday
  (verified with a real case: 2026-10-17 is a 3rd Saturday — normally
  working — but is also Durga Puja Maha Sashthi, so the answer
  correctly says "off... it's Durga Puja Maha Sashthi").
- **Pending leave / leave status** — own `leaveRequests`,
  `status:'pending'` only.
- **Leave history / my leaves / past leave** — own `leaveRequests`, all
  statuses.
- **Days-until-holiday countdown** — arithmetic on the existing
  `upcomingHolidays()` result.

**Known, accepted overlap, disclosed not fixed:** the existing
`attendance` category's bare `"days"` keyword and the new `countdown`
category's `"days until"` phrase both legitimately contain the whole
word "days" — "how many days until Christmas" matches both categories
and shows both answers. This is the same disclosed multi-match design
as every other overlap already accepted in this feature (e.g. a
question mentioning two topics gets both answers); not a bug, just
worth knowing about if it ever reads as noisy in practice.

**Apply-flow reconfirmed byte-for-byte identical to `main`**:
`renderLeaves()`, `submitLeaveBtn`, `cancelLeave()`, `renderApprovals()`
— zero lines changed.

**Testing:** `test/feat-quick-ask-enhancements.test.js` (new, 45/45) —
chip markup and click-wiring, the actual rendered bubble HTML structure
(not just answer text), scrollback cap + Clear, the Saturday check
against four known real dates (2nd Saturday off, 5th Saturday working,
the holiday-overrides-a-working-Saturday case, and asking on a Saturday
itself), pending/history proven own-data-scoped against a store holding
a second user's leave records, countdown arithmetic, still-not-AI
source-text proof, and the apply-flow byte-for-byte checks. Updated
`test/feat-quick-ask-keyword-hr.test.js` (44/44) — adapted its
answer-reading mechanism from the removed single answer div to reading
the last bubble in `quickAskHistory`; all 11 original category
assertions unchanged in substance. Full regression: 34/34 test files
green. Isolation confirmed.

Client change → `APP_VERSION` `2026-09-13.1`. Not merged — held for
review.

## Project stage-completion / progress-tracking — built, not merged (2026-09-13)

Branch `feat-project-stage-status`, commit `dbe27c3`. Pairs stage
completion with the existing per-phase hour budgets on Projects &
Budget — the missing "progress" half of project economics, per the
scoped design.

**Data model:** `phaseStatus: {[phaseName]: 'ready'|'confirmed'}` on
each project doc, reusing the existing `PHASES` taxonomy — no new
stage vocabulary. `phaseStatusMeta: {[phaseName]: {readyBy, readyAt,
confirmedBy, confirmedAt}}` alongside it for accountability record-
keeping (mirrors the leave `approvedBy`/`approvedAt` pattern exactly).

**The required collision fix — both parts, proven, not assumed:**
1. `phaseStatus`/`phaseStatusMeta` are written *only* via
   `saveProjectPhaseStatus()`'s targeted dot-path `.update()`. Confirmed
   `saveProject()` itself is byte-for-byte unchanged from `main` — only
   its call site changed.
2. The Edit Project save handler now explicitly carries the project's
   existing `phaseStatus`/`phaseStatusMeta` forward before calling
   `saveProject(data)` (a full `.set()`). Proven against the *actual*
   handler source (executed in a fixture, not reimplemented): editing a
   project's name preserves an existing confirmed+ready `phaseStatus`
   map byte-for-byte; a brand-new project gets a safe empty `{}`.

**Two-step flow**, one click each, no form, on the existing phase row:
`[Mark Ready]` (anyone) → 🔵 Ready badge + `[✓ Confirm Done]`
(admin-only, same role-gate `renderApprovals()` already uses) → ✅ Done.
Reuses `badge-pending`/`badge-approved` (leave status) and
`btn-ghost.btn-sm` (Log Time/Edit on the same card) — no new visual
language, no restructuring, just appended to the row.

**Write-safety:** `saveProjectPhaseStatus()` freeze-checks the current
*server* value before writing. Marking 'ready' is **blocked** if the
phase is already 'confirmed' — a stale click can never regress it.
Re-confirming an already-confirmed phase is an idempotent no-op that
preserves the *original* `confirmedBy`/`confirmedAt` instead of
overwriting them on every re-click.

**At-risk signal**, pure and project-level: `isPhaseAtRisk()` = hours
status `!== 'ok'` (the *existing* ≥80%/100% thresholds every other
budget bar already uses) AND `phaseStatus !== 'confirmed'`. A
project-card "⚠ N phases at risk" chip surfaces the count next to the
existing % figure — the founder-level at-a-glance value the whole
feature exists for.

**Guardrail confirmed, not just claimed:** a comment-stripped
source-text scan proves every occurrence of `readyBy`/`readyAt`/
`confirmedBy`/`confirmedAt` in live code lives inside
`saveProjectPhaseStatus()` itself — nothing anywhere else reads or
aggregates them. No per-person progress/speed/ranking metric exists.

**Testing:** `test/feat-project-stage-status.test.js` (new, 46/46) —
`isPhaseAtRisk()` truth table, the write-safety freeze-check (blocked
regression + idempotent no-op, both via a dot-path-aware mock
Firestore), the collision fix executed against the real save-handler
source for both an edit and a new project, role-gating, the at-risk
chip count (1-at-risk and 0-at-risk cases), the no-aggregation
guarantee, and isolation (`saveProject()`/`renderLeaves()`/
`submitLeaveBtn`/`cancelLeave()`/`renderApprovals()` all byte-for-byte
unchanged from `main`). Full regression: 35/35 test files green.

Client change → `APP_VERSION` `2026-09-13.2`. Not merged — held for
review.

## 2026-09-13 — Bug report: at-risk chip invisible, per-phase buttons off-screen

Read-only diagnosis, no fix. Pulled live Firestore data: Club One
World's Final Design phase is genuinely at 84% budget / 'near' status
with no confirmed phaseStatus — `isPhaseAtRisk()` correctly flags it,
the chip logic and data are both correct. Built an isolated visual
repro at 768px–1440px; `getBoundingClientRect()`/`scrollWidth` ground
truth showed no hard CSS overflow at any tested width. Root cause
diagnosed as a *positioning* problem, not an overflow bug: both
signals (`.phase-status-cell{margin-left:auto}` and the at-risk chip in
the right-side stats block) are right-anchored, so they're the first
thing squeezed out or missed on narrow/zoomed screens — at the mercy
of whatever space the bar/numbers leave over. Recommendation: move
both left, near what they describe. (Caveat disclosed to the user:
the Browser pane's resize tool wouldn't go below ~980px effective
width in that session, limiting how narrow could be directly tested.)

## 2026-09-13 — V28: layout fix + flag-work-after-done + reversibility

Branch: `feat-stage-status-layout-flag-undo` (cut from `main`,
NOT merged — built for review). Version bumped to `2026-09-13.3`.

Three additive changes on top of the shipped stage-completion feature,
acting on the diagnosis above:

1. **Layout fix.** `.phase-status-cell` moved to sit immediately after
   `.phase-name` and before `.phase-bar-wrap` in the row template;
   dropped `margin-left:auto` entirely (left-anchored now — visible at
   any width by construction, not by however much space is left over).
   The at-risk chip moved out of the right-side stats block into the
   project title div, right next to `${proj.name}` — top-left primary
   reading position. Chip text shortened to "⚠ N at risk" to fit
   inline with the title. Purely repositioning: same badges, same
   data, same logic — confirmed via structural checks in
   `test/feat-stage-status-layout-flag-undo.test.js`.

2. **Flag work-after-done.** New pure function `minsLoggedAfterDone
   (projectId, phase, confirmedAt)` sums minutes from timeLog entries
   whose work `date` is strictly after `confirmedAt`'s date, reading
   the same `allTimeBudgetLogs`/`timeLogs` fallback the hours-vs-budget
   figures already use. Renders as a small `.phase-afterdone-flag` line
   under any confirmed phase row where it's >0: "⚠ Xh logged to
   [phase] after marked done." Logging itself is completely untouched
   — `saveTimeLog()` and `openLogModal()` are confirmed byte-for-byte
   unchanged from `main`, so this is purely a display signal, never a
   block.

3. **Reversibility.** New `saveProjectPhaseStatusUndo(projectId, phase,
   action)` — deliberately a SEPARATE function from
   `saveProjectPhaseStatus()` (which stays byte-for-byte unchanged;
   that function's whole job is to protectively block accidental
   regression, which is the wrong posture for an intentional undo
   click). Same write-safety shape: freeze-checks the current server
   value first, dot-path `.update()`, never `.set()`. Three actions:
   `clear-ready` (ready→none; the original ready-marker or any admin),
   `revert-to-ready` (confirmed→ready, admin-only, preserves the
   original readyBy/readyAt while clearing confirmedBy/confirmedAt),
   `clear-confirmed` (confirmed→none, admin-only). Uses
   `firebase.firestore.FieldValue.delete()` on the now-stale meta leaf
   fields rather than leaving them dangling. Small "↩ revert"/"✕ clear"
   links added next to each badge, role/ownership-gated in the render.

Testing: new fixture `test/feat-stage-status-layout-flag-undo.test.js`
(47 checks) covers the row/title structural layout, `minsLoggedAfterDone`
correctness (fires only when strictly after, never on/before), logging
isolation (byte-for-byte `saveTimeLog`/`openLogModal`), all three undo
actions × role-gating × stale-state rules against a dot-path-aware
mock Firestore, and re-confirms `isPhaseAtRisk`/`saveProjectPhaseStatus`/
the collision fix all still hold. Updated
`test/feat-project-stage-status.test.js` where V28 legitimately changed
behavior it asserted on: the chip text format ("N at risk" instead of
"N phase(s) at risk"), and the accountability-metadata invariant — that
test previously required `readyBy`/`readyAt`/`confirmedBy`/`confirmedAt`
to appear ONLY inside `saveProjectPhaseStatus()` (write-only); V28's
undo function and the flag/layout code now legitimately READ them
(ownership checks, the after-done comparison), so the test now checks
the real remaining invariant — every occurrence is accounted for by one
of four known, audited call sites, and none of them aggregate/group by
person. Full regression: all 35 `test/*.test.js` files, 0 failures.
Isolation check: `git diff main -- index.html` shows zero touches to

— only comment text and reused CSS/badge class names matched those
keywords. `saveProjectPhaseStatus()` and `saveProject()` both confirmed

## 2026-09-13 — Read-only scoping: What's Due + Recognition

Before building anything, scoped two Dashboard features read-only, per
the owner's request, so they could decide which/whether to build.

**What's Due**: confirmed no due-date field exists on projects today
(only startDate/endDate). Key finding: unlike phaseStatus, a due-date
field entered on the Edit Project form needs NONE of the collision-fix
discipline that feature required — phaseStatus needed it because it's
written through a SEPARATE path (saveProjectPhaseStatus()'s dot-path
.update()) that bypasses the form; a due date typed into the same form
as start/end has no second writer to collide with. Recommended reusing
the pct/status/atRiskCount already computed in renderProjectsBudget()
rather than any new "on track" logic, and placing the view as a new
Dashboard card (matching the existing Who's Working Now/Needs
Reconciliation pattern) rather than a new tab.

**Recognition**: confirmed project-level positive facts (all-confirmed-
and-clean, under-budget) are safely derivable read-only from existing
data. Person-level recognition (the owner's "someone who mentored a
lot" example) was scoped HONESTLY as not safely achievable: mentoring
exists in this codebase only as a private, per-person, admin-authored
Employee Review rubric rating (Studio Health) — not an objective,
derivable fact. Surfacing it publicly would mean exposing private
review content, a different and much bigger decision than a read-only
recognition card. Recommended project-level only for now; flagged that
any future person-level recognition should be a separate, deliberately
non-metric, admin-authored shout-out feature, not a data-surfacing one.

   form next to Start/End Date, read into the same rebuilt `data`
   object the save handler already builds (`dueDate:
   document.getElementById('pDueDate').value||null`). Confirmed this
   needed zero collision-fix work: nothing else ever writes to it, so
   there's no second path to defend against. `openProjectModal()`
   populates it when editing.

2. **`computeProjectStats(proj)`** — extracted the exact
   pct/status/atRiskCount computation `renderProjectsBudget()` already
   did per card into a shared pure helper, with zero formula changes,
   so both new cards and Projects & Budget read identical numbers
   instead of a second implementation that could drift. Also returns
   `allPhasesConfirmed` (new), used by both cards below.

3. **"📅 What's Due" card** — lists projects that are overdue or due
   within 14 days (`WHATS_DUE_SOON_DAYS`), sorted soonest/most-overdue
   first. Fully-confirmed projects are excluded even if their on-file
   due date is in the past (nothing left to catch up on); projects with
   no due date set are excluded (this card is about urgency, not a
   nudge to fill in a field). Each row reads e.g. "BURDWAN RD — ⚠ 4
   days overdue · 48% used · ⚠ 2 at risk". Overdue rows and due-soon
   rows carrying at-risk phases are highlighted (red background/left
   border); a due-soon row with nothing at risk stays plain. Placed
   first in the Dashboard card stack (before Who's Working Now) —
   deadlines are the most actionable item a founder should see.

4. **"🎉 Wins This Quarter" card** — PROJECT-level positive facts only:
   "N projects completed clean this quarter" (all phases confirmed,
   zero at-risk), "[Project]: completed under its overall hour
   budget". "This quarter" = the project's `endDate` falls inside the
   current FY quarter, reusing `getQuarter()`/`quarterEndDate()`
   (already used by Studio Health — one definition of "quarter" across
   the app). Capped to 4 facts, never ordered by relative performance,
   no project ever named as NOT on the list. Placed LAST in the
   Dashboard stack, after all operational cards, per the scoping's
   "celebratory, not action-needed" placement call.

Testing: new fixture `test/feat-dashboard-duedate-recognition.test.js`
(46 checks) covers the dueDate save-path (including that it doesn't
clobber phaseStatus/phaseStatusMeta, and that a blank field saves
`null` not `""`), `computeWhatsDue()`'s sorting/pairing/exclusion rules
executed against real synthetic data, `renderWhatsDue()`'s highlight
rules, `computeQuarterWins()`'s project-level-only/no-ranking/capped/
quarter-scoping guarantees (explicitly checked against all 5 real user
names to confirm none ever appear in a fact), and that both cards'
markup exists in the Dashboard page with `renderDashboard()` actually
calling both render functions, in the right stack order. Updated
`test/feat-project-stage-status.test.js` and
`test/fix-alltime-budget-tracking.test.js` where the
`computeProjectStats()` extraction legitimately moved logic those
fixtures asserted on directly (identical behavior, now behind the
shared helper). Full regression: all 36 `test/*.test.js` files, 0
failures. Isolation confirmed: `saveProject`/`saveProjectPhaseStatus`/
`saveTimeLog`/`openLogModal`/`saveProjectPhaseStatusUndo` all
byte-for-byte unchanged from `main`; `git diff main -- index.html`
shows zero matches for save/sync/heartbeat/revival/approval/Quick-Ask
function names in added lines.

## 2026-09-13 — Read-only scoping: attendance auto-deduction → flag-for-review

Before building, scoped converting `runAttendanceAutoDeduction()` from
silent auto-deduct to flag-for-review, per the owner's request to stop
wrong deductions and match written policy ("no automatic deduction
until attendance verified; notify the person").

Key findings: the existing Approvals infrastructure already does most
of what a review queue needs — `renderApprovals()`'s Approve/Reject
buttons and `updateApprovalBadge()`'s pending-count badge work on ANY
`leaveRequests` doc with `status:'pending'`, and `renderLeaves()`'s "My
Leave" list already surfaces every request (any status) to its owner —
so "notify the person" and "admin review queue" were both achievable
by reusing existing UI, not building new. Pulled live production data:
**264 `autoDeducted:true` leaveRequests docs exist across 15 people**
(far beyond the 8 holiday-related ones already reversed on 2026-09-11)
— including Souvik and Rai's CL balances, deliberately left negative
(−5 and −8 respectively) after that reversal. Querying live data for
this scoping found **Souvik has since drifted further, to −8** — live
proof the engine keeps
generating new false positives on every admin login, which is exactly
why a manual correction alone can't make anything stick. Also found:
the Hours Tracker's excess-CL salary-deduction calculation reads
`casLeft` directly, so these contaminated negative balances are
currently feeding a real salary-impact figure shown to admins — a
reason the eventual 264-doc reversal (explicitly deferred, see below)
matters beyond leave-balance cosmetics.

## 2026-09-13 — V30 shipped (on branch): flag-for-review, NOT auto-deduct

Branch `fix-attendance-flag-not-deduct` (cut from `main`, NOT merged —
built for review). Version bumped to `2026-09-13.6`.

Built PART 1 ONLY per instruction — the 264-doc historical reversal is
explicitly deferred as a separate, careful step, and employee
self-dismiss is explicitly out of scope.

**What changed**: `runAttendanceAutoDeduction()`'s trigger is
byte-identical in logic (working day, no approved leave, zero OMS
timeLog activity — still never checks biometric data, none exists
in-app). Only the consequence changed: it now writes a
`status:'pending'`, `isSystemFlagged:true`, `autoDeducted:false`
leaveRequests doc instead of `status:'approved'` + an immediate
`casLeft`/`medLeft` decrement. The function no longer calls
`saveUser()` or assigns to either balance field anywhere — confirmed
by source-text checks and a behavioral test proving zero `saveUser()`
calls during a scan that finds real absences.

The balance write moved entirely into `renderApprovals()`'s existing
Approve handler, which is now the ONLY place `autoDeducted` flips to
`true` (only for `isSystemFlagged` items) and the ONLY place
`casLeft`/`medLeft` change for them — proven by executing the real
Approve handler against synthetic data and checking both the Firestore
write payload and the in-memory balance math. Reject dismisses with
zero balance/`autoDeducted` impact, proven the same way.
`renderApprovals()` also gained a "🚩 Possible Absence" card label and
distinct body text for system-flagged items, so an admin can't mistake
one for a person-submitted request. The 3-consecutive-months warning
(unchanged) reads `autoDeducted`, so it keeps counting only *confirmed*
absences — proven behaviorally with two scan runs (3 months of
`autoDeducted:true` triggers it; 3 months of still-pending flags does
not). `renderLeaves()`'s "My Leave" list no longer shows a Cancel
button on a system-flagged pending item (self-dismiss out of scope)
but the item is still fully visible there, satisfying "notify the
person" the same way any other pending request already does.

**Testing**: new `test/fix-attendance-flag-not-deduct.test.js` (36
checks) executes the real extracted `runAttendanceAutoDeduction()`/
`renderApprovals()` against synthetic data — flags instead of
deducting, the original trigger still correctly skips

balance only changes on Approve, `autoDeducted` only flips on Approve,
the 3-month-warning behavior, Reject dismisses cleanly, and the label
distinguishes system-flagged from real requests. Updated 5 pre-existing
fixtures whose blanket "renderLeaves()/renderApprovals() byte-for-byte
unchanged from main" isolation checks needed to account for this
legitimate change — each now strips the exact known V30 additions back
out and proves byte-identity to `main` holds for everything else, so

## 2026-09-16 — V31: Submission Tracker, redirected mid-build to a hybrid spec

Branch `feat-submission-tracker`, cut from `main` @ `a1df9ab`. This
revives the "What's Due" item parked during the V29 descope (project
due dates are per-submission and shift, so a single `project.dueDate`
field was the wrong shape — see the 2026-09-13 V29 descope entry
above).

**First draft, built then fully reverted.** Started against the
initial spec: `project.submissions: [...]` — an array field on the
project doc, each submission carrying a deliverables checklist and a
reschedule `shiftLog`, managed through a per-project modal. Fully
implemented (granular `.update({submissions:[...]})` writer, the
required phaseStatus-style collision-fix carry-forward in the
`saveProjBtn` handler, per-project modal UI, a Dashboard "What's Due"
card). **Before any fixtures were written**, Ranit revised the spec —
per the "confirm the data model before writing fixtures" instruction,
this was exactly the checkpoint working as intended. Since nothing
had been committed yet, `git checkout -- index.html` cleanly reverted
the entire first draft with zero residue, and the rebuild started
fresh against the new model. No code from the abandoned draft shipped.

**Revised spec, built and pushed.** Submissions moved to their own
Firestore collection, `submissions` — one doc per submission:
`{id, projectId, plannedDate, actualDate|null, remark, status:'open'|
'done', createdAt}`. Single store, two reads: the existing weekly
calendar (Dashboard) reads by `plannedDate`/`actualDate`, a new
founder-only KPI card reads by `projectId`. Deliberately leaner than
the first draft — no deliverables checklist, no shift-log (moot once
`plannedDate` is fixed at creation and never rewritten).

Key design decisions:
- **Single-store integrity is structural, not conventional.** Because
  submissions live in a wholly separate collection, `saveProject()`'s
  own full `.set()` and `upsertTimeLog()`'s planEntries writes cannot
  touch a submission even in principle — confirmed by executing the
  real `saveProjBtn` handler and checking its rebuilt `data` object
  never mentions `submissions` at all. This is a stronger guarantee
  than the phaseStatus-on-project-doc pattern, which needed an
  explicit collision-fix carry-forward specifically because it shares
  a document with fields other code paths rewrite wholesale.
- **Slip handling**: `plannedDate` is written once, at creation, and
  no function anywhere ever reassigns it (checked structurally).
  Ticking "submitted" sets `status:'done'` and stamps `actualDate` to
  today — reversible, unticking clears both back to `open`/`null`. An
  unticked submission whose `plannedDate` has passed renders as
  overdue ON that same planned day; a late-but-done submission still
  shows a marker on its original planned day (tagged "(late)") plus a
  second echo marker on the day it actually landed — the slip is
  visible from either day, never erased or silently moved.
- **Display**: a new, dedicated non-user "📅 Submissions" row on the
  *existing* weekly calendar grid (reusing `renderDashboard()`'s own
  `cal-grid`/`cal-row` machinery) — day-level and project-level only,
  deliberately not one of the per-person rows below it, so a
  submission can never read as "assigned to someone." Team-visible,
  no gating.
- **KPI**: `computeSubmissionsKPI()` aggregates strictly by
  `projectId` — on-time vs. slipped counts, average slip-days — never
  by person. Rendered in a new admin-gated Dashboard card (hidden
  entirely for non-admins, same pattern as the reconciliation queue),
  consistent with the money-layer's existing founder-only posture.
  No reminder/notification wiring anywhere in the new code — pull-based
  rendering only, checked via source-text sweep for
  `setInterval`/`setTimeout`/`Notification`/`showToast`.

**Testing**: new fixture `test/feat-submission-tracker.test.js` (51
checks) executes the real `createSubmission()`/`toggleSubmissionDone()`
against a recording mock Firestore — reversible tick/untick, partial
`{merge:true}` writes touching only `status`/`actualDate`,
`plannedDate` byte-identical before and after a full tick→untick round
trip; `submissionChipsForDate()` proven to place the overdue/late
marker on the planned day and the completion echo on the actual day;
`computeSubmissionsKPI()` proven correct across multiple
projects/submissions with an explicit check that no output field name
matches anything person-shaped; single-store integrity proven by
executing the real `saveProjBtn` handler and sweeping
`upsertTimeLog()`/`openLogModal()` for any submissions reference; and
isolation via a strip-back-to-main diff proving `renderDashboard()`'s
only delta is its two new render calls, with
`saveProject`/`renderLeaves`/`renderApprovals`/`saveTimeLog`/
`upsertTimeLog`/`runAttendanceAutoDeduction` all confirmed
byte-for-byte unchanged from `main`.

**Incidental fix along the way**: 5 pre-existing fixtures
(`feat-dashboard-recognition`, `feat-project-stage-status`,
`feat-quick-ask-enhancements`, `feat-quick-ask-keyword-hr`,
`fix-merge-leave-hrinfo-tabs`) had a latent, now-triggered bug: their
V30 isolation checks were written as "strip the V30 addition out of
this branch, then compare to `main`" — correct only while V30 hadn't
merged yet. Since V30 shipped to `main` last session, `main` now
already contains what these checks were trying to strip, so they'd
fail on `main` itself, independent of this build. Converted all 5 to
plain byte-identity assertions (both sides now have the change),
matching a fix already applied to an identical situation elsewhere in
this codebase (`renderHrInfo()`/`hrInfoPoliciesCache` in
`feat-quick-ask-keyword-hr.test.js`).

Full regression: 38 test files, 0 failures. Version bumped to
`2026-09-16.1`.

**Pushed, not merged**: `git push -u origin feat-submission-tracker`
succeeded — plain `git`, no dependency on `gh`. Confirmed `gh` is
genuinely not installed on this machine (`gh: command not found`,
`which gh` empty) — this is a real environment gap, not a one-off
session glitch, and it will block any *later* step that specifically
needs the GitHub CLI (e.g., opening a PR via `gh pr create`). It does
not block anything done so far, since branch pushes and the eventual
merge both work over plain `git`.

**Still open**: the preview deployment URL for Ranit's review.
`vercel`/`gh` CLIs are both unavailable in this environment, and
guessing the Vercel git-branch preview URL pattern
(`https://<project>-git-<branch>-<org>.vercel.app`) failed
(`DEPLOYMENT_NOT_FOUND` — this Vercel project's slug is
`vite-react-eeq8`, not a guessable "square-office"-shaped name, and
the org slug isn't derivable from the linked `.vercel/project.json`
either). The repo's Vercel integration is confirmed git-connected

from the Vercel dashboard directly, or a Vercel API token/the `vercel`

## 2026-09-16 — V31 fix: wrong surface (Dashboard, not Weekly Planner)

Ranit's preview review (`82c5651`) reported the feature invisible
anywhere in the running app. Root cause confirmed: the "📅 Submissions"
marker row was wired into `renderDashboard()`'s `calGrid` — Dashboard's
weekly calendar, a read-only summary view. The spec called for "the
SAME surface used to assign/self-assign work," which is Weekly
Planner's own `renderPlanner()`/`plannerGrid` (a separate page,
`page-planner`, with its own per-user rows and click-to-assign
cells) — a different function and a different DOM tree from
Dashboard's calendar entirely.

Checked and ruled out the other two hypotheses before concluding it
was purely a placement bug: neither the marker row nor the founder-only
KPI card ever conditionally hides on zero records — the row always
renders with a "+ submission" affordance in every day cell, and the
KPI card always shows (with an explanatory empty state) for an admin
regardless of how many submissions exist. So the entry point to create
the first submission was never hidden by an empty-state guard; it
was simply rendering on a tab nobody was looking at for this feature.

**Fix**: moved `renderSubmissionsCalendarRow()`'s call from
`renderDashboard()` to `renderPlanner()`, and switched its container
classes from `cal-row`/`cal-cell`/`cal-name` to
`planner-row`/`planner-cell`/`planner-name` to match the Planner grid
it now lives in. `toggleSubmissionDoneAndRefresh()` and the
Add-Submission save handler now call `renderPlanner()` instead of
`renderDashboard()`. Dashboard keeps only `renderSubmissionsKPI()` —
that placement (founder-only monitoring, admin-gated) was correct and
untouched.

**Testing**: updated `test/feat-submission-tracker.test.js` — the
`renderDashboard()` isolation check now asserts it carries only the
KPI call and explicitly does NOT reference the marker row; a new
`renderPlanner()` isolation check (strip-and-compare to main) proves
the marker-row call is its only delta. Added two new behavioral checks
executing the real `renderSubmissionsCalendarRow()`/
`renderSubmissionsKPI()` against zero-record synthetic data, locking
in that neither ever hides on empty state — the exact question Ranit
asked. 57/57 passing. Full regression: 38 test files, 0 failures.

**Confirmed exact entry point for retest**: Weekly Planner tab → the
"📅 Submissions" row (top of the grid, above the per-person rows) →
click "+ submission" under any day's cell → modal opens prefilled with
that date → pick project + remark → Add. Pushed (`8d871f9`), not
merged.

## 2026-09-16 — V31.2: bug fix, "early" was wrongly labeled "(late)"

Reported directly: a submission planned for Thu 17/9 and ticked done
on Wed 16/9 (early) was showing "(late)". Root cause:
`submissionChipsForDate()`'s done-branch tested only
`actualDate !== plannedDate`, with no check on which side of
`plannedDate` `actualDate` fell — any difference, in either direction,
read as "late."

Fix: added `computeSubmissionOutcome(sub, todayStr)` as the single
source of truth for a submission's status, now called by BOTH
`submissionChipsForDate()` (the calendar chip) and
`computeSubmissionsKPI()` (the founder-only KPI), so the two can never
diverge on what "late" means:
- done, `actualDate < plannedDate` → `'early'`
- done, `actualDate === plannedDate` → `'on-time'`
- done, `actualDate > plannedDate` → `'late'`, with `lateDays = actual − planned`
- not done, `plannedDate` in the past → `'overdue'`
- not done, `plannedDate` today/future → `'upcoming'`

`computeSubmissionsKPI()` updated to match: early and on-time both
count as met (`onTime` bucket); only a genuine `'late'` outcome counts
as slipped, with slip-days taken directly from the outcome's own
`lateDays` (always > 0 for `'late'`, so no separate clamp needed).
Investigating this confirmed the KPI aggregation's *slipped* logic had
already been correct before this fix (it already only counted
`slipDays > 0` toward the slipped bucket) — the actual gap was
entirely in the calendar chip's label, which the KPI never touched.

**Testing**: 13 new checks in `test/feat-submission-tracker.test.js`
covering all 5 outcome cases via `computeSubmissionOutcome()`
directly (including the exact reported scenario verbatim: planned
2026-09-17, actual 2026-09-16 → `'early'`, never `'late'`), an
end-to-end check through the real `submissionChipsForDate()` renderer
confirming the early chip's text never contains `"(late)"`, a
same-day on-time case showing neither label, and a KPI-level check
that an early submission counts toward `onTime` (not `slipped`) with
the genuinely-slipped project's `avgSlipDays` unaffected by it. 77/77
passing. Full regression: 38 test files, 0 failures. Isolation
reconfirmed byte-for-byte for `saveProject`/`saveProjectPhaseStatus`/
`saveTimeLog`/`saveUser`/`upsertTimeLog`/`runAttendanceAutoDeduction`/
`renderApprovals`/`renderLeaves`/`computeProjectStats`/
`computeQuarterWins`.

Version bumped to `2026-09-16.3`. Pushed (`4c6d7df`), not merged.

**Preview URL**: same environment gap as the last two entries — `gh`
and `vercel` both re-confirmed unavailable this turn
(`command not found`), no API token present. This is a push to the
same branch, so the existing bookmarked preview URL should update to
this commit once Vercel's build finishes; flagged rather than guessed
at again.

## 2026-09-16 — feat-submission-tracker merged; gh device-code detour, resolved

Ranit approved on preview and merged `feat-submission-tracker` → `main`
via the GitHub web UI (`f303b29`, "Merge pull request #2") — a
one-off, because `gh` came up unavailable in this session. Production
confirmed live at `2026-09-16.3` immediately after, matching `main`
exactly.

**Root-cause check on `gh`, done properly rather than assumed**:
checked this repo's actual merge-commit history. Every merge before
today is a plain `Merge branch 'X'` commit — what `git merge` +
`git push` produces, the exact path used for every ship this whole
engagement (V27 through V31). `f303b29` is the *only* GitHub-style
"Merge pull request #N" commit anywhere in the log. Conclusion: `gh`
was never installed or used in this environment before tonight — there
was no prior working credential to have expired. `gh --version`
confirmed this directly (`command not found`) before installation.

Installed `gh` (v2.101.0, user-scope via `winget install --id
GitHub.cli --scope user` — the default machine-scope MSI install
failed with exit 1602, an interactive-install/UAC issue; user-scope
succeeded cleanly). Attempted `gh auth login` device-code flow four
times (two via `--web`, two without); every attempt either expired or
failed with `context deadline exceeded` on the polling step, despite
Ranit completing the browser-side authorization each time — points to
something in this environment's network path delaying or blocking
`gh`'s outbound polling requests to GitHub's OAuth token endpoint.
Not resolved this session.

**Decision, per Ranit**: don't chase `gh` auth further. Going forward,
**the merge path for this project is plain git** — `git checkout main
&& git pull && git merge --no-ff <branch> && git push origin main`,
exactly the discipline already used for every ship in this engagement
(dry-run merge check, isolation, full regression, then commit and
push). No PR workflow, no `gh`, unless explicitly asked for. This note
exists so a future session doesn't re-attempt the device-code detour
from scratch — `gh` remains installed but unauthenticated, and that's
fine; it isn't on the critical path for merging.

## 2026-09-16/17 — V32: Personal Growth Goals, built and pushed for review

Branch `feat-personal-growth-goals`, cut from `main` @ `b811c10`
(post the `gh` device-code decision). A private, self-owned
skill-growth checklist — the whole point is that it belongs to the
individual, with deliberately no founder/admin view, no aggregation,
no reporting.

**The privacy guarantee is structural, not conventional.** Data lives
at `users/{currentUser.id}/growthGoals/{goalId}` — a subcollection
under the person's own user doc, not a top-level `growthGoals`
collection filtered by a `userId` field. The difference matters: a
`.where('userId','==', X)` shape puts the entire privacy guarantee on
every call site remembering to pass the right `X` forever; the
subcollection shape makes the wrong call impossible to *express* —
`loadMyGrowthGoals()`, `addGrowthGoal(skill)`, and
`toggleGrowthGoal(goalId, currentStatus)` all hardcode `currentUser.id`
as the only path segment they ever touch, and none of their function
signatures accept a `userId`/`targetUser` parameter at all. `isAdmin`
is never referenced anywhere in this feature's code — not "checked and
denied," genuinely absent, so there's no gate to misconfigure or forget.

Ticking a goal stamps `achievedAt` and moves it from "want to learn" to
"built"; unticking clears `achievedAt` and reverses it — reversible,
silent, same grammar as the Submission Tracker's tick/untick. Curated
14-skill starter list (Leadership, Client handling, Site supervision,
Project management, Material selection, Costing/estimation,
Construction documentation, Schematic design, 3D visualization,
AutoCAD, Revit, SketchUp, Team coordination, Presentation/pitching)
plus free-text "add your own." No gamification anywhere — no streaks,
no completion %, no badges, no "haven't updated" warnings; an empty
list renders with zero prompt, exactly per spec. New low-key
"Personal" nav section, its own page (`page-mygrowth`), with the
"This is private to you — no one else, including admins, can see
this" line kept visible on every view, not just first-open.

**Testing**: new `test/feat-personal-growth-goals.test.js` (44 checks).
The privacy fixture — the one the whole feature rests on — is proven
both structurally (function signatures, source-text sweep for
`isAdmin`/`userId`) and behaviorally: an ADMIN `currentUser` calling
`toggleGrowthGoal()` with a *target* user's real goal id, against a
mock Firestore seeded with both users' data, still writes under the
admin's own subcollection and leaves the target's actual doc
completely untouched — not merely "didn't happen to write" in that
run, but structurally incapable of doing otherwise given the function
never accepts a user identifier to redirect to. Also covers tick/untick
stamping, add-your-own, empty-state neutrality, absence of any
`collectionGroup` query or admin-facing aggregate function anywhere in
the file, and isolation (byte-for-byte unchanged: `saveProject`,
`renderLeaves`, `renderApprovals`, `renderDashboard`, `renderPlanner`,
`saveTimeLog`, `runAttendanceAutoDeduction`, and the Submission
Tracker's `createSubmission`/`toggleSubmissionDone`/
`computeSubmissionsKPI` — plus a scoped check that the shared nav-click
router gained exactly the one new `mygrowth` route line).

Incidentally fixed 2 checks in `test/feat-submission-tracker.test.js`
that hit the identical post-merge "strip the addition, compare to
main" staleness seen twice before in this codebase — V31 merged to
`main` since those checks were written, so `main` now already contains
what they were stripping. Converted to plain byte-identity assertions.

Full regression: 39 test files, 0 failures. Isolation confirmed
byte-for-byte for every function above; this build touches zero
existing surfaces (timers, leave, reconciliation, Submission Tracker)
— purely additive. Version bumped to `2026-09-16.4`.

Pushed (`3947df6`) via plain `git push`, per the merge-path decision
above. Not merged — awaiting Ranit's review on preview, same
discipline as V31. Preview URL: same environment gap noted for every
V31 push — `gh`/`vercel` remain unauthenticated/unavailable for
fetching it programmatically; Vercel's git integration should still
generate a preview deployment for this branch automatically, findable
via the Vercel dashboard.

## 2026-09-17 — Reported privacy leak on preview: diagnosed, NOT a code/data bug

Ranit tested the V32 preview by hand with two accounts (Admin and
Tasmin) and reported both showing the identical growth-goals list —
flagged DO NOT MERGE, correctly, since this is the guarantee the whole
feature rests on.

**Investigated directly against production Firestore (read-only)
rather than guessing.** Findings:
- Every one of the 9 real user docs has an `id` field stored inside
  the doc that matches its own Firestore doc key exactly — zero
  mismatches, zero duplicates.
- `users/undefined/growthGoals` is empty — rules out the classic
  `db.collection(...).doc(undefined)` footgun this codebase has hit
  before (the V14.3.27 duplicate-timeLogs root cause).
- `users/u1/growthGoals` (Admin) held exactly the 5 goals Ranit
  reported seeing on "both accounts." `users/mmllonwbwgp/growthGoals`
  (Tasmin) held 2 **genuinely different** goals — Leadership
  (achieved), Material selection (learning) — that were never in
  Admin's list at all.

**Conclusion: the data was never shared, and `currentUser.id` resolves
correctly per login.** What actually happened: this app persists login
via a single **shared `localStorage` key** (`currentSession`),
auto-restored on every page load (the `loadData().then(...)` block).
Opening two tabs of the *same browser* and logging into a different
account in each makes both tabs snap to whichever account logged in
**most recently**, on their next reload — so the tab believed to be
"Tasmin's session" was, at the moment it was checked, silently showing
Admin's session instead. This is a pre-existing, app-wide property of
how this app has always persisted sessions (every per-user page —
Leave & HR balance, My Leave, Time Log — has the exact same
same-browser cross-tab behavior); Personal Growth Goals didn't
introduce it and can't fix it on its own without touching core
session/auth code, which is out of scope for this feature. **Correct
verification needs two separate browser profiles, or one normal + one
private/incognito window** — not two tabs of the same profile.

**Testing added anyway**, since a stronger test was genuinely
warranted: extended `test/feat-personal-growth-goals.test.js` with a
section that runs the REAL, unmodified `doLogin()` (not a
reimplementation, not a hand-built `currentUser`) for two sequential
real logins — admin then Tasmin, using the exact skill names from the
original report — with every other side effect `doLogin()` fires
(timer/heartbeat/leave-listener monitors) stubbed as a harmless no-op.
Confirms `currentUser.id` switches correctly on each real login and
`loadMyGrowthGoals()` reflects exactly the currently-logged-in user's
own goals both times, never a stale or previous one. 7 new checks,
51/51 passing overall (up from 44). **No `index.html` changes** — the
scoping code was already correct; this was fixture-only. Full
regression: 39 test files, 0 failures.

Pushed (`8a58516`). Still not merged — awaiting Ranit's re-verification
on preview using two separate browser contexts.

Ranit re-verified on preview with proper session isolation this time
(logged in as Tasmin, checked, logged out, logged back in as admin,

showed genuinely different growth goals (Tasmin: Material selection /
Leadership; Admin: Site supervision, Presentation, Costing / Client
handling, Project management), confirming the diagnosis above:
privacy was correct all along, the earlier report was a same-browser

above.

  (`feat-personal-growth-goals.test.js`: 51/51,
  `feat-submission-tracker.test.js`: 77/77, all others green).
- Isolation check: byte-for-byte comparison against `main` for
  `saveProject`, `saveProjectPhaseStatus`, `saveTimeLog`,
  `openLogModal`, `saveProjectPhaseStatusUndo`, `saveUser`,
  `saveLeaveRequest`, `upsertTimeLog`, `runAttendanceAutoDeduction`,

(`curl https://squareoffice.vercel.app/version.json`).

Post-deploy sanity check against the live production bundle (not just
the branch): `APP_VERSION` is `2026-09-16.4`; the "My Growth" nav item
and `#page-mygrowth` are present and not gated behind an admin-only
flag; the persistent privacy sentence ("This is private to you... No
one else — including admins — can see this.") is present in the

present in the live script with no `isAdmin` reference anywhere near

All 9 checks passed.

## Leave-bank reconciliation, round 1 — Ridhi + Suravi (2026-09-18)

Team confirmations are coming in against the leave-bank data quality
gap surfaced earlier ([the `users`-collection-is-authoritative table
above]). Ranit is going person-by-person; 7 more (5 at time of Ridhi/
Suravi, since revised) remain pending. Per-person discipline: freeze-check
(`source:'server'` read immediately before each write) → targeted
field-only write (`updateDoc`/`deleteDoc` via the app's own public
Firebase web config, same trust model as `api/finalize-stale.js` —
no Admin SDK, no new secret) → `source:'server'` verify immediately
after. Every write below was done this way, one doc at a time, never
batched blind.

**Suravi** — clean, no conflicts:
- `medLeft`: 4 → **9**, `casLeft`: 0 → **2**. Verified.
- Checked for a `leaveRequests` doc on 2026-08-28 (she confirms
  present that day) — none exists. Nothing to delete, confirmed by a
  fresh read.

**Ridhi** — one real conflict surfaced and resolved before any bank
write:
- Her confirmed casual dates (04.05, 09.07, 10.07) didn't cleanly map
  onto the stored data: `leaveRequests` doc `mqjfkp7a7lmrc1` held
  **one 3-calendar-day range, 09–11 July**, not three separate docs.
  11.07 was in Ranit's requested-delete (present) list, but deleting
  the whole doc would have erased the confirmed 09–10 leave too.
  Bank write was held until this was resolved — did not guess at a
  split/shorten without confirmation.
- Root cause, confirmed by Ranit: 11.07 was the 2nd Saturday
  (non-working), and sandwich-leave counting is revoked through July
  — so 11.07 was never a real leave day and should never have been
  in the range at all. Fix was to **shorten**, not split: `mqjfkp7a7lmrc1`
  `endDate` 2026-07-11 → **2026-07-10**, `days` 4 → **2** (the stored
  `days:4` was already inconsistent with even the original 3-day span
  — a separate, pre-existing data error, now corrected as part of the
  same write). This left her confirmed 3-day casual total (04.05 +
  09.07 + 10.07) intact and unaffected.
- With that resolved: `medLeft`: 0 → **2.5**, `casLeft`: 0 → **2**.
  Verified.
- 9 confirmed-present-day `leaveRequests` docs deleted (05-12, 05-19,
  05-21, 06-30 `mr1m8pnitjn9ix`, 07-01, 07-08, 07-17, 07-28, 07-29 —
  one delete per date; freeze-checked and verified gone
  individually). **Deliberately kept**: `mr1m8qpbiiyves`, a second doc
  also dated 06-30 — this is a `type:'warning'`, `days:0` system
  notice ("3 consecutive months of unplanned absences"), not a leave
  record, doesn't touch the bank, and deleting it would erase a real
  HR event. 19.08 and 21.08 (also confirmed-present) turned out to
  have no `leaveRequests` docs at all — nothing to delete, confirmed
  by a fresh read, not assumed from the original recon.
- **Second discrepancy surfaced and fixed**: two auto-deducted
  biometric-absence docs, 07-15 (`mrn196fkvrfk6u`) and 07-16
  (`mrohcewwho0qzq`), were stored `type:'casual'`/`leaveType:'CL'`
  even though Ridhi's confirmation lists both as medical. Root cause:
  the auto-deduction logic (`index.html:6089`,
  `deductFrom = medLeft > 0 ? 'medical' : 'casual'`) had correctly
  fallen back to casual because her running medical balance was
  already exhausted by mid-July from her earlier confirmed medical
  days that month — not a mistyping, just balance-driven logic
  disagreeing with what actually happened. Since her confirmation is
  now the source of truth for the bank figures, both docs were
  retyped `casual`→`medical` / `CL`→`ML`, **field-scoped**: only
  `type`/`leaveType` touched, every other field (days, dates, reason,
  `autoDeducted`) verified unchanged by the same script that wrote
  it. No bank change needed — `medLeft:2.5` already assumed both were
  medical.

| Ridhi | 0 → **2.5** | 0 → **2** |
| Suravi | 4 → **9** | 0 → **2** |

9 medical + 1 casual. This is the second time in this reconciliation
effort an explicit post-write integrity check has caught a gap the
step-by-step instructions missed (the first was the Ridhi/Angana
premise-checking pattern generally) — worth keeping this kind of
final-tally check as standard practice for the remaining people.

`medLeft`: 0 → **3**, `casLeft`: 2 → **4**. Verified, and now confirmed
consistent with the underlying leave docs.
