## Leave-bank reconciliation, round 5 — Rai (2026-09-18)

Same discipline throughout: freeze-check (`source:'server'`) →
targeted write → `source:'server'` verify, one doc at a time. 26 total
docs, the largest set of any person reconciled so far.

**Sensitive case, resolved with a paper trail before any delete**: two
of three "Ritual for maternal grandmother's death" bereavement docs
(21/22/30 May, all Admin-approved, all created in the same session on
2026-05-22) were disputed by Rai as never taken. Pulled the full
approval trail on all three before touching anything — all three share
identical reason text and were filed in one admin batch, not
individually. The July biometric report resolved it cleanly: 22.05
shows her present and working 08:35 (10:27–19:02), 30.05 shows her
present and working 04:34 (10:25–14:59) — neither leave was actually
taken. Both voided (`mpds1phu1kkfzs`, `mpds3crm9p8s6i`). The third,
21.05 (`mpdmrgwwfiqdzq`), shows a genuine absence that day — kept,
untouched.

**A genuine duplicate**: two docs on 21.05, one the (kept) bereavement
casual doc, one an auto-deducted medical "Absent (biometric)" doc
(`mpgfzcela58apb`) — a double-deduction for a day already covered by
approved leave. The medical duplicate was voided.

**Clean voids from the biometric report** (present, full working
days): 30.06 (`mr1m8pwrc2axai`), 06.07 (`mra6pr9spbd8m2`), 08.07
(`mrd1hz6uyhcmbu`).

**Range-doc + calendar investigation, same pattern as Ridhi's/Tasmin's
range conflicts, this time catching a code bug rather than a data
mismatch**: `msb7l7vp8lrqpq` covered 01.08→02.08 (2 days) as one
auto-deducted absence. Checked both dates against the calendar and the
app's own `is24Sat()` logic before accepting the 2-day figure: **01.08
is the 1st Saturday of August — a working day under this company's
2nd/4th-Saturday-off policy, so a report showing her absent that day
is a genuine working-day absence.** 02.08 is a Sunday — never a
working day regardless of report status, so it was never a real
absence to begin with. Under Ranit's stated sandwich rule ("Holiday =
a listed company holiday OR a 2nd/4th Saturday... trailing Sunday
counts only once a Saturday-holiday is grabbed"), neither day
qualifies as a trigger here — 01.08 isn't a 2nd/4th Saturday or a
listed holiday, so there's no "holiday" in this sequence to grab, and
a plain Sunday isn't independently a holiday under that definition.
So the sandwich rule does **not** apply to this case, despite the
system having auto-extended the deduction into Sunday.

**Standing bug identified, not fixed in this pass**:
`runAttendanceAutoDeduction()`'s forward-sandwich probe
([index.html:6070-6081](index.html)) is broader than the actual
business rule — it extends ANY flagged absence forward through *any*
following Sunday or 2nd/4th Saturday (`while(probe.getDay()===0 ||
is24Sat(probe))`), regardless of whether the anchor day itself was a
holiday. The stated business rule only pulls in a trailing Sunday
*after* a 2nd/4th-Saturday-or-listed-holiday is grabbed — a plain
working-Saturday absence rolling into the next Sunday, like this one,
isn't supposed to trigger anything. This is why the range doc had
`days:2` in the first place. **Flagged as a real gap between code and
policy, to fix in a later pass** — this reconciliation only corrected
the resulting data (shortened the doc to 1 day), it didn't touch the
function.

**Fix applied**: `msb7l7vp8lrqpq` shortened `endDate` 02.08 → 01.08,
`days` 2 → 1, retyped casual → medical (01.08's absence is medical).
`mu6hxlrmjtppuk` (17.09, a recently admin-approved system-flagged
absence that hadn't been part of Rai's original 21-confirmed/3-disputed
count) retyped casual → medical. All other docs in the classification
(all April/May medical, all late-July casual, 21.05 casual, 22.06
self-filed medical) were already stored correctly — verified via
read-only sanity checks, no writes needed.

Final tally: **13 medical days, 6 casual days** — integrity-checked
against the surviving 19 non-warning docs, passed on the first run.
`medLeft`: 0 → **−1**, `casLeft`: 0 → **−1**. Verified.

**Both negative balances are correct, not a bug**: Rai genuinely used
13 medical days against a 12-day allotment and 6 casual days against a
5-day allotment — a real, earned 1-day overage on each bank, distinct
from the wrong −8 CL she was carrying before this reconciliation
started (the old figure was inflated by exactly the wrong deductions
voided above, not a reflection of real usage).

**Before → after, all 5 people reconciled so far (Rai completes the
first pass across everyone with a confirmed split):**

| person | medLeft before→after | casLeft before→after |
|---|---|---|
| Ridhi | 0 → 2.5 | 0 → 2 |
| Suravi | 4 → 9 | 0 → 2 |
| Angana | 6 → 7 | 3.5 → 3.5 |
| Taskiya | 4 → 12 | 5 → 1 |
| Tasmin | 0 → 3 | 2 → 4 |
| Rai | 0 → **−1** | 0 → **−1** |

1 person still pending (Neha — flagged separately as also carrying
a wrong-deduction-inflated negative CL, same root cause as Rai's old
figure, per the Aug-15 holiday-reversal table earlier in this file).
Reconciliation caveat stays visible. No `index.html` changes in this
round, no `APP_VERSION` bump.

## Cross-person bug sweep + Neha follow-up fix (2026-09-18)

After the 7-person series closed, Ranit asked for a read-only sweep of
the ENTIRE `leaveRequests` collection (271 docs, 16 userIds) checking
for the two systematic bugs (range-doc `days` mismatches, auto-deducted
docs landing on a Sunday/2nd-4th-Saturday) plus missing-field
anomalies, to see if anyone outside the reconciled 7 was affected.

**Self-caught bug in the sweep script itself, worth recording**: the
first pass built per-day date strings with `d.toISOString().slice(0,
10)`, which shifts every date back by one day in IST — the exact
historical footgun `index.html`'s own `fmt()` comment warns about
(`new Date("2026-04-02").toISOString()` → `"2026-04-01T18:30:00Z"`).
Caught before reporting anything as a real finding, rewritten to build
date strings from local Y/M/D components, and re-run. The corrected
sweep found far fewer issues than the first (wrong) pass.

**Findings**: two small leftovers inside the reconciled 7 (Suravi's
`mr8rpnzr1wyx7d` including a stray Sunday never in her confirmed
leave; Neha's `mriqzu3bqfel20` including 11.07, a 2nd Saturday). Four
real-looking accounts never touched by this reconciliation effort at
all — **Souvik**, **Sarbani** (the departed employee with no live
`users`-collection doc, noted earlier in this file), **Shreya**, and
**Snaha** — each with the same weekly-off-deduction pattern, and
Sarbani/Snaha also with a `days`-mismatch doc. Three IDs (`u3`/`u4`/
`u5`, "Priya Sharma"/"Amit Patel"/"Sonia Verma") that match
`index.html`'s hardcoded seed-user array exactly, with near-identical
leave docs across all three — flagged as likely stale demo data rather
than real people, not assumed either way.

**Neha's 11.07 fix, applied**: `mriqzu3bqfel20` shortened 10–11 Jul →
10 Jul only (`days` 2→1) — 11.07 is the 2nd Saturday of July, a
non-working day, same root cause as Ridhi's original 09–11 July range
fix. Freeze-checked, verified. Integrity check re-run and passed: 5
medical + **9** casual (down from 10). `casLeft`: −5 → **−4**
(`medLeft` unaffected, stays 7). Both verified via `source:'server'`.

**Suravi's stray-Sunday fix — deferred, then completed as a full
re-reconciliation (see below).** Investigating it before writing
surfaced that her reconciliation (round 1, "clean, no conflicts") only
ever set her bank numbers directly — unlike everyone after her, her
actual `leaveRequests` docs were never individually audited against
her confirmed 3-medical/3-casual split. Reported back to Ranit rather
than silently computing a number from an incomplete premise or
unilaterally voiding/retyping the other four docs.

(`renderTeamLeaveBank()`, [index.html:9684](index.html)) against actual
Firestore data, prompted by displayed numbers that didn't obviously
reconcile.

`casLeft` is never consulted. That is why Neha (med 7, cas −4) reads
"OK". Real gap; not fixed.

**"Used (month)" column** ([index.html:9701-9709](index.html)): a pure
sum over existing approved `leaveRequests` docs whose `startDate`
matches the current month — no deduction logic of its own, so it is
only as accurate as the underlying docs. Souvik's "6 days" in
September includes an 11–13.09 auto-deduction covering 12.09 (2nd
Saturday) and 13.09 (Sunday): **the `runAttendanceAutoDeduction()`
forward-probe bug flagged in Rai's round is still producing wrong

Vishwakarma Puja doc. Also noted: `thisMonth` is derived via
`new Date().toISOString().slice(0,7)`, the same IST-shift footgun as
in the sweep script (harmless mid-month, wrong near midnight on the
1st).

**History panel** (`showLeaveHistory`, [index.html:9777](index.html))
reads the same in-memory `leaveRequests` array, so it agrees with the
"Used" column by construction; it only diverges from the stored
balance where the balance itself has drifted (Ridhi, Suravi, Souvik).

## V33: Team Leave Bank STATUS badge — BUILT on `fix-leave-bank-status-badge`, awaiting review (2026-09-19)

**Bug** (found in the bank-display audit above): `renderTeamLeaveBank()`
keyed the STATUS badge on `medLeft` alone, so Neha (med 7 / cas −4) and
Taskiya (med 12 / cas 0) read "OK".

**Fix** (display-only): new pure helper `leaveBankStatus(medLeft,
casLeft)` takes the worst state across both buckets and names the
bucket(s): `Negative · casual`, `Exhausted · med+cas`, `Low · medical`,
plain `OK`. Rules as decided with Ranit: medical Negative <0 /
Exhausted 0 / Low ≤3; **casual** Negative <0 / Exhausted 0 / Low **≤1**
(a 5-day pool, so ≤3 would flag almost everyone). Badge styling
unchanged, only the state source and label.

**Deviation from the verify list, flagged**: Suravi (med 5.5 / cas 1)
reads **Low · casual**, not OK — under the chosen casual Low line (≤1)
her one remaining casual day is Low. Ranit chose that threshold with
this pointed out.

**Verified against fresh Firestore balances** (real `leaveBankStatus()`
run over the live `users` collection): Angana OK; Ridhi Low · medical;
Souvik Exhausted · med+cas; Suravi Low · casual; Tasmin Low · medical;
Rai Negative · med+cas; Neha Negative · casual; Taskiya Exhausted ·
casual. Also verified by executing the REAL `renderTeamLeaveBank()` in a
vm and checking each rendered row.

**Testing:** `test/fix-leave-bank-status-badge.test.js`, 36/36 (truth

## Taskiya: pro-rated entitlement recorded (2026-09-19)

Taskiya joined 1 July 2026 (the reason her 01 Jul biometric absence was
voided in round 3), so 9 of the 12 financial-year months (Apr 1 – Mar
31) apply: **medical 12 × 9/12 = 9, casual 5 × 9/12 = 3.75**. Used per
docs: 0 medical, 5 casual. Written and verified via `source:'server'`:
`medLeft` 12 → **9**, `casLeft` 0 → **−1.25** (her earlier "12 / 0"
from the 18.09 close-out is superseded).

**Entitlement is now recorded on her user doc** as `medEntitlement: 9`
and `casEntitlement: 3.75`, so integrity checks must use
`(u.medEntitlement ?? 12)` / `(u.casEntitlement ?? 5)` minus doc totals
instead of a flat 12/5. Integrity check passes on that basis (9 − 0 = 9,
3.75 − 5 = −1.25). Nothing in the app reads these fields yet; they are
a data-layer record only. `saveUser()` writes the whole in-memory user
object, so the fields survive normal saves.

**3.75 vs rounding to 3:** Ranit left this as "confirm" but gave
−1.25 in the set values, so 3.75 was used (a rounding to 3 would be a
one-field change). It is also what the stored HR policy implies: casual
leave is "5 CL/year, accrued 1.25/quarter", and Jul–Mar is three
quarters (3 × 1.25 = 3.75).

**Flagged, not acted on:**
- **`newJoiners` policy: "No leave in first 1.5 months."** Taskiya's
  20 Jul casual leave (`mru63htaz36j2v`) falls inside that window
  (1 Jul – ~15 Aug), yet counts against her bank. Whether it should
  count, be unpaid, or stand is Ranit's call.
- **Display still assumes 12/5:** `renderTeamLeaveBank()` hard-codes
  `/ 12`, `/ 5` and the bar maths, so she shows "9 / 12" and
  "−1.25 / 5". The status badge is unaffected (it uses absolute
  balances). The Approve handler also caps at 12/5. Not changed here.
- Any tab holding a stale copy of her user doc could overwrite the
  new values on its next `saveUser()`; same exposure as every balance
  written in this series.

## V34: leave-bank entitlement denominators — BUILT on `fix-leave-bank-entitlement-denominators`, awaiting review (2026-09-19)

**Bug:** after Taskiya's pro-rating (`medEntitlement: 9`,
`casEntitlement: 3.75` on her user doc) the Team Leave Bank still
printed "/ 12" and "/ 5".

**Fix (display-only):** `renderTeamLeaveBank()` reads
`u.medEntitlement ?? 12` / `u.casEntitlement ?? 5` for the "/ X"
denominators, the used-total and the bar maths. Same treatment in
`renderLeaves()` (the person's own balance card) — otherwise Taskiya
would have seen "3 used of 12" against 0 actually used. Balances, the
V33 status badge and the "Used" columns are untouched.

**Swept for other flat-12/5 assumptions; deliberately NOT changed
(balance logic, not display), reported instead:** the Approve
handler's `Math.max(0, …)` clamp and its failure-path revert caps
`Math.min(12, …)` / `Math.min(5, …)` (~index.html:9915, 9969); Studio
Health's excess-CL rule `casLeftVal < -5` and `?? 5` fallback (~7177);
`runAttendanceAutoDeduction()`'s `medLeft ?? 12` bucket choice (~6089);
`saveUser()`'s 24-day medical cap; new-user defaults of 12/5. None of
these misbehave for Taskiya today; the revert caps and the −5 excess-CL
line would need entitlement-awareness if pro-rated people ever hit those
paths. The Team Leave Bank's bar colour thresholds (`<=2`, `<=5`) and
V33 badge thresholds are absolute-balance rules, unchanged.

**Verified against fresh Firestore users** (real `renderTeamLeaveBank()`
run over the live collection): Taskiya reads "/ 9" and "/ 3.75"; all
seven others still "/ 12" and "/ 5"; every balance and badge unchanged
(Taskiya's badge is Negative · casual since her −1.25 is below zero).

**Testing:** new `test/fix-leave-bank-entitlement-denominators.test.js`
29/29 (real functions executed: denominators, bar maths for full/partial/
no entitlement, own-card text and %, no write calls, isolation).
Full regression 42/42. Isolation: five `index.html` hunks — the
`APP_VERSION` line, the `renderLeaves()` card line + its two `used of`
lines, and the denominator/bar lines in `renderTeamLeaveBank()`; the
rest of the script is byte-identical to `main`, none of the added lines
touch save/sync/heartbeat/diffMode.

**Fixture upkeep:** seven fixtures asserted `renderLeaves()` is
byte-identical to main; they now compare modulo this entitlement change
on both sides (true before and after merge). My own V33 fixture's
"diff vs main" checks had gone stale on merge (the recurring pattern);

**Cross-check against Tasmin's own submitted list** surfaced two clean
removals and two dates needing an actual biometric read (not something
the OMS stores — see the note on that below):
- **26.06** (`mqyqz0ke0saif5`, medical, auto-deducted) — unclaimed on
  her list, not a sandwich day. **Removed.**
- **04.05** (`llzdev57jvw0pn5r3m`, medical, auto-deducted) — she called
  this "Elections"; Ranit's ruling: election/voting day is a separate
  paid holiday, not CL or ML. **Removed.**

Both freeze-checked before delete and verified gone via
`source:'server'`. **Recount of her 8 surviving approved non-warning
docs: 7 medical + 1 casual**, matching the expected total exactly.
`medLeft`: 3 → **5**, `casLeft`: 4 → 4 (unchanged). Verified. Integrity
check against her entitlement (12/5, full financial year — she is not
pro-rated, unlike Taskiya) confirmed consistent.

**Held, not touched:** 29.07 (casual, "Absent (no timelog activity)")
and 24.08 (currently no doc). Both need an actual biometric-report
read, which this app cannot supply — `api/biometric-sync.js`'s own
comments confirm attendance reconciliation is a manual, external step;
there is no biometric-records collection anywhere in Firestore. The
closest OMS-side proxy, `timeLogs`, was checked and reported
separately (0 logs on 29.07; 6.43h on 24.08) but is not a substitute
for the real report, and this pass makes no change based on it.

**Process note, logged per Ranit's instruction:** Taskiya
(`mr21gc49q81zlv`) and Tasmin (`mmllonwbwgp`) have confusingly similar
names. A same-session lookup for Tasmin's `timeLogs` was initially run
against Taskiya's userId by mistake — caught before being reported (the
script now verifies `name` on the user doc before printing or writing
anything) and re-run correctly. **Always verify the resolved id's
`name` field against the intended person before any read is trusted or
any write is made, for these two specifically.**
